# Changelog

All notable changes to the `claudemd` plugin. This changelog tracks plugin artifact changes (hooks, scripts, commands). Spec content changes live in `spec/CLAUDE-changelog.md`.

## Versioning policy (set in v0.2.1)

- **Plugin manifest `description` fields** carry spec version at **major.minor only** (e.g. `"AI-CODING-SPEC v6.10 …"`). Patch-level spec updates (v6.10.0 → v6.10.1) do NOT re-bump manifest descriptions. Rationale: description is marketplace-list tagline — user absorbs version family, not full semver; churn across 3 manifests every patch has no signal.
- **Canonical spec version source**: `spec/CLAUDE.md` top-line title (`# AI-CODING-SPEC vX.Y.Z — Core`) + `spec/CLAUDE-changelog.md` top `##` entry.
- **Plugin semver vs spec semver** are independent: plugin patch (0.2.0 → 0.2.1) may ship when spec is unchanged (this release); plugin minor (0.1.9 → 0.2.0) ships when spec minor updates (v0.2.0 shipped spec v6.10.0).

## [0.124.0] - 2026-12-01

**Two hooks do less work on most Bash calls. Replayed on 66,220 real commands, every decision and log row is the same as 0.123.0's. Spec v9.0.2: no rule text changed.**

**What changes for users.**
- `pre-bash-safety` first looks for the words and literals its rules need (`rm`, `find`, the package runners, the fetch commands, `socat`, `/dev/tcp`, the network calls of an interpreter one-liner, an escape token), also with quotes and backslashes removed, and lets a command with none of them through at once. A command holding a backslash-newline or `IFS` gets the full check, as before. 66,220 real commands were replayed through 0.123.0 and this version, with logging on and from the same directory: 76% of calls took the new exit, and the output, exit code and log rows matched for every command. On those calls the hook went from about 36 to 7 ms on the maintainer's machine.
- One difference, found by construction and in none of those commands: the full check deletes some text that bash keeps (a `#` inside a double-quoted string holding `$` or a backtick, or inside a quote it reads as unterminated, and a here-doc operator), so it could join two halves into any of the triggers above (`r#m`, `n#px`, `so<<'E'cat`), a word bash never runs. 0.123.0 denied such a command; this version allows it.
- `tmp-sweep` reads its interval stamp before loading anything: inside the 10-minute interval a Bash call costs about 2 ms instead of 12. A lock a killed hook leaves beside a fresh stamp is now cleared by the first call after the interval, and the sweep runs on the call after that.
- `~/.claude/OPERATOR.md` records that the per-change local commit and the `## 汇报` report are kept by preference, at a measured cost, and that these two hook changes are an exception to the hook freeze.

**What to do.** Run `/claudemd-refresh` and restart Claude Code. To go back, install 0.123.0 as `docs/ROLLBACK.md` describes ("Local machine needs the previous version back").

**Not changed.**
- The rules of the four blocking hooks, their escape tokens and kill switches. No rule family was removed, and `ship-baseline`, `memory-read-check` and `banned-vocab` were not touched.
- The new check has no switch of its own: `BASH_READONLY_FAST_PATH=0` still turns off only the read-only fast path.
- The text of every rule in the core, the modules and the extended spec.

## [0.123.0] - 2026-11-28

**Removes 16 hooks and 5 commands that served rules spec v9.0.0 deleted, or did nothing by default. The hooks that block (`pre-bash-safety`, `ship-baseline`, `memory-read-check`, `banned-vocab`) decide exactly as before. Spec v9.0.1: no rule text changed.**

**What changes for users.**
- Hooks removed. On by default: `session-summary`, `mem-audit`, `residue-audit`, `session-extended-read`, `memory-prompt-hint`, `branch-prune`, `spec-module-gate` (log only by default; its opt-in `SPEC_MODULE_GATE=deny`, which refused a release command until `ship.md` was read, goes with it) and `deferred-notice`. Opt-in: `evidence-gate`, `verify-log`, `rework-breaker`, `ledger-staleness`, `transcript-structure-scan`, `transcript-vocab-scan`, `cross-repo-write-check`, `test-failure-debug`. Each Stop now runs 2 hooks instead of 8, and a successful Bash call 6 instead of 12.
- Session start no longer injects a `tasks/*-ledger.md` after a compaction or resume, or prints the last-session summary; session end no longer prints the batch-review cadence note.
- Commands removed: `/claudemd-analyze`, `/claudemd-rules`, `/claudemd-sparkline`, `/claudemd-sampling-audit`, `/claudemd-bypass-audit`. `/claudemd-audit` drops its `selfCompliance` block. `/claudemd-doctor` drops `rule-usage`, `memory-maintenance:*`, `runbook-review-step` and `tasks-review-cadence`, and gains `memory-index-links` (MEMORY.md lines naming a missing file, and memory files no line names).
- The `DISABLE_*` switches of removed hooks, `DISABLE_SESSION_SUMMARY_BANNER`, `DISABLE_LEDGER_INJECT` and `DISABLE_BATCH_CADENCE_ADVISORY` now do nothing; leaving them set is harmless.
- `~/.claude/CLAUDE-changelog.md` shrinks from 221 KB to 6 KB (v9 entries only).

**What to do.** Run `/claudemd-refresh` and restart Claude Code. To go back, install 0.122.1 as `docs/ROLLBACK.md` describes ("Local machine needs the previous version back").

**Not changed.**
- Every allow or deny decision of the four blocking hooks, their escape tokens and kill switches, and the `spec_section` IDs they log.
- Install and uninstall still remove a `settings.json` entry left by an old hand install of any removed hook.
- `banned-vocab` and the `claudemd-cli` package stay until the re-measure after 2026-12-01.

## [0.122.1] - 2026-11-26

**Hook messages that still named spec v8 sections now point at text spec v9.0.0 has. No hook allows or blocks anything differently, and the spec is unchanged (v9.0.0).**

**What changes for users.**
- Session-end checkpoint: `tasks/session-end-<sid>-paused.md` is titled "session ended with unverified changes" and says no check ran after the last mutation; the end-of-session line reads `session-exit checkpoint:` instead of `mid-SPINE session-exit:`.
- The batch-review cadence note counts sessions with a gate deny or warning and points at `/claudemd-rules` (OPERATOR.md §13.1). It no longer recommends `/claudemd-sampling-audit` for the removed §13.2 promotion gates. `/claudemd-status --verbose` describes its switch, `DISABLE_BATCH_CADENCE_ADVISORY`, without §13.2.
- The temp-pressure note leaves the agent what this task created rather than this session, in line with v9's ask-before-deleting line (core §5).
- Section names in other messages: `spec-module-gate` (advisory or deny mode only) cites core §12 Ship; `sandbox-disposal-check` and `residue-audit` cite core §8, temporary files; `mem-audit` and the opt-in `ledger-staleness` name the hook instead of §11-EXT or §11; the opt-in `transcript-vocab-scan` cites extended §10-V.

**What to do.** Run `/claudemd-refresh` and restart Claude Code. To go back, install 0.122.0 as `docs/ROLLBACK.md` describes ("Local machine needs the previous version back").

**Not changed.**
- Every allow or deny decision, event name and kill switch, and the `spec_section` IDs written to `~/.claude/logs/claudemd.jsonl`.
- The opt-in `evidence-gate` still writes "§7 Iron Law #2": v9 keeps §7 and the `§iron-law-2` rule ID.
- `pre-bash-safety` keeps allowing exact-version npx such as `npx pkg@1.2.3`; the decision and its numbers are in `docs/S8-RESIDUALS.md` F17.

## [0.122.0] - 2026-11-25

**Spec v9.0.0: the core drops from 24 KB to 2.8 KB and the spec modules from nine to two. An A/B on coding tasks judged by hidden tests found that the v8 spec gave the same or lower correctness than no spec, at 1.8–2.4 times the cost. Also: MEMORY.md tags with a space now match, a new `memory-index-lines` hook, and a `/claudemd-clean-residue` fix.**

**What changes for users.**
- `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` becomes the v9 core: reply language, the operations to ask about first, one local commit per change, "done" only after a check ran, the safety lines, and the three-part report, whose open-items part now holds only what you must decide or know. Levels L0–L3, Iron Laws #1 and #3, spec files, the session rules and the banned-vocabulary list are gone from what the agent reads. On the A/B (Claude Opus 5.5 at xhigh, 9 tasks, 8 runs each) the v9 draft matched no-spec correctness (66/72 vs 65/72) at 1.2–1.3 times its cost, with a local commit and the report in 41 to 72 of 72 runs, depending on how the spec was delivered.
- `~/.claude/spec-modules/` keeps `ship.md` and `review.md`; the refresh removes the other seven. The pre-tag review now runs only when a release changes a delete path, auth or secrets, install or publish scripts, a gate's decision, a data format, or a published default, or when you ask.
- Apart from the tag fix below, no hook blocks or allows anything differently. Eight hooks' messages that cited removed sections were reworded: memory-read, banned-vocab, the session-end checkpoint and ship-baseline point at `~/.claude/CLAUDE-extended.md` §13 or `ship.md`; the rework note and memory hint cite nothing; the npx deny quotes the v9 safety line and says to ask first; the temp-pressure note drops the deleted `auth.md`. The opt-in `test-failure-debug` hook injects nothing (no debug module).
- A MEMORY.md tag with a space inside (`npm run lint`) now matches that phrase in the memory hint and the `memory-read-check` deny, not its words run together (`npmrunlint`); 131 of the maintainer's 1,269 tags have one. Separate tags with commas: spaces make one phrase.
- New `memory-index-lines` hook (advisory): when the agent writes a MEMORY.md index line over 150 characters, or a shell command quotes one whole, it is told to shorten it. Off with `DISABLE_MEMORY_INDEX_LINES_HOOK=1`.
- `/claudemd-clean-residue`: the age walk reads file names as bytes, so a fresh file under a non-UTF-8 directory name keeps its entry from being deleted.

**What to do.** Run `/claudemd-refresh` and restart Claude Code. To stay on spec v8, keep 0.121.1; to go back after updating, follow `docs/ROLLBACK.md` ("Local machine needs the previous version back").

**Not changed.**
- Every kill switch, and every other allow or deny decision.
- The `claudemd-cli` linter, the commands and their flags.
- Other hooks' messages, some of which still name v8 sections.
- A pinned `npx pkg@1.2.3` still passes `pre-bash-safety`, though v9 says to ask first.

## [0.121.1] - 2026-11-20

**`/claudemd-clean-residue --apply` no longer deletes an entry with a mount point at or under it, and the `tmp-sweep` note points to the spec's list of conditions instead of copying it. Spec unchanged (v8.6.0).**

**What changes for users.**
- `/claudemd-clean-residue`: every pass that deletes recursively (the `claudemd-*` entries in `$TMPDIR`, the entries of `~/.claude/tmp`, the probe project dirs under `~/.claude/projects`) removed a stale entry with a recursive delete, which enters a filesystem mounted at or under it; FUSE mounts (sshfs, rclone) need no root. Such an entry is now kept, in a dry run and under `--apply`, and listed under `protected` with `"reason": "mount-point"`. An entry whose check could not finish (an error other than a permission error while walking it) is kept the same way, with `"reason": "mount-unchecked"`. An entry that contains the command's own working directory or `$TMPDIR` stays protected as before, now with `"reason": "contains-cwd-or-tmpdir"`; `protected` entries had no reason field until now. The stderr line counts each reason. A bind mount of the same filesystem is not detected, the same limit `tmp-sweep` has had since 0.120.0. The change only keeps entries: nothing kept before is deleted now.
- `tmp-sweep`: when a temp root is 80% full, its note said an earlier session's `agent-tmp-*` dir or scratchpad is deleted without asking only when it meets every condition of the spec's Agent temp residue rule, and then listed them. The list left out two: the dir resolves inside its temp root, and a scratchpad's session transcript has had no write for 24 h. The note now says to read the list in `auth.md` first.

**What to do.** Run `/claudemd-refresh` and restart Claude Code; from 0.120.1 one refresh also brings 0.121.0 (spec v8.6.0). A script that reads `protected` from the command's JSON gets one more field per entry. A run that exited 3 because a delete failed inside a mount (a FUSE mount whose daemon died, say) now keeps that entry under `protected` and can exit 0. To go back to 0.121.0, follow `docs/ROLLBACK.md` ("Local machine needs the previous version back").

**Not changed.**
- The command's flags, what each exit code means, and its other JSON fields; a protected entry still does not count as `remaining`.
- The spec, and every hook other than `tmp-sweep`'s note.

## [0.121.0] - 2026-11-20

**Spec v8.6.0: L3 work drops plan rules copied from skills the spec no longer uses, an agent asks before deleting a temp directory with a mount point at or under it, and four HARD rules state their reason.**

**What changes for users.**
- Spec v8.6.0 (`~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` and `~/.claude/spec-modules/`):
  - L3 work (`plan.md`): a plan lists slices, each with the files it touches and how it is verified, not 2-5 minute tasks with code. The CEO / design / eng self-critique is gone; a plan review runs when you ask for one, from the angles you name. The spec no longer requires sending sub-tasks to subagents, or a fresh-subagent review for each. The one independent review before shipping stays. These lines came from skills the spec stopped routing to, and Anthropic's Claude Opus 5 migration guidance says to remove delegate-more guidance written for earlier models and that self-check instructions trigger extra work.
  - `orchestrate.md` drops the cross-session reference section ("上次 / yesterday": scan `tasks/`, confirm) and two lines (a strategy line and a pointer to the removed step-6 text); core §11 no longer points cross-session questions there. `debug.md`'s Iron Law #3 states its goal, the root cause and the chain from trigger to symptom before any change, instead of four phases.
  - An agent deletes a leftover `agent-tmp-*` or scratchpad directory without asking only when no mount point is at or under it (`auth.md`, core §8.V4); otherwise it asks first. A recursive delete enters a mounted filesystem, and FUSE mounts (sshfs, rclone) need no root. Since 0.120.0 `tmp-sweep` keeps a directory that is or holds a mount of another filesystem (a bind mount of the same filesystem is not detected); its note when a temp root is 80% full now lists the new condition too.
  - Core: §0 Hard-AUTH override, the §10 report shape, Honesty and Specificity each carry a one-line reason; §2 drops a pointer only this repository used. §4.FULL builds per slice, and an L3-lite task that turns into full L3 re-confirms plan and risk with you. `review.md` no longer repeats its `## §12 PLUGINS` heading.

**What to do.** Run `/claudemd-refresh` and restart Claude Code. For a review per sub-task in L3 work, ask for it in the task. To go back to 0.120.1 (spec v8.5.0), follow `docs/ROLLBACK.md` ("Local machine needs the previous version back").

**Not changed.**
- No HARD rule is added or removed; rule IDs and anchors in `spec/hard-rules.json` are unchanged.
- No file under `commands/` or `bin/`; under `hooks/`, only that `tmp-sweep` note.

## [0.120.1] - 2026-11-09

**The §8 `npx` check now checks every package named by `--package` or `-p`, not only the first one written with a space. Spec unchanged (v8.5.0).**

**What changes for users.**
- `pre-bash-safety-check`: `npx --package=<pkg> ./run.js` and `npm exec --package=<pkg> -- ./bin` were allowed for an unpinned package that is not installed where the command runs: the check skipped `--package=<pkg>` and judged only the path after it. A second `-p` (`npx -p cowsay@1.5.0 -p <pkg> cowsay`) went unchecked the same way. Now each value of `-p`, `--package`, `-p=` and `--package=` before the first word that does not start with `-` is checked as `--package <pkg>` already was: allowed when it is a path, pinned (`<pkg>@1.2.3`), or found in the lockfile or `node_modules`; denied otherwise. This covers `npx`, `npm exec`, `npm x`, `bunx`, `bun x`, `pnpm dlx` and `yarn dlx`. The old check is unchanged and still runs, so what 0.120.0 denied is still denied, unless the added checks push a very long command past the hook's 3-second limit (E3 in `docs/S8-RESIDUALS.md`).
- A remote `--package=` value gets the spaced form's verdict, so `https://…/x.tgz`, `github:user/repo` and `git+https://…` are denied, and so is a local `file:` spec (`--package=file:./pkg.tgz`), as `-p file:./pkg.tgz` already was; that one is a known false deny, registered under F37 in `docs/S8-RESIDUALS.md`.
- An allowed `--package=` value found in the lockfile or `node_modules` writes its own `npx-allow-local` row to `~/.claude/logs/claudemd.jsonl`.

**What to do.** Run `/claudemd-refresh` and restart Claude Code; from 0.119.0 one refresh also brings 0.120.0's changes. If a package you trust is denied, pin it (`--package=<pkg>@<version>`) or install it in the project. To go back to 0.120.0, follow `docs/ROLLBACK.md` ("Local machine needs the previous version back").

**Not changed.**
- When another flag that takes a value comes first (`npx --cache ./c <pkg>`), the check still reads that value as the package, and a `--package=` after it is not reached (F37).

## [0.120.0] - 2026-11-09

**`banned-vocab-check` no longer denies a commit for a word inside backticks or a path, and quotes a decimal ratio whole. `doctor` names the memory files the §11 gate actually denied. `tmp-sweep` keeps an `agent-tmp-*` directory that is or holds a mount of another filesystem. Spec unchanged (v8.5.0).**

**What changes for users.**
- `banned-vocab-check`: the commit-message scan now skips fenced blocks, backtick spans, paths and file names, as the prose scan already did, so a word in backticks or in a path- or file-shaped word (`work/pass`, `robust.and`, `msg-robust.txt`, not an upper-case extension) no longer denies a commit. Skipped text becomes a space, so the words around it can now join into a phrase that is denied, as in `claudemd-cli lint`; lint pairs backticks across lines, the hook within a line. A ratio such as `1.8x` was quoted as `8x` in the deny text and the log row; it is now quoted whole, which on its own changes no verdict.
- `memory-read-check` deny rows in `~/.claude/logs/claudemd.jsonl` also record which `MEMORY.md` tags matched (`extra.tags`). `doctor`'s `memory-tag-specificity` check lists first any memory file the gate denied 3 or more times in 30 days, with its count and tags (`tags not recorded` for rows written before 0.120.0); those lines do not change whether the check passes.
- `doctor`'s `runbook-review-step` check also counts `pre-tag review` or `fresh … subagent(s)` anywhere in a ship runbook, a lesson included, as its review step; `refresh … subagents` does not count.
- `tmp-sweep`: when several sweeps run at once, a directory another sweep already removed is no longer recorded as an error that made the sweep exit 3. An `agent-tmp-*` directory that is, or holds, a mount of another filesystem (FUSE: sshfs, rclone) is kept, and listed as skipped (`mount-point`) when otherwise due; a bind mount of the same filesystem is not detected.
- `sandbox-disposal-check` warn and block rows name the reported directories (`extra.names`: basenames, at most five). `sampling-audit --json` gives `askRate` a `precision` and a `status` (today `null` and `collecting`), as `byRule` has, so its `assent` count is not read as a measured rate.

**What to do.** Run `/claudemd-refresh` and restart Claude Code. To go back to 0.119.0, follow `docs/ROLLBACK.md` ("Local machine needs the previous version back").

**Not changed.**
- `banned-vocab-check` still does not read the file of `git commit -F FILE`, and still scans words in double quotes. When the command also holds a quoted `-m` or `--message` value (a `git tag -m "v1"` after an `-F -` here-doc, a `python3 -m 'venv'` inside it), it scans only those values, so a banned word in the here-doc message passes.
- The §8 `npx` check still denies when a flag's value or a redirection sits where it expects the package name, as in `npx --version 2>&1`; this is registered as F37 in `docs/S8-RESIDUALS.md`.

## [0.119.0] - 2026-11-03

**Spec v8.5.0: a temp directory that an earlier task or session left is deleted without asking you, under stated conditions. `tmp-sweep` also reaps `agent-tmp-*` directories idle 72 h without a `.keep`.**

**What changes for users.**
- Spec v8.5.0 (`~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` §5, §5.1 and §8.V4, `~/.claude/spec-modules/auth.md`). Deleting a directory outside the project needed your approval, even a temp directory an earlier session made and left. In the maintainer's transcripts of 2026-10-06 to 11-03, 8 report open items asked for that, and no reply refused. A new Soft class, Agent temp residue, covers an `agent-tmp-*` directory in `$TMPDIR`, `/tmp`, `/var/tmp` or `~/.cache/tmp`, or an ended session's scratchpad directory, when it is yours, not a symlink, unchanged for 24 h, has no `.keep`, and no open spec file, paused checkpoint or deferred item names it. The agent names it before deleting it (not needed under `AUTONOMY_LEVEL: aggressive`) and lists each path and its size in the report. Anything else outside the project still needs your approval. §8.V4 now has agents name the temp directories they create `agent-tmp-<task>-…`, and a `.keep` say why and until when.
- `tmp-sweep` (PostToolUse on Bash, on by default) also deletes an `agent-tmp-*` directory in those roots that you own, with no `.keep` at its top and no socket or FIFO inside, in which no entry's mtime or ctime is within 72 h; it does not follow symlinks. `/var/tmp` is scanned too, for vitest's run directories as well. Its temp-pressure note states those conditions instead of telling the agent that everything it did not create is your call. `sandbox-disposal-check` also reports `agent-tmp-*` directories made during the session that are still there, in the places it scans (`/tmp`, `/var/tmp`, `~/.claude/tmp`).

**What to do.** Run `/claudemd-refresh` and restart Claude Code. To keep an `agent-tmp-*` directory, put a `.keep` file at its top. `DISABLE_TMP_SWEEP_HOOK=1` turns the whole sweep off. To go back to 0.118.0, follow `docs/ROLLBACK.md` ("Local machine needs the previous version back").

**Not changed.**
- Apart from vitest's run directories, the sweep deletes only `agent-tmp-*` directories; Claude Code's `cc-socks` directories are not touched.
- The vitest arm's signature and floors (60 min, 24 h while a watcher runs).
- The §8 `rm` variable gate.

## [0.118.0] - 2026-11-02

**Spec v8.4.0: the rule for worktree subagents names the commands Claude Code refuses inside them and sends reviews and experiments to a temp clone instead. `/claudemd-refresh` no longer says that other open windows' hooks fail after a refresh.**

**What changes for users.**
- Spec v8.4.0, §EXT §11-O Worktree spawn (`~/.claude/CLAUDE-extended.md` and `~/.claude/spec-modules/orchestrate.md`). The rule said only that a worktree agent's prompt must not name the main checkout's absolute path. It now lists what Claude Code refuses inside an `isolation: "worktree"` agent (the main checkout's absolute path, compound or piped git, a loop or subshell, text handed to a shell, a value computed at runtime, a `HOME` change), asks for one plain command per step, and says that work needing a sandbox `HOME`, an old version beside the new, or paths outside the repo is spawned without worktree isolation, in its own temp clone. In the maintainer's transcripts of 2026-10-06 to 11-02, Claude Code refused 877 tool calls in worktree agents. No HARD rule is added or removed; core changes only in its title line.
- `/claudemd-refresh`, `scripts/refresh-plugin.sh` and README §Update said a refresh removes the old version's cache folder, so other open windows fail on every hook until reloaded; `/claudemd-uninstall` and README §Uninstall said `/plugin uninstall` deletes the plugin cache. Neither deletes the folder: Claude Code marks it for cleanup, and both Claude Code and the install keep a marked folder for at least 14 days (checked in Claude Code 2.1.309, 2.1.310 and 2.1.316). Other open windows keep running the previous version's hooks, which work. These texts now say so; the advice is unchanged: run `/reload-plugins` in each other window, and run `/claudemd-uninstall` before `/plugin uninstall`.

**What to do.** Run `/claudemd-refresh` and restart Claude Code; the first new session syncs `~/.claude`. To go back, follow `docs/ROLLBACK.md` ("Local machine needs the previous version back").

**Not changed.**
- `hooks/`, and which cache folders the install keeps or removes.

## [0.117.7] - 2026-11-02

**An install no longer removes an older version folder while Claude Code records a running process as using it, whatever the log setting. No hook or spec text changes.**

**What changes for users.**
- Claude Code writes a file under `.in_use/` in each plugin version folder a process loads, holding that process's pid and start time, and removes it when the process exits (versions 2.1.309, 2.1.310 and 2.1.316 were checked). The install's cache prune now reads those files: an older folder under `~/.claude/plugins/cache/claudemd/claudemd/` stays while one of them names a running process, listed in `cachePruned.held` with the reason `in-use`. Until now the prune looked only at Claude Code's `.orphaned_at` mark and the hook log, so when the session using a folder had `DISABLE_RULE_HITS_LOG=1` in its own environment only, or the log held no row from that version in the last 14 days (a deleted log, say), an install from another session could remove that folder, and every claudemd hook in the session still using it failed with exit 127. 0.117.6 listed this under Not changed (D#652).
- Claude Code itself does not delete a folder it marked while such a record names a running process. `/claudemd-install` now says that, where it said Claude Code deletes a marked folder 14 days after marking it.

No action is needed. To go back, follow `docs/ROLLBACK.md` ("Local machine needs the previous version back").

**Cache prune** (4941a98, D#652). `scripts/lib/cache-prune.js` checks the `.in_use/` records before the three reasons 0.117.6 had (`orphaned-recently`, `hook-rows-recently`, `no-log-signal`), so a folder in use reports `in-use` even when its mark is older than 14 days or the log is off. A record counts when its `pid` is a positive integer that answers signal 0 (a permission error counts as running) and, when the record has a `procStart` string and `/proc/<pid>/stat` can be read, that string equals the process's start time, so a pid the system gave to a new process does not count; a record without a `procStart` string counts on its pid alone. A record that is empty or not JSON, a dead pid, and a pid that is not a positive integer hold nothing. The prune never writes or deletes a record itself; a folder it removes goes with its records. When no record names a running process, it keeps and removes the same folders as 0.117.6.

**Install page** (ebac656). `commands/claudemd-install.md` names the fourth reason and Claude Code's own rule; the cache-prune row of `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` names the new hold.

**Not changed.**
- `hooks/` and `spec/` (spec v8.3.1).
- Without `/proc` (macOS) the pid alone decides, so a record whose pid now belongs to another process keeps its folder until Claude Code clears the dead record. The prune does not compare the start time Claude Code writes on Windows either.
- With a Claude Code version that writes no `.in_use/` records, the prune decides by the three earlier reasons.
- The prune ages `.orphaned_at` by the number written in it, Claude Code by the file's modification time. They agree unless another program rewrites the file.
- For a version folder that is a symlink, Claude Code keeps the record under `~/.claude/plugins/.in_use-links/` instead, which the prune does not read.
- Where the prune reads records differently from Claude Code 2.1.316, it holds more in two cases: a permission error from the pid check, and a `procStart` that is not a string. It holds less in others: Claude Code counts a record it cannot read as live, and a `.tmp.` one under 10 minutes old.
- Claude Code reads only regular files up to a size cap; the prune reads any entry in `.in_use/`, so an entry that is not a regular file, which Claude Code does not write, can stall the install.
- `CLAUDEMD_SPEC_ACTION=restore` still stops at an empty backup dir or one with only a `hooks/` folder. Current versions write no `hooks/`-only one, and leave an empty one only when an install stops, or loses the file, between creating the dir and moving the file in. Restore then reports `restore-empty` and exits 3, and the older backup stays where it is.

**Tests.**
- `tests/scripts/cache-prune.test.js`: D#652.1 (live records hold four folders as `in-use`, one with a 20-day-old mark and one with a 1-day-old mark among them, while an unmarked folder without a record is removed) and D#652.2 (with `DISABLE_RULE_HITS_LOG=1` a live record reports `in-use`, an unmarked folder beside it `no-log-signal`) failed before 4941a98. D#652.3 (a dead pid, an empty file, text that is not JSON, pids 0 and -1, a pid written as a string, `null`, a directory where a record should be, a file where `.in_use/` should be) and D#652.4 (a live pid with a different start time) hold nothing before and after. D#652.5 runs only where `/proc` is missing. D#652.6 (a live record beside a dead one and one that is not JSON holds its folder; an empty `.in_use/` holds nothing) came from the pre-tag review: with `some` changed to `every` in the check, it is the one case that fails. Nine mutants of the new check each fail at least one case; the tenth (an unreadable `/proc` entry counts as a dead pid) survives on Linux, where every case passes, and fails D#652.5 on a simulated host without `/proc`.
- `tests/scripts/command-page-behaviour.test.js`: the changed page line is pinned whole (it failed on the old text), and `pruneCache` runs with a live `.in_use` record beside a folder for each of the other three reasons.
- On ed1ec48, whose tree the tagged commit changes only in this entry and the release record: `npm run check` exits 0, the node suite 1757 of 1760 passed with 3 skipped and every shell suite passed; `npm run smoke` passes 2 of 2. PR CI: 7 of 7 jobs green on ed1ec48 (run 41457576588).

**Pre-tag review.** One round by a fresh reviewer (57fd12a..48af4d5): 0 Critical, 0 High, 1 Medium, 8 Low. M1: every D#652 case put one entry in `.in_use/`, so a check that asked every record to name a live process, instead of any one, passed the suite; D#652.6 now covers an empty `.in_use/` and a folder with dead and live records, both shapes found in a real cache. The Lows: this entry described the start-time rule more narrowly than the code applies it; code comments said the prune reads records as Claude Code does, which 2.1.316 does not bear out (the differences are now under Not changed); Claude Code writes the record elsewhere for a symlinked version folder; the entry said current versions write no empty backup dir, which an install stopped between creating the dir and moving the file in does; "never deletes a record" left out that a removed folder takes its records with it; a commit message named the three checked versions as a range. Three are left as they are: a FIFO in `.in_use/` stalls the prune (Claude Code writes none; under Not changed), the parse of a process name containing `)` has no test (real names have no space), and one mutant is caught only when the tests do not run as root (CI runners do not). The repairs are a test and text, so they went through a claim-drift pass instead of a second review; 4941a98's message keeps the earlier wording.

## [0.117.6] - 2026-11-02

**With `DISABLE_RULE_HITS_LOG=1` an install no longer removes an older version folder that nothing marked, and restore passes over a newer backup of spec files that has no `CLAUDE.md`. No hook or spec text changes.**

**What changes for users.**
- With `DISABLE_RULE_HITS_LOG=1` the hooks write no log, so the hook-row check the prune makes besides Claude Code's mark could not show that a session still ran an older version's hooks, and an install removed an older folder under `~/.claude/plugins/cache/claudemd/claudemd/` that carried no `.orphaned_at` mark. In a session that still used that folder every claudemd hook then failed with exit 127, the failure 0.117.4 fixed for the log-on case. Such a folder now stays, listed in `cachePruned.held` with the reason `no-log-signal`, until Claude Code marks it or the log is on again. A folder Claude Code marked more than 14 days ago is still removed, and without the setting nothing changes.
- `CLAUDEMD_SPEC_ACTION=restore` (`/claudemd-uninstall restore`) now passes over a newer backup that holds nothing but spec files when it has no `CLAUDE.md` too, lists it in `skippedSpecOnly` and leaves it in place. A `/claudemd-update` before 0.68.3 wrote such a dir when `CLAUDE.md` had not changed, and an install before 0.111.0 wrote one when the home folder had spec files but no `CLAUDE.md`. Restore used to copy those old spec files back and leave your own older backup where it was. A dir holding any file whose first line is not the spec heading is still restored, and so is an empty one. A spec file you only added notes to keeps its heading, so a dir of such files is passed over too, as one with a `CLAUDE.md` already was in 0.116.6; it stays in place and is named in `skippedSpecOnly`.

No action is needed. To go back, follow `docs/ROLLBACK.md` ("Local machine needs the previous version back").

**Cache prune** (4639a6a, f57656b, D#643). `scripts/lib/cache-prune.js` checks each older folder in the same order as before: a mark under 14 days old (`orphaned-recently`), then a hook row from its version in the last 14 days (`hook-rows-recently`). Next, with `DISABLE_RULE_HITS_LOG` set to `1` (the value `hooks/lib/hook-common.sh` reads) and no mark, it holds the folder as `no-log-signal`; otherwise it removes it. Such a folder takes about 11 MB and stays until one of the two changes or it is deleted by hand. `/claudemd-install` names the third reason.

**Restore** (56ef606, D#644). `holdsOnlySpecFiles` in `scripts/lib/backup.js` no longer needs a `CLAUDE.md`: a dir counts when it has at least one entry and every entry is a spec file name, a regular file whose first line is the spec heading. A file the user wrote from scratch fails that heading test and keeps its dir a restore source; a spec file the user only added notes to passes it, which 0.116.6 already accepted for dirs with a `CLAUDE.md`. 0.116.6 left the no-`CLAUDE.md` update dir as a restore source at its review depth limit; the 0.117.5 pre-tag review reproduced the case. The install and uninstall pages, README's restore row and `uninstall.js --help` say "with or without `CLAUDE.md`".

**Not changed.**
- `hooks/` and `spec/` (spec v8.3.1).
- With the log on but no row from any version in 14 days (a deleted log, say), the cache prune still removes an unmarked older folder.
- An empty backup dir, or one holding only a `hooks/` dir, still stops restore at that dir, as in 0.116.6.
- doctor's `backup-namespace-legacy` row lists only dirs with a spec-shaped `CLAUDE.md`, so its output is as before.
- The prune reads `DISABLE_RULE_HITS_LOG` from the install's own environment. Set only for one project or one shell, another session's install still removes an unmarked older folder that a session with the log off uses.
- The prune does not read the `.in_use/` files Claude Code writes in each version folder, one per process using it, whatever the log setting (D#652).

**Tests.**
- `tests/scripts/cache-prune.test.js`: D#643.1 (log off: an unmarked folder beyond the three kept is held as `no-log-signal`, one marked 20 days ago is removed, one with a recent row reports `hook-rows-recently`) failed before 4639a6a; D#643.2 (the setting unset, `0`, `true`, empty) removes the unmarked folder on both sides. Mutants that drop the no-mark condition, read any non-empty value as off, or remove the branch each fail one case. The suite clears `DISABLE_RULE_HITS_LOG` around each case: with it exported in the shell, four older cases failed when the file ran on its own.
- `tests/scripts/uninstall.test.js`: the D#644 case (a personal backup, then two newer dirs of spec files without `CLAUDE.md`, one with a byte-order mark before its heading) failed before 56ef606. 0.116.6's control row that asserted such a dir is not passed over became that case; two controls took its place, a user-written `OPERATOR.md` beside a spec file and an empty dir. Mutants that drop the non-empty check or require `CLAUDE.md` again each fail one case.
- `tests/scripts/command-page-behaviour.test.js`: the changed page lines are pinned whole; one case runs `pruneCache` with the log off (one folder for each of the three reasons), another runs the restore and checks that README and `--help` give the new rule and no longer give the old exception. Both failed on the old text.
- On 3171f33, whose tree the tagged commit changes only in this entry and the design record (`scripts/lib/cache-prune.js` and the logic of `scripts/lib/backup.js` as in ed5b8cb; later commits change a comment in `backup.js`, the `uninstall.js --help` text, tests and docs): `npm run check` exits 0, the node suite 1752 of 1754 passed with 2 skipped and every shell suite passed; `npm run smoke` passes 2 of 2. PR CI: 7 of 7 jobs green on ed5b8cb (run 41430993174).

**Pre-tag review.** One round by a fresh reviewer (b09df4e..ed5b8cb): 0 Critical, 0 High, 2 Medium, 7 Low. M1: this entry, a `backup.js` comment and 56ef606's message said a file the user wrote fails the heading test; a spec file the user only added notes to passes it, and an install before 0.111.0 moved such a sibling (an edited `OPERATOR.md`, say) into a dir with no `CLAUDE.md`. The behaviour matches 0.116.6's for dirs with a `CLAUDE.md`, and the text now says so. M2: the entry said an install had nothing to tell it whether a session used an older folder; Claude Code's `.in_use/` files do, whatever the log setting, and the prune does not read them (now under Not changed, D#652). The Lows: `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` and the legacy-backup note did not name the change; "holds nothing but spec files" read as covering an empty dir, which is not passed over (five places reworded); README's description of `DISABLE_RULE_HITS_LOG` did not name the new hold; a log-off setting in one session's environment only (Not changed); four older prune cases failed with the setting exported when the file ran alone; the README and `--help` checks did not fail if the removed exception came back. All repairs are text and tests, so they went through a claim-drift pass instead of a second review; 56ef606's message keeps the earlier wording.

## [0.117.5] - 2026-11-01

**`/claudemd-doctor`'s status-line row names a fix that works when `CLAUDEMD_NO_STATUSLINE=1` is set, and three command pages now describe `cachePruned.held`, restore's `skippedSpecOnly` and doctor's `advisory` rows. No hook or spec text changes.**

**What changes for users.**
- With `CLAUDEMD_NO_STATUSLINE=1`, a stale or missing `~/.claude/claudemd-statusline.sh` is still a red `statusline` row, and its fix now reads "run /claudemd-statusline and have it refresh the renderer (with CLAUDEMD_NO_STATUSLINE=1, /claudemd-install does not recopy it)". The row used to name `/claudemd-install`, which leaves the status line alone under that setting, so the row stayed red after the fix it named. Without the setting the row reads as before.
- `/claudemd-install` names `cachePruned.held` (older version folders kept because a running session may still use them, with `orphaned-recently` or `hook-rows-recently` as the reason) and says which backups restore passes over. `/claudemd-uninstall` says which backup restore uses and relays `skippedSpecOnly`. `/claudemd-doctor` says a failing row with `advisory: true` does not count toward the exit code. These are the pages the model reads before it runs the commands; apart from the doctor row above, the scripts behave as in 0.117.4.

No action is needed. To go back, follow `docs/ROLLBACK.md` ("Local machine needs the previous version back").

**Doctor** (e5dec8d). With `CLAUDEMD_NO_STATUSLINE=1`, install.js returns `statusline.action: "opted-out"` and does not touch the renderer. `/claudemd-statusline` recopies it when the user asks: its page re-runs `adopt` for claudemd's own slot and a registered guest, and offers `adopt --empty-only` when a host provider runs the file under another id. In the states doctor's tests build, each copies the renderer and writes no setting; `adopt` also re-registers a guest entry whose command differs from the one this version writes. Only the value `1` switches the fix text, the test install.js makes; the `, or /claudemd-statusline remove to unwire it.` clause keeps its conditions.

**Command pages** (7a6cc1a). Each sentence was written from the code it describes: `scripts/lib/cache-prune.js`, `scripts/uninstall.js` and `countsTowardExit` in `scripts/doctor.js`. The install page said restore "cannot return a spec instead of the user's own file". That holds for the backups install writes, but not for the spec backups a `/claudemd-update` before 0.68.3 wrote into the same namespace. Since 0.116.6 restore passes over such a backup when it holds a `CLAUDE.md` and nothing but spec files; one with no `CLAUDE.md` is restored. The install and uninstall pages now say both.

**Evaluations** (e31937b; design records, nothing shipped). Two local evaluations ended, recorded in `tasks/specs/cross-repo-write.md` (r9) and `tasks/specs/wtree-evidence.md` (r2). cross-repo-write (opt-in `CROSS_REPO_WRITE=1`) had 4 hits in 36 days on the maintainer's machine, all from subagents doing work they were asked to do and none an unauthorized write, so the advisory stays off by default. The working-tree fingerprint (`EVIDENCE_WTREE=1`) was compared with evidence-gate's verdict on 108 completion claims that three blind raters labelled. Counting only the claims they could decide, an evidence-gate fire was a code claim left unverified in 0 of 29 cases, and a fingerprint "unverified" in 1 of 62. Both are under the 0.8 precision required before either is shown to the model, so neither is. Most of the false alarms were messages that claimed no code change at all.

**Not changed.**
- `hooks/` and `spec/` (spec v8.3.1).
- When `DISABLE_RULE_HITS_LOG=1` keeps the hook log empty, the cache prune still removes an older folder that carries no `.orphaned_at` mark.
- Which backup restore picks: the 0.116.6 rule stands, and only the pages that describe it changed.
- evidence-gate and cross-repo-write stay off by default, and evidence-gate's claim detection is as before.
- The skill re-scan (D#598) is still short of its 62 sessions.

**Tests.**
- `tests/scripts/doctor.test.js`: four cases set `CLAUDEMD_NO_STATUSLINE=1` (a stale renderer in claudemd's own slot, a missing one there, a missing one under a registered guest, a stale one under code-graph's `_previous`) pin the whole fix clause and follow both fixes: `scripts/install.js` leaves the row red, and the command the `/claudemd-statusline` page names for that state (`adopt`, or `adopt --empty-only` in the host-run one) turns it green with `settings.json`, the host registry and its mirror unchanged byte for byte. All four fail on 0.117.4's `doctor.js`. Three more cases (`0`, `true`, empty) keep the `/claudemd-install` advice. Mutants that never take the opt-out branch, read any non-empty value as the opt-out, drop the stale fix's period, drop the remove clause under the opt-out, or add text after the advice each fail at least two cases.
- `tests/scripts/command-page-behaviour.test.js`: three cases pin the new lines whole and run what they name: `pruneCache` holding one folder for each reason, a restore that passes over a newer backup holding a `CLAUDE.md` and only spec files and lists it, and `countsTowardExit` on a failing row with and without `advisory`; all three failed on the old pages. A fourth runs a restore past a newer backup of spec files with no `CLAUDE.md`, which is restored, as both pages now say.
- On 5c4d6b6, whose tree the tagged commit changes only in this entry and the design record (`scripts/doctor.js` as in e5dec8d apart from a comment): `npm run check` exits 0, the node suite 1749 of 1751 passed with 2 skipped and every shell suite passed; `npm run smoke` passes 2 of 2. PR CI: 7 of 7 jobs green on 4bd7e25 (run 41346208317), whose code is the same apart from that comment.

**Pre-tag review.** One round by a fresh reviewer (17eac21..4bd7e25): 0 Critical, 0 High, 1 Medium, 6 Low. M1: both restore sentences said restore passes over every newer spec-only backup; it passes over one only when it holds a `CLAUDE.md`, and a backup of spec files with no `CLAUDE.md` is restored (reproduced in a temp HOME). Both pages now say so, with a test of that case. The Lows: `adopt --empty-only` is what the `/claudemd-statusline` page offers in one of the three states, not all three (this entry, a `doctor.js` comment and the tests now use the command the page names); the install page's new "hold only the user's own files" (three spec files move with the user's `CLAUDE.md`); "beyond the three newest" (install keeps the current version and the newest others); a mark that does not read as a number holds its dir on every install, so "nothing needs doing" went; four mutants of the fix text passed the tests, which now pin the whole clause and the `=== '1'` test; the fourth doctor case also fails on 0.117.4, which the Tests paragraph said only of the first three. The repairs are text, tests and a `doctor.js` comment, so they went through a claim-drift pass instead of a second review. The messages of e5dec8d and 7a6cc1a keep the earlier wording of the M1 and first Low points.

## [0.117.4] - 2026-11-01

**An install no longer deletes an older version folder that a running session's hooks still run from. Two `/claudemd-statusline` states 0.117.3 still got wrong are fixed, and spec v8.3.1's §7 check before a push skips GitHub's Dependabot job, as the push gate already does.**

**What changes for users.**
- After an install, claudemd keeps the three newest version folders under `~/.claude/plugins/cache/claudemd/claudemd/` and removes the older ones. Until now it removed one even while a session opened earlier still ran its hooks from it. In that session every claudemd hook then failed with "No such file or directory" (exit 127), which Claude Code does not treat as a block, so the §8 gates stayed off there until `/reload-plugins` or a restart (seen here on 2026-11-01 in two sessions). An older folder now stays while either of two things says a session may still use it: Claude Code's `.orphaned_at` mark in the folder is under 14 days old (or does not read as a number), or the hook log has a row from that version in the last 14 days. Claude Code deletes a marked folder itself 14 days after marking it. The install result's `cachePruned` gains `held`, which lists each folder kept this way and why.
- `statusline-adopt.js adopt --supersede=claudemd` exits 1 and writes nothing; it used to save claudemd's own entry as a provider to restore. A restore record an older version wrote that way is now read without that entry, so `remove` takes claudemd out and deletes the renderer nothing else runs; earlier versions put claudemd's entry back over the renderer they had just deleted.
- `remove` no longer restores a saved provider over a host provider that has the same id now. That one stays in `~/.claude/.claudemd-state/statusline-prev.json`, the result lists it as `notRestored`, and the human output says where it is. Before, the saved entry replaced the live one, and when the live one ran the renderer, the renderer was deleted too.
- Spec v8.3.1 (core §7, Push fires CI/Release): "check the color of the newest completed run in `gh run list --branch "$(git branch --show-current)" --limit 5`, skipping GitHub's own `Dependabot Updates` job". It said `--limit 1`, whose first row on this repo's `main` on 2026-11-01 was a failed Dependabot job, which `ship-baseline-check` already skips.

No action is needed. A session still running from a folder an earlier install removed needs `/reload-plugins` or a restart. To go back, follow `docs/ROLLBACK.md` ("Local machine needs the previous version back").

**Cache prune** (853e6dd, D#635). `scripts/lib/cache-prune.js` still keeps `keep` (3) version folders: the current one and the newest others. Each older folder is held while its `.orphaned_at` is under 14 days old, dated in the future or not a number (a mark that cannot be read at all counts as no mark), or while `claudemd.jsonl`, `.1` or `.2` has a row with its `hook_version` from the last 14 days; otherwise it is removed as before. The log is read only when a folder is past `keep`: on this machine the three generations (11.5 MB, 36,798 rows) read in 54 to 58 ms. A log that cannot be read throws before anything is removed, and `install.js` already catches a prune failure, so then nothing is removed. Claude Code's documentation states the 14 days and the reason ("so a session that already loaded the old version keeps running", code.claude.com/docs/en/plugins/loading, "Cleanup of previous versions"); the log window matches it because a session can stay open for days without firing a hook.

**Statusline** (86c72b3, 5efc96b, D#628). `adopt()` throws before any write when `supersede` is `claudemd`, dry run included. `readSupersededList` drops an entry with claudemd's id. `remove()` restores a saved provider only when no live provider has its id; the others are written back to the record and returned as `notRestored`. `detect()`'s `rendererRestoredBy` applies the same split, so it names only providers `remove()` will restore. One visible effect: with a saved provider that runs the renderer, a live one with the same id that does not, and the renderer missing, `/claudemd-doctor`'s statusline row now also offers `/claudemd-statusline remove`, which then leaves the saved provider in the record. `/claudemd-statusline`'s remove text says when a saved provider is not restored and tells the model to report `notRestored`.

**Spec v8.3.1** (3d329b8, D#613). Patch: the §7 sentence now describes the check `ship-baseline-check` makes (newest completed run of the last 5, GitHub's `Dependabot Updates` job with event `dynamic` skipped). No HARD rule added or removed; every rule keeps its ID.

**Not changed.**
- Claude Code's own cleanup of marked folders after 14 days.
- With `DISABLE_RULE_HITS_LOG=1` the hook log has no rows, so a folder with no `.orphaned_at` mark is removed as before.
- `commands/claudemd-install.md` describes `cachePruned.removed` only; it does not name `held`.
- D#598 (the skill re-scan) waits for 62 release-word sessions; the scan on 2026-11-01 counted 38.

**Tests.**
- `tests/scripts/cache-prune.test.js`: six cases (D#635.1 to .6), each failing on 0.117.3's `cache-prune.js`: a dir marked 1 day ago is held and one marked 20 days ago with no rows goes; unmarked and marked-long-ago dirs with a row in the last 14 days are held, one with only a 30-day-old row goes, and a kept version's row holds nothing else; a row in `claudemd.jsonl.1` counts; a mark that is not a number holds; with nothing past `keep`, `held` is empty; a log that cannot be read throws before any removal. Mutants that drop the mark check or the log check each fail two named cases.
- `tests/scripts/statusline-adopt.test.js`, `tests/scripts/statusline-cli.test.js`: six cases, each failing before 86c72b3: `adopt` refuses `supersede: 'claudemd'` (dry run too) with the registry unchanged and no record; the CLI exits 1 for it; a record holding claudemd's own entry restores nothing and the renderer goes; a saved `_previous` is not restored over a live `_previous` that runs the renderer, which stays, while a saved provider with no live namesake is restored and the blocked one stays in the record; `rendererRestoredBy` leaves out a saved provider with a live namesake and `remove()` then deletes the renderer; the human output names `not-restored=` and the record. Mutants that drop the refusal, the self filter, the live-namesake split or detect's use of it each fail at least one named case.
- `tests/scripts/spec-structure.test.js`: the pins of the two preambles, core §7, Recent changes and both heading inventories follow v8.3.1.
- `tests/scripts/command-page-behaviour.test.js`: the two `/claudemd-statusline` lines it pins whole (the remove transition and the remove report) follow the new text.
- On the tagged commit (code as in 345702b; the later commits change a comment in `scripts/lib/cache-prune.js`, a row of `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`, the design record and this entry): `npm run check` exits 0, the node suite 1738 of 1740 passed with 2 skipped and every shell suite passed; `npm run smoke` passes 2 of 2. No `hooks/` file changed, so no bash 3.2 run. PR CI: 7 of 7 jobs green on 345702b (run 41335111751).

**Pre-tag review.** One round by a fresh reviewer (5126416..345702b): 0 Critical, 0 High, 2 Medium, 6 Low. M1: this entry dated the self-supersede defect from 0.117.1, and 0.117.0 already behaved the same (replayed on its tree); it now says earlier versions, and the summary line no longer dates the two states. M2: the Not changed list named `/claudemd-doctor`'s statusline row, which in the live-namesake state with the renderer missing now also offers remove; that bullet is gone and the Statusline paragraph says what changed. L1: the entry, a code comment and 853e6dd's message said an unreadable mark holds a folder, but a mark that cannot be read counts as no mark and only one that is not a number holds; the entry and the comment now say so. L2: "`keep` newest plus the current one" was wrong, since the current one is one of the three; the entry is reworded. Both commit messages were pushed before the review and are not rewritten. Not repaired, each true of 0.117.3 as well or outside the published tree: L3, a hand-edited record that holds one id twice restores both entries and can then delete the renderer (`adopt` writes each id once); L4, a provider left in the record is still cleared by a later `adopt` into an empty slot, `adopt --force` or a `remove` of a claudemd-owned slot; L5, `command-page-behaviour.test.js` fails on 5efc96b and 147acaf, fixed in 345702b, which matters only to a bisect; L6, the command page names the default record path, which `CLAUDEMD_STATE_DIR` moves. The repairs are text and one comment, so they got this repository's claim-drift pass instead of a second review.

## [0.117.3] - 2026-11-01

**`claudemd-cli` is published to npm through npm trusted publishing (GitHub Actions OIDC) instead of a stored `NPM_TOKEN`. 0.117.2 never reached npm, and README's example pinned it; the pin now names 0.117.3.**

**What changes for users.**
- Nothing in the plugin: `hooks/`, `spec/`, `commands/`, `scripts/` and `bin/` are as in 0.117.2.
- 0.117.2's npm publish failed with E403 ("Two-factor authentication is required to publish this package but an automation token was specified"): the package refuses tokens, and the workflow still sent one. `claudemd-cli@0.117.2` does not exist on npm, while README's GitHub Actions example ran `npx --yes claudemd-cli@0.117.2`. The example now pins `claudemd-cli@0.117.3`; a workflow that copied the 0.117.2 pin needs the same change. The npm package's code (`bin/claudemd-lint.js`, `scripts/lib/lint.js`, `scripts/lib/argv.js`, `hooks/banned-vocab.patterns`) is the same as 0.117.1's.

No action is needed for plugin users. To go back, follow `docs/ROLLBACK.md` ("Local machine needs the previous version back").

**Publishing** (d72b804). npm-publish.yml's `publish` job runs on Node 24 (trusted publishing needs npm 11.5.1 or later and Node 22.14.0 or later; Node 20 and 22 bundle npm 10) and passes no token: `NODE_AUTH_TOKEN` and `secrets.NPM_TOKEN` are gone from the workflow. npm exchanges the job's OIDC token (the `id-token: write` that 0.117.2 limited to this job) for a short-lived publish credential; the trusted publisher on npmjs.com is GitHub `sdsrss/claudemd`, workflow `npm-publish.yml`, no environment, with `npm publish` among its allowed actions (a publisher created after 2026-09-03 allows only `npm stage publish` unless that is ticked). `--provenance` and `registry-url` stay. npm falls back to a token in the environment whenever the OIDC exchange fails; with a token the package accepted, a wrong trust configuration would still publish and the job would stay green. The package refuses tokens today, so with none in the workflow a successful publish went through OIDC, and that stays true if the setting is relaxed. The workflow header, ci.yml's comments and the action-pin test's text no longer describe a stored token.

**Not changed.**
- The `NPM_TOKEN` repository secret still exists; no workflow reads it.
- setup-node stays at v6.5.0, which writes a placeholder `NODE_AUTH_TOKEN` when `registry-url` is set (v7 dropped it). npm cannot publish with that placeholder, so it does not bring the token fallback back.
- `spec/`: D#613, the §7 push check that reads a failed Dependabot job as red CI, is still open.

**Tests.**
- `tests/scripts/actions-sha-pin.test.js`: no workflow line other than a full-line comment supplies an npm token: `NPM_TOKEN` or `NODE_AUTH_TOKEN` in any case, `_authToken`, `toJSON(secrets)` or `secrets[…]` (failed on de77844 at `npm-publish.yml:228`, and on each of those forms inserted into 321b580's workflow); a control pins what the pattern does and does not see.
- On the tagged commit (workflow steps and `scripts/` as in 321b580; the later commit changes a test, comments and this entry): `npm run check` exits 0, the node suite 1726 of 1728 passed with 2 skipped and every shell suite passed; `npm run smoke` passes 2 of 2 (on 077c984, same code). No `hooks/` file changed. PR CI: 7 of 7 jobs green on 321b580 (run 41322599302).
- The publish path runs only on a tag, so this release's npm-publish run is its end-to-end test, read from `npm view claudemd-cli@0.117.3 _npmUser`, which must name GitHub Actions rather than a person, not from the job's colour.

**Pre-tag review.** One round by a fresh reviewer (de77844..321b580): 0 Critical, 0 High, 3 Medium, 2 Low. M1: the reason given for removing the token said a wrong trust configuration would still publish through it, but this package refuses tokens, so that fallback fails today; the text now says what holds. M2: the token test matched only the upper-case names, while secret names resolve case-insensitively (`secrets.npm_token`); it now also catches `_authToken`, `toJSON(secrets)` and `secrets[…]`. M3: npm's trusted publisher has a fifth setting, allowed actions, and one created after 2026-09-03 allows only `npm stage publish` unless `npm publish` is ticked; the workflow header and this entry name it. L1: workflow comments said no token is in the environment, while setup-node v6.5.0 sets a placeholder; reworded. L2: the comment stripper the token test shared also cut a ` #` inside a quoted run-script string; the token test now skips only full-line comments. All five were repaired in the last commit (one test, comments and this entry), so no second round ran. d72b804's message still carries the M1 sentence; it was pushed before the review.

## [0.117.2] - 2026-11-01

**The plugin cache no longer gets claudemd's devDependencies: the repo stops tracking `package-lock.json`, the file Claude Code installs a plugin's dependencies from. `/claudemd-doctor` no longer offers `/claudemd-statusline remove` for a missing status-line renderer that a superseded provider runs.**

**What changes for users.**
- Claude Code installs a plugin's dependencies when the plugin's folder holds `package.json` and a lockfile. claudemd's `package-lock.json` listed only devDependencies (eslint, @eslint/js, c8, prettier, jscpd, acorn, globals), which only the repo's own lint and metrics tooling uses and no hook or command loads, so each cached version carried a 37M `node_modules` of its 47M. From 0.117.2 there is no lockfile, so nothing is installed. Caches of earlier versions keep theirs until the plugin's cache cleanup removes them (it keeps three versions).
- After `statusline-adopt.js adopt --supersede=_previous`, code-graph's `_previous` entry, which runs `~/.claude/claudemd-statusline.sh`, sits in claudemd's restore record. If that file was missing, the doctor row offered `/claudemd-statusline remove`; that remove restores `_previous` and keeps the file for it, so the row stayed red. The row now advises `/claudemd-install` alone in that state.
- `detect --json` lists a host provider whose command runs the renderer and whose `id` is missing, empty or not a string as `(no id)` in `rendererRunBy` and in `remove`'s `rendererKept`; a missing one was `null`. It still keeps the file: code-graph's composite runs every entry, with or without an id.

No action is needed. To go back, follow `docs/ROLLBACK.md` ("Local machine needs the previous version back").

**Plugin dependencies** (489cb89, D#612). Claude Code 2.1.316 does not run npm in the plugin folder. It writes a rebuilt `package.json` (named `plugin-dependencies`, devDependencies kept) and the lockfile's package list into a temporary folder, runs `npm ci --ignore-scripts --workspaces=false --no-audit --no-fund` there and moves `node_modules` in; the 0.117.1 cache's `node_modules/.package-lock.json` carries that name. An `.npmrc` with `omit=dev` in this repo is never read on that path, so the lockfile went instead. It is gitignored, `package.json` already pins every devDependency to an exact version, and the two CI `static` jobs run `npm install --ignore-scripts --no-audit --no-fund` without setup-node's npm cache, which keys on a lockfile. The devDependencies' own dependencies now resolve fresh on each CI run, with no integrity pin, so `--ignore-scripts` keeps their install scripts from running (none of the 120 packages the last lockfile held had one), and npm-publish.yml grants `id-token: write` to its `publish` job only instead of to the whole workflow, so neither the `static` job nor the `test` matrix can mint the token that signs provenance. `version-cascade-check` drops the lockfile's two copies, so the plugin version has five sites (the four manifest fields and README's `claudemd-cli@` pin).

**Statusline** (621a984, D#618). `detect()` returns `rendererRestoredBy`: with claudemd's guest entry registered, the superseded providers in `statusline-prev.json`, other than an entry with claudemd's own id, that `remove()` puts back and whose command runs the renderer. doctor offers `remove` for a missing renderer only for claudemd's own slot, or for a guest entry when `rendererRunBy` and `rendererRestoredBy` are both empty. `rendererInUse` and `rendererRunBy` are unchanged, since a superseded provider renders nothing until it is restored.

**Not changed.**
- `hooks/`, `spec/` and `commands/`. The `/claudemd-statusline` page does not name `rendererRestoredBy`; its remove transition text already says the renderer stays for a superseded provider the remove restores.
- The spec's §7 check before a push still reads `gh run list --branch … --limit 1`, which returns a failed Dependabot job first when that is the newest run (D#613).
- npm publishing still uses the `NPM_TOKEN` secret.
- After `adopt --supersede=claudemd` (claudemd's own entry superseded), doctor still offers remove for a missing renderer, as 0.117.1 did; that remove puts claudemd's entry back, so the row stays red.

**Tests.**
- `tests/scripts/version-cascade-check.test.js`: git tracks none of the six lockfile names Claude Code knows (`package-lock.json`, `npm-shrinkwrap.json`, `bun.lock`, `bun.lockb`, `yarn.lock`, `pnpm-lock.yaml`) at the root, and every devDependency is an exact version (failed before 489cb89 on the tracked `package-lock.json`); a stray local lockfile is not a version site.
- `tests/scripts/statusline-adopt.test.js`: after `adopt({ supersede: '_previous' })`, `rendererRestoredBy` is `['_previous']` and `remove()`'s `rendererKept` equals it with the file kept; a superseded provider that runs another script gives `[]` and the file is deleted; a restore record without the guest entry gives `[]`; an id-less entry running the renderer is listed as `(no id)` and keeps the file. Each failed before 621a984.
- `tests/scripts/doctor.test.js`: the supersede state built by the real `adopt()`, the renderer deleted, the row advises `/claudemd-install` only, and running `scripts/install.js` turns it green (failed before 621a984: the row offered remove).
- `tests/scripts/actions-sha-pin.test.js`: every install-family npm command in a workflow (`install`, `ci`, `install-test`, `install-ci-test` and the aliases npm lists for them, options in front included) passes `--ignore-scripts` (not `=false`), and `id-token: write` is granted once, inside npm-publish.yml's `publish` job (quoted, flow-map and `permissions: write-all` forms count as grants); both failed on the reviewed commit 3f5665e (the two static installs; the workflow-level grant). A control pins each pattern on the shapes it must and must not match.
- Mutants that drop doctor's `rendererRestoredBy` clause, drop the guest-entry condition, restore the `undefined` id or filter id-less entries out each fail one named case.
- On the tagged commit (its `scripts/` and workflow steps as in 9532857; the later commit changes tests, comments and this entry): `npm run check` exits 0, the node suite 1724 of 1726 passed with 2 skipped and every shell suite passed; `npm run smoke` passes 2 of 2 (on 3f5665e, whose `scripts/` match). No `hooks/` file changed, so no bash 3.2 run. PR CI: 7 of 7 jobs green on 3f5665e (run 41312476271) and on 9532857 (run 41314477851).

**Pre-tag review.** Two rounds by fresh reviewers. Round 1 (e09f254..3f5665e): 0 Critical, 0 High, 1 Medium, 7 Low. M1: with no lockfile the static jobs' unlocked `npm install` would run any install script a dependency ships, in a workflow that granted `id-token: write` to every job; repaired in 9532857 (`--ignore-scripts`, the grant moved to the `publish` job) with tests that failed before it. Five Lows were wording: four in the repo (the `(no id)` label's reach, `bun.lockb` listed as installed, "runs `npm ci` on" the plugin, a stale six-site comment) and a site count in the maintainer's local release notes; two were statusline states 0.117.1 already had, deferred (D#628), one of them listed under Not changed. Round 2 (3f5665e..9532857): 0 Critical, 0 High, 0 Medium, 4 Low: comments still describing a workflow-wide grant, test patterns that missed a quoted grant, `write-all`, `--ignore-scripts=false` and other npm install spellings, and three wordings; repaired in the last commit (tests, comments and this entry). 489cb89's message lists `bun.lockb` among the lockfiles Claude Code installs from and 9532857's says the old install "ran" install scripts; both were pushed before the review and are not rewritten.

## [0.117.1] - 2026-11-01

**The ship-baseline gate no longer reads GitHub's failed Dependabot security-update job as red CI; claudemd's status-line renderer is refreshed and checked when code-graph runs it under another provider id, and `/claudemd-statusline` no longer adds a second claudemd segment there; one `/claudemd-doctor` sentence no longer calls a checkout a real install; spec unchanged (v8.3.0).** Apart from the version in the two `.claude-plugin/` manifests, five shipped files change: `hooks/ship-baseline-check.sh`, `scripts/lib/statusline.js`, `scripts/statusline-adopt.js` (its `--help` text and the human-readable `remove` line), `scripts/doctor.js` and `commands/claudemd-statusline.md`.

**What changes for users.**
- A `git push` that the ship-baseline gate checks is no longer denied when the newest completed run on the branch is GitHub's Dependabot security-update job (event `dynamic`, workflow `Dependabot Updates`), which fails whenever no fix can be applied. The gate passes over runs that have both of those values when it picks the baseline run and when it records the status for `pending-no-baseline`. Every other run counts as before, including other `dynamic` runs and a repository workflow named `Dependabot Updates`. Passing over those runs also changes two other outcomes: when the newest completed run is a successful Dependabot job and the newest counted run behind it is red, the push is now denied (0.117.0 allowed it on the Dependabot run's colour); and when all five runs the gate reads are Dependabot jobs, it records `pending-no-baseline` with status `unknown` and allows the push (0.117.0 decided on the newest of them, denying when it had failed).
- code-graph's composite status line can hold claudemd's command under its own provider id (`_previous`, kept when code-graph took over a slot claudemd had). claudemd is then not a registered guest, so nothing refreshed `~/.claude/claudemd-statusline.sh` and doctor said the renderer was not required. In that state, install now refreshes the file (`statusline.action: renderer-refreshed`) without touching `settings.json`, the host's registry or its mirror, and no longer prints the note that tells you to add a claudemd segment; `statusline-adopt.js detect --json` has a new `rendererInUse` field; doctor's `statusline` row checks the file, so a missing or stale copy is a red row that counts, as it already was for a registered guest; `detect --json` also lists those providers' ids in `rendererRunBy`; and `/claudemd-statusline` reports the provider id and stops, where it used to register a guest that showed claudemd's segment twice.
- If 0.117.0's `/claudemd-statusline` already added that second segment, `/claudemd-statusline remove` now takes out the duplicate: it unregisters claudemd's own entry and keeps `~/.claude/claudemd-statusline.sh`, which the other entry runs, and its JSON names that entry in `rendererKept`. 0.117.0's remove deleted the file, so the remaining entry printed "No such file or directory" on every render. `/claudemd-uninstall`, which calls the same remove, keeps the file in that state too.
- With `CLAUDEMD_NO_STATUSLINE=1`, install leaves the status line alone and does not refresh the renderer. In the `_previous` state doctor now checks that file, so a stale copy is a red row that counts, and the fix it prints, `/claudemd-install`, does not recopy the file while the opt-out is set. Run `/claudemd-statusline` instead: it offers `adopt --empty-only`, which refreshes the copy and changes no setting. For our own slot and a registered guest this was already so in 0.117.0; there `/claudemd-statusline` refreshes the copy when you ask it to (for our own slot it also offers to).
- `/claudemd-doctor`'s `hook-drift` row, when it compares against the root a hook recorded, no longer says "Both sides are real installs": the record can name a checkout.

Apart from that opt-out case and a duplicate segment 0.117.0 already added, which stays until you run `/claudemd-statusline remove`, no action is needed. To go back, follow `docs/ROLLBACK.md` ("Local machine needs the previous version back").

**Ship-baseline** (cdfdb56). On 2026-11-01 the Dependabot job for alert #5 (minimatch, a dev dependency whose only fix is a new major version) failed on `main` after v0.117.0's tag, became the newest completed run there, and the gate denied a push whose CI was green; that push went out with a `known-red baseline:` line. The gate now asks `gh` for two more JSON fields, `event` and `workflowName`; both exist in every `gh` that has `displayTitle`, which the request already needed (2.16.0 and later). The `pending-no-baseline` row in `docs/RULE-HITS-SCHEMA.md` says which run's status it carries.

**Statusline** (03d0c8c, ba19345, 274aa69, and the pre-tag review repair). `detect()` counts a host provider as running the renderer when its command contains `claudemd-statusline.sh`, the same test it applies to the slot, whatever the provider's id. code-graph's composite expands `$HOME` in a command only for `_previous`, so a `$HOME` form under another id is counted although it does not render. `guestRegistered` keeps its meaning (our own entry). With only `_previous` running the file, `/claudemd-statusline remove` still returns `not-ours` and keeps it; with our guest entry beside it, remove unregisters ours and keeps the file. Either way remove repairs nothing while another provider runs the file, so when the file is missing, doctor's advice there is `/claudemd-install` alone, without the `/claudemd-statusline remove` alternative it gives for our own slot and for a guest entry no other provider shares. `statusline-adopt.js --help` now says `--empty-only` also refreshes a renderer copy something already runs, and README's command row says when `remove` keeps the file and when the command stops instead of registering. The command page change restores documented behaviour: the command says it registers idempotently. The decision record is `tasks/statusline-host-wrap-deferred.md`.

**Doctor** (427da14). The `hook-drift` text for a hook-recorded root dropped the clause; the rest of the sentence and both fixes it names hold for either kind of root.

**Not changed.**
- `hooks/` apart from `ship-baseline-check.sh`, and `spec/`. The spec's §7 check before a push still reads `gh run list --branch … --limit 1`, which returns a failed Dependabot job first when that is the newest run, so an agent following the spec text can read red where the hook passes.
- doctor still prints `/claudemd-install` as the fix under the opt-out above.
- After `statusline-adopt.js adopt --supersede=_previous`, `rendererRunBy` is empty, so for a missing renderer doctor still offers `/claudemd-statusline remove`, as 0.117.0 did; that remove restores `_previous`, which runs the same file, so it keeps the file and the renderer stays missing.
- A host provider entry with no `id` whose command runs the renderer is listed in `rendererRunBy` as `null`. code-graph writes an `id` on every entry.

**Tests.**
- `tests/hooks/ship-baseline.test.sh`: cases 63–64 (a failed Dependabot job newest with green CI behind it; only a Dependabot job completed) were denied before the change; controls 65–66 (another `dynamic` run; a push-triggered workflow with that name) stay denied, and each fails when its half of the condition is removed. Cases 67 (a green Dependabot job in front of a red CI run: deny) and 68 (five Dependabot jobs fill the window: `pending-no-baseline`, status `unknown`) fail on 0.117.0's hook, which allowed the push in 67 and denied it in 68.
- `tests/scripts/statusline-adopt.test.js`: in the `_previous` state, install-time adopt refreshes the file and writes nothing else (it failed before 03d0c8c), its dry run writes nothing, and `remove()` returns `not-ours` with the file and registry kept. With our guest entry beside `_previous`, `remove()` unregisters only ours, keeps the file and returns `rendererKept: ['_previous']`; with our guest entry alone it deletes the file and returns `rendererKept: null` (both failed before the review repair).
- `tests/scripts/doctor.test.js`: the `_previous` state end to end (stale renderer, red row, `scripts/install.js`, green row, no segment note; before 03d0c8c the row said "renderer not required"); a missing renderer in that state is advised `/claudemd-install` only (failed before 274aa69), so is one that our guest entry and `_previous` both run (failed before the review repair), and the own-slot and guest cases now assert the remove clause; a hook-recorded checkout no longer reads "real installs" (failed before 427da14 on that assertion).
- `tests/scripts/command-page-behaviour.test.js`: the page's three host-branch lines, its remove transition line and its remove report line, with `detect --json` and `adopt --empty-only` run on the `_previous` state in a sandbox HOME (failed before ba19345: the page lacked the line).
- Mutants that drop `detect()`'s provider-command test, the install-time refresh branch, the remove clause's condition, `remove()`'s keep test or doctor's `rendererRunBy` check each fail a named case.
- On the tagged commit (its `hooks/` and `scripts/` as in 695c7b3; the later commit changes tests, text and the command page's remove transition line): `npm run check` exits 0, the node suite 1716 of 1718 passed with 2 skipped and every shell suite passed; the 44 hook suites pass with the hooks executed under a locally built bash 3.2.57; `npm run smoke` passes 2 of 2. PR CI: 7 of 7 jobs green on 1db0f3f (run 41296282880) and a556226 (run 41298453342).

**Pre-tag review.** Two rounds by fresh reviewers. Round 1 (eb499c5..1db0f3f): 0 Critical, 0 High, 1 Medium, 8 Low. M1: with our guest entry beside code-graph's `_previous` (the duplicate 0.117.0's page produced), `remove` deleted the renderer `_previous` runs and doctor offered it as a fix for a missing file; repaired in 695c7b3 (`rendererRunBy`, `rendererKept`), with tests that failed before it. The Lows were text, repaired in 695c7b3 and a556226: alert #5 was already dismissed, the opt-out case sat under "Not changed", two other ship-baseline outcomes were missing, README and `--help` predated the change, a claim that a provider runs the renderer whatever its id, and the spec's own `--limit 1` check (left as is, above). Round 2 (1db0f3f..a556226, a new spawn because M1 changed code): 0 Critical, 0 High, 0 Medium, 7 Low. It compared `rendererInUse` over 1508 generated states with 1db0f3f (0 differences) and ran uninstall in both guest states. Two Lows are the `--supersede=_previous` gap and the entry without an `id`, both listed under "Not changed" and deferred; the others were the page's remove transition line, an overstated refresh offer and "no action is needed", the decision record, and no test for the two other ship-baseline outcomes (cases 67–68 above). Those were repaired in the tagged commit through the claim-drift pass, without a third round, since they change no code under `hooks/` or `scripts/`.

## [0.117.0] - 2026-11-01

**`/claudemd-sampling-audit` reports all eight detectors closed and no longer reads a dropped API response as the agent stopping; `/claudemd-doctor` no longer exits 3 on a row whose compared root is only a guess; no hook changes; spec unchanged (v8.3.0).** Apart from the version in the two `.claude-plugin/` manifests, four shipped files change: `scripts/sampling-audit.js`, `scripts/doctor.js`, and one sentence each in `commands/claudemd-audit.md` and `commands/claudemd-sampling-audit.md`.

**What changes for users.** `/claudemd-sampling-audit` and the `selfCompliance` section of `/claudemd-audit` now report `status: "closed"` with a `closedReason` for §10-V and §11-turn-yield, as they already did for the other six; in their `--json` rows `closedReason` replaces the `note` key. The closure changes no number: `selfCompliance.rate` was null and stays null, and `/claudemd-sampling-audit` prints a violations/opportunities ratio for every rule, as before, as collection data.
