The API-error fix below does lower §11-turn-yield's count. §11-turn-yield stops counting a "继续" typed after Claude Code's own `API Error: …` record. `/claudemd-doctor`: when `installed_plugins.json` names no usable claudemd install, three rows that compare against the newest version directory in the plugin cache (`hook-drift`, `plugin cache:staleness`, `plugin-root:stale-registration`) still print their red line but carry `advisory: true`, say they do not count, and no longer set exit code 3. With a usable registry entry nothing changes, and `hook-drift` also stays counted whenever a hook has recorded the root it runs from. No action is needed; to go back, follow `docs/ROLLBACK.md` ("Local machine needs the previous version back").

**Sampling audit** (869f04e, 98cb71d). The two detectors the 2026-07-24 pass left `collecting` were waiting for a re-label of ~50 flagged samples each (the pre-registered calibration gate). Every transcript still on disk (Claude Code deletes them after 30 days) held 3 flags for each — on one fixed window, `--global --days=60 --until=2026-11-01T07:40:00Z` (147 main transcripts on disk at the cutoff across all projects, the oldest from 2026-10-06; the 136 with assistant text in the window are the ones scanned), 3 of 8293 opportunities for §10-V and 3 of 1042 for §11-turn-yield. At that rate 50 can never accumulate, so the pass labeled the whole flagged population: three raters, each without the others' labels, labeled the 6 flagged items, the turn-yield ones mixed with 9 unflagged controls so the raters could not tell which were flagged (§10-V had no unflagged turn holding a banned word to mix in, so its three were all flagged). 0 of the 6 were violations, unanimous on all six.
- §10-V: two quoted mentions of a banned word (a test commit message, a discussion of the word list) and one remark about the statistical significance of an external metric. With none of the three a violation, the 95% upper bound on its precision is 0.71, below the 0.8 gate. Closed.
- §11-turn-yield: all three followed the record Claude Code writes when the API drops a response (`isApiErrorMessage: true`, model `<synthetic>`). The scanner took its text as the agent's last words. The record now clears the prior text, so the existing exemption for a turn cut off externally applies; on the window above it flags 3 of 1042 with 0.116.6's scanner and 0 of 1042 with this one (§10-V: 3 of 8293 on both). With nothing left to calibrate, closed.

The record is the 2026-11-01 section of `tasks/sampling-detector-labeling-2026-07-24.md`; `spec/OPERATOR.md` already listed the A4 measurement track as closed. H4's `ASK_RATE_PRECISION` is outside this pass and stays null.

**Doctor** (bba4536). With no hook record and no usable registry entry, `activePluginRoot()` and `runningPluginRoot()` fall through to `plugin-cache`: the newest semver directory in the cache, which nothing checks Claude Code loaded. In a sandbox HOME with a checkout, a healthy install and an interrupted install's partial `0.200.0` directory, `hook-drift` was the only counted red row — "33 hook script(s) differ" against a directory nothing runs — and doctor exited 3; with that leftover's `package.json` present, `plugin cache:staleness` joined it, and `plugin-root:stale-registration` reads the same guess from a cache dir. 0.85.0 made `hook-drift` count again because the running root had become measurable; on this basis it is not. The three rows now carry `advisory: true` on this basis, and the CLI exit code is computed by the newly exported `countsTowardExit`, which skips them. Rows on the `installed-plugins` and `hook-fired` bases stay counted. The pre-tag review found one more way for the guess to decide: `hook-drift` skipped itself whenever `plugin-root:stale-registration` fired, so on this basis a guessed registration silenced a drift measured from a hook record (exit 0 with a leftover, 3 without). That skip now applies only when the registration is not the guess. The decision record, `tasks/hook-drift-plugin-cache-basis-not-verified.md`, is now tracked.

**Statusline tests** (64c4c6d). The four coverage gaps the v0.26.0 review listed: the `code-graph` / `claudemd` id exclusions in `manualPsCandidates` now decide a case; `cgRead` prefers the primary when primary and mirror disagree; `detect()`'s `providers[]` is asserted; and the four `install.js` statusline notes are asserted by text. No product code changes.

**Not changed.**
- `hooks/` and `spec/`. The §8 gate: F27 (a backtick before quoted prose read as a command position) and F34 (a fetch-and-execute inside a `case` arm) stay accepted residuals in `docs/S8-RESIDUALS.md`; the two notes that asked for a decision now record it.
- `commands/claudemd-doctor.md` does not mention the new `advisory` field; the row's own text says it does not count.
- `remove()` still leaves `~/.claude/claudemd-statusline.sh` when the slot is a composite host that does not list claudemd. Deleting it there was proposed in July; on the maintainer's machine code-graph runs that renderer under its own `_previous` provider id, so the file is in use in exactly that state. The same state keeps claudemd from refreshing the file or reporting it stale; that is a separate change.

**Tests.**

## [0.116.6] - 2026-10-25

**`CLAUDEMD_SPEC_ACTION=restore` passes over a backup that holds only spec files; no hook or command changes; spec unchanged (v8.3.0).** Apart from the version in the two `.claude-plugin/` manifests, five shipped files change: `scripts/uninstall.js`, `scripts/lib/backup.js` (the test restore applies, and a comment), `scripts/doctor.js` (the text of one check and a comment), and `scripts/update.js` and `scripts/install.js` (a comment each).

**Restore** (9109e50, and the pre-tag review repair). `/claudemd-uninstall restore` puts back the `~/.claude/CLAUDE*.md` you had before claudemd, from the newest `~/.claude/backup-<stamp>/`. Before 0.68.3, `/claudemd-update` backed up the spec files it was about to replace into that same namespace, every one that differed from the plugin's, so on a machine that ran it then, the newest dirs can hold only old spec files while your own `CLAUDE.md` sits in an older one, and restore put the old spec back. It now passes over a dir that holds only spec files: `CLAUDE.md` is in it, and every entry is one of `CLAUDE.md`, `CLAUDE-extended.md`, `CLAUDE-changelog.md` and `OPERATOR.md`, a regular file whose first line is the `# AI-CODING-SPEC …` heading, the line every spec file from 0.1.0 to 0.68.2 opens with. It restores the newest dir left. A spec you appended your own notes to still opens with that heading, so a dir holding one is passed over when an older backup is left; its path is on stderr. Not passed over: a dir with any other entry, which may be a backup from an install before 0.23.11 with your files beside the spec; a dir whose files are links, or that cannot be listed or read; and, when every backup holds only spec files, the newest one, as before. Two layouts are left as in 0.116.5. An update dir without `CLAUDE.md` (from an update where `CLAUDE.md` matched the plugin's: 2 of the 47 tag-to-tag steps up to 0.68.2 that changed a spec file) is not passed over, and restore puts its old spec files back and leaves `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` as it is. And the search stops at the first dir it does not pass over: an empty dir, or one holding only a `hooks/` subdirectory, between the spec-only dirs and yours is what restore uses (a restore that copies nothing exits 3 with `restore-empty`), and a dir there that cannot be listed makes restore fail with `EACCES`. The dirs passed over stay on disk and are listed in a new `skippedSpecOnly` field of the JSON output and on stderr, with the dir restored; to restore one of them anyway, copy its files back by hand (`cp ~/.claude/backup-<stamp>/CLAUDE.md ~/.claude/`). `/claudemd-doctor`'s `backup-namespace-legacy` row said that every dir it lists makes restore return a spec; it now marks the ones restore passes over, with the same test.

**Not changed.** `commands/claudemd-uninstall.md` still says restore uses "the most recent backup" and does not mention `skippedSpecOnly`, and `commands/claudemd-install.md` still says restore "cannot return a spec instead of the user's own file", which holds for the backups install writes but not for the pre-0.68.3 update dirs above. Both are text the model reads; the restore output names the dirs it passed over.

**Tests.**
- `tests/scripts/uninstall.test.js`: eight cases. A newer backup holding a lone spec `CLAUDE.md`, and one holding the four spec files, are each passed over with a manifest and after a default uninstall removed it (four cases); the CLI names the dir passed over and the dir restored, in its JSON and on stderr. A newest dir that cannot be listed is not passed over, and restore fails on it with `EACCES`, as 0.116.5 does on the same layout. Two control cases: each of eight newest dirs is restored (with a sibling file, with a subdirectory, with a symlinked `CLAUDE.md`, with a `CLAUDE.md` that is not spec-shaped, with a sibling spec file name the user wrote, with the heading below the first line, with a spec-headed file under another name, and with spec files but no `CLAUDE.md`), and when every backup holds only spec files the newest is restored. On 0.116.5 the four pass-over cases and the CLI case fail and the other three pass; on 9109e50's code (before the review repair) the two four-file cases, the CLI case, the unlistable case and the first control fail. Ten mutants each fail a named case: no spec-name check, `CLAUDE.md` not required, `stat` for `lstat`, the loose heading test, an unlistable dir counted as spec-only, either result field dropped, no fallback to the newest, the stderr line without the restored dir, and doctor marking nothing.
- `tests/scripts/doctor.test.js`: one case for the new `backup-namespace-legacy` text over four dirs (a lone spec, a spec with a notes file, two spec files, a symlinked spec); it fails on 0.116.5's text and on 9109e50's.
- On the tagged commit (it differs from 6d57653 only in this entry): `npm run check` exits 0, the node suite 1697 of 1699 passed with 2 skipped and every shell suite passed; `npm run smoke` passes 2 of 2. PR CI: 7 of 7 jobs green on 990e578 (run 40166174756), b71b1ba (run 40171689494) and 6d57653 (run 40173130113).

**Pre-tag review.** Two rounds by fresh reviewers. Round 1 (01950e0..990e578): 0 Critical, 1 High, 0 Medium, 8 Low. The High: a pre-0.68.3 `/claudemd-update` backed up every spec file that differed from the plugin's, so a real update dir holds several spec files, and the first version passed over only a dir holding a lone `CLAUDE.md`; a replay of v0.68.2's `update.js` showed restore still putting back `# AI-CODING-SPEC v6.24.0`. The rule became the one above (80b46b8), and with it the Lows that were code: a dir that could not be listed was read as holding nothing else (L1), the spec test took the heading anywhere in the first 256 characters (L2), doctor said restore returns a `+` dir only when it is the newest (L3), and no test pinned the restored dir on stderr (L4); the other four were text (L5–L8, 80b46b8 and b71b1ba). Round 2, on the repair (990e578..b71b1ba): 0 Critical, 0 High, 1 Medium, 6 Low, all text or tests, repaired in the tagged commit's parent. The Medium: the README and `--help` read as if every update dir were passed over, while one without `CLAUDE.md` is not; that layout and the empty-dir one (L1) are now stated above as left as before. The Lows: `backup.js`'s namespace comment, README's install paragraph and the legacy-backup note still described restore in the present tense of 0.116.5 or said it never returns a spec (L2–L4); the design record said dirs with user files are unaffected, while a spec with appended notes is passed over (L5); the symlink control failed under a relative `TMPDIR` (L6, the link target is now absolute). Not changed: 9109e50's and cb4e842's messages describe the first version's rule, and 9109e50's counts four controls where the tests have two cases; all are published as written. The round-2 repair changes comments, text and tests (the symlink control's path, and the README line `readme-drift.test.js` pins), so it got a claim-drift pass instead of a third review.

## [0.116.5] - 2026-10-24

**`version-cascade-check` checks README's pinned `claudemd-cli` version; nine §8 residual families registered, with no verdict changes; no hook or command changes; spec unchanged (v8.3.0).** Apart from the version in the two `.claude-plugin/` manifests, two shipped files change: `scripts/version-cascade-check.js` and `spec/hard-rules.json`, where only the review dates move.

**README pin** (5d646db). README's GitHub Actions example runs `npx --yes claudemd-cli@X.Y.Z lint --stdin` on every commit of a pull request. The example arrived in 0.115.0 already pinned to 0.113.4 and kept that pin through 0.116.4, six releases, while the lint ruleset moved under it. `runPluginSemverCheck` now treats each `claudemd-cli@X.Y.Z` in `README.md` as a site of its own, named by its line, beside the six manifest sites; a README with no pin adds none. The example now pins this release, and `docs/ADDING-NEW-HOOK.md` and `docs/ROLLBACK.md` no longer say "six".

**§8 residual families F28–F36** (90d2afd, and F36 from the pre-tag review). `docs/S8-RESIDUALS.md` lists the shapes the §8 gate is known to allow and has accepted, so that a review files each once. A triage of old task notes found eight families the gate allows that no row listed, and the pre-tag review a ninth. They are registered under that file's review rule 4, not patched, each with `xfn` corpus rows and controls:
- F28: a quoted runner name, `"bash" -c '…'`, is not unwrapped; the unquoted form is.
- F29: commands run by `xargs`, such as `cat list | xargs rm -rf`, a fetched script given to `xargs bash`, and `xargs npx unpinned-pkg`.
- F30: a fetch fed to a shell through a here-string, `sh <<< "$(curl …)"`.
- F31: an rm target that is a command substitution, `rm -rf $(cat list)`.
- F32: a command word taken from a variable, `R=rm; $R -rf "$Y"`, and mktemp provenance revoked through an indirect name (`unset "$T"`, `printf -v "$T"`, `declare -n`).
- F33: `$((1<<n))` read as a heredoc opener and closed by a later line `n`.
- F34: fetch-and-run in a `case` arm. The rm and npx checks deny the same shape. The 2026-09-08 decision to change the four fetch anchors only on a listed trigger stands.
- F35: a literal root target: `rm -rf /`, `rm -rf /*`, `rm -rf --no-preserve-root /`, `find / -delete`. The gate checks unvalidated variables, and its deny text says a literal path passes. GNU rm's default `--preserve-root` refuses only `/` itself as an argument.
- F36: a runner named by its path, `/bin/bash -c '…'`, `sudo /bin/bash -c '…'`, `/usr/bin/ssh host '…'`, is not unwrapped; `/usr/bin/env bash -c` and `command bash -c` are.

Over the 110,056 unique Bash commands in this machine's transcripts, the task-work hits for F29 are 6 `xargs rm` cleanups (4 `find … -print0 | xargs -0 -r rm -rf --` under literal paths, one `ls | grep -v … | xargs -r rm -f --` inside `"${SP:?}"`, one `xargs -r -d '\n' rm -f -- < <list file>`), all allowed, which a deny for `xargs rm` would have stopped, and 29 commands that run `sh -c` (11) or a `.sh` script (18) through xargs, most of them parallel runners (`xargs -P N …`), none fed fetched content. F32's 8 matches with an indirect `unset "$v"` (6 loops over environment names, 2 heredoc bodies written to files) guard their rm target with `:?`, so mktemp provenance does not decide them. F36's 3 matches are prose and two `/bin/sh -c` commands with nothing to deny. Every other match is a probe, test text or prose.

**§13.1 review dates** (f502b78). The 26 HARD rules' `last_demote_review` stood at 2026-08-16 (25 rules) and 2026-09-07 (1), 69 and 47 days against the 28-day cadence `hard-rules-audit` and `spec/OPERATOR.md` §13.1 set. The review ran on the 30-day window over a 49-day log. Its one candidate, `§7-user-global-state`, is kept: `residue-audit` writes a row only when residue grows past its threshold, so no rows in the window is that alarm's steady state, and the rule is also self-enforced. No rule is demoted and no rule text changes; all 26 dates read 2026-10-24.

**Documents.** The 36 notes under `tasks/` that doctor's `tasks-review-cadence` listed as untouched for 30+ days each end with a dated triage section (8489493): 12 closed, 5 obsolete, 12 partly closed, 5 waiting on a maintainer decision, 2 open, one of them on purpose. 26 of them are tracked.

**Not changed.** `CLAUDEMD_SPEC_ACTION=restore` still restores the newest personal-namespace backup. On a machine that ran `/claudemd-update` before 0.68.3, that can be a spec-only backup rather than the user's own `CLAUDE.md`. `/claudemd-doctor`'s `backup-namespace-legacy` row names such directories. Skipping them would also skip the pre-0.23.11 backups that hold the user's files next to a spec-shaped `CLAUDE.md` (`tasks/legacy-spec-backup-migration.md`), so the choice is left open.

**Tests.**
- `tests/scripts/version-cascade-check.test.js`: one case. Two README pins, one stale, fail; one matching pin passes and adds one site; an unpinned mention adds none. It failed before the change ("a stale README pin must fail"), and three real-repo cases (two CLI runs and the repository's own `runPluginSemverCheck`) went red on README's 0.113.4 until the pin moved.
- `tests/fixtures/bash-safety/corpus.tsv`: 45 rows for F28–F36: 33 `xfn`, and 1 `pass` and 11 `deny` controls. With all 45 labels inverted, all 45 fail (1224 of 1269 pass).

## [0.116.4] - 2026-10-23

**`doc-check` resolves a `§`-reference to the document's own numbered heading; no hook or command changes; spec unchanged (v8.3.0).** Apart from the version in the two `.claude-plugin/` manifests, one shipped file changes: `scripts/doc-check.mjs`.

**Anchors** (ce2abcb). `scripts/doc-check.mjs`, the self-check for long-form analysis documents, looked a `§`-reference up only in the spec sources (`spec/CLAUDE.md`, `spec/CLAUDE-extended.md`, `spec/hard-rules.json`, `scripts/sampling-audit.js`), so a document that cites its own sections failed once per such reference: the 8 anchor problems `docs/spec-model-capability-review-2026-10-15.md` reported once this release's §9.5 was in were all its own headings (`§3.2`, `§3.6`, `§3.7`, `§7.3` and `§9.1`–`§9.4`; 6 on 0.116.3's text). A heading line such as `## 3.` or `### 9.4 …` now defines its number; a body line that starts with the number defines nothing, but, as for the `### G<n>` headings the G-ref check reads, a `#` line inside a fenced code block counts as a heading. A spec anchor renamed while the document has a heading with the same number still resolves, so the `anchors:` line now says how many references only a heading resolved (`; N by the document's own headings`), and a failure reads `not found in spec sources or the document headings`.

**Documents.**
- `docs/S8-RESIDUALS.md`, E3 (d) (385a152): located. The slow step is the inner `${x##*[![:space:]]}` of the trailing-whitespace trim each segment gets in the rm loop and in the npx loop; its time grows about with k × (n − k/2) for a segment of n characters that ends in k blanks (bash 5.3.9, this expansion alone: 0.30 / 1.16 / 4.67 s for n ≈ k = 2,500 / 5,000 / 10,000). Blanks at the start of a segment are slow too, with the square of k alone and a smaller constant: the leading trim removes them as a k-blank literal prefix, several times per segment, and the whole gate takes 0.13 / 1.15 / 4.32 s for 5,000 / 20,000 / 40,000 leading spaces, the last past the timeout. Blanks in the middle are not (40,000: 0.23 s). Not fixed, as (b) and (c) were not (D#275). Over 108,612 real commands the longest blank run is 83, and a command's length times its longest blank run peaks at 1,759,600, about 9× below the 1.6 × 10⁷ that reaches the 3 s hook timeout and about 1.5× below it after the macOS and heavy-load factors E3 lists. That bound is loose: the 8 commands with the highest product run the whole gate in 0.16–0.28 s, and on the 80 highest the pre-tag review measured a trailing run of at most 1 per segment.
- `docs/S8-RESIDUALS.md`, F27 (385a152): the two fetch-execute / reverse-shell denies logged since 0.89.0, found again by their `tool_use_id` and replayed on the current gate (both still denied), are both gate-testing text in this repository: one is Pattern 3b on a quoted heredoc line, the other the `/dev/tcp` check.
- `docs/spec-model-capability-review-2026-10-15.md`, §9.5 (cbc223a): the K6+K9 ablation is dropped, because at 20 runs per arm a drop from 100% to 80% is detected 37% of the time; the skill-usage re-scan due on 10-29 waits instead until 62 release-word sessions have run, about 11-08. Appendix C gives the document a doc-check block, and it passes its own check (acc6e08). The additions are dated by the local day (44f3d25).

**Tests.**
- `tests/scripts/doc-check.test.js`: one case. A reference to the document's own `### 9.4` and `## 3.` headings resolves and is counted; renumber the heading, or leave the number only at the start of a body line, and it fails. It and the updated message assertion failed on 0.116.3's `doc-check.mjs` (2 of 19). In a copy, three mutants (body lines count as headings; every id resolves; nothing is counted) each fail it.
- On the tagged commit (it differs from a625dab only in this entry): `npm run check` exits 0, the node suite 1687 of 1689 passed with 2 skipped and every shell suite passed; `npm run smoke` passes 2 of 2. PR CI: 7 of 7 jobs green on d0fc9a3 (run 40069021690) and on a625dab (run 40070376340).

**Pre-tag review.** One round by a fresh reviewer (c97f595..d0fc9a3): 0 Critical, 0 High, 1 Medium, 6 Low. The Medium and five Lows were repaired in text and tests (a625dab); the sixth Low is a published commit message. M1: this entry and the E3 row said blanks at the start of a segment are not slow; 40,000 take 4.32 s, past the timeout, as stated above now. L1: the n × k bound missed blanks before `#`, `(`, `)` and a backtick, so it was not an upper bound; the bound above is a command's length times its longest blank run. L3: a `#` line inside a fenced code block reads as a heading, now stated above and in the JSDoc. L4: the test now also pins that a heading resolves exactly its own number and may carry the sign; the three mutants the reviewer found passing it now fail it. L5: the E3 row's description of the linear rewrite now names its all-blank branch and that it leaves leading runs slow. L6: `docs/spec-optimization-roadmap-2026-09-21.md` says `§` always means the spec there, which doc-check no longer enforces for the numbers the roadmap uses as headings; it now says so. L2, not changed: ce2abcb's message says "all 7 anchor problems" where 8 is right, and 385a152's says leading runs are not slow (M1); both are published as written. The repair is text and test only (the one change in `scripts/doc-check.mjs` is its JSDoc), so it got a claim-drift pass instead of a second review.

## [0.116.3] - 2026-10-23

**The §8 gate starts 8 fewer `sed`s per run, and a command that ends in many newlines is parsed in one pass instead of in time that grew with their square; the same verdicts, stdout and exit codes on every replayed real command; spec unchanged (v8.3.0).** Apart from the version in the two `.claude-plugin/` manifests, two shipped files change: `hooks/pre-bash-safety-check.sh` and `hooks/lib/hook-common.sh`.

**`unwrap_indirect` in one `sed`** (363dfb0). It rewrites `bash -c '…'`, `su … -c`, `ssh host '…'` and `eval` forms so that the gate checks the command inside them, and it ran its nine `s///g` rewrites as nine `sed`s, each in its own `$(printf … | sed …)`. The nine expressions, unchanged byte for byte, now go to one `sed` as `-e` arguments in the same order. Each is a one-line substitution whose replacement adds no newline, so applying them in turn to each line gives what the chain gave. Programs started by the gate alone, counted as in 0.116.2 (a logging shim on PATH in front of 35 tools, bash 5.3.9, rule-hits logging off, an empty `HOME`, a one-commit repository): 38 → 30 for `ls /tmp >/dev/null` and for `git commit -m '…'`, 45 → 37 for `rm -rf "${D:?}/x"`; since 0.116.1, 46 → 30 and 53 → 37.

**Trailing newlines** (809410b). `hook_read_bash_fields`, the event parser through which six hooks read a Bash command (the §8 gate, `banned-vocab-check`, `ship-baseline-check`, `memory-read-check`, `branch-prune` and `verify-log`), strips the command's trailing newlines, as `$(…)` did. It removed one per loop iteration, and each iteration copied and matched the whole command, so time grew with the square of their number, once in each of those hooks. `rm -rf $X/y` followed by 40,000 newlines took 15.6 s in the §8 gate on 0.116.2 (best of 3), past its 3 s hook timeout, and a hook killed at its timeout lets the command run (residual E3 in `docs/S8-RESIDUALS.md`); the 0.116.2 pre-tag review measured about 50 s for 70,000 behind a `pip install git+…`. One newline is now removed in place and any more go through one `$(…)`: the 40,000 in 0.059 s, the 70,000 behind the pip install in 0.09 s under bash 5.3.9 and 0.14 s under bash 3.2.57, each denied. Of the 106,433 real commands replayed here, 66 end in one newline and none in more, so none of them takes the new fork. Newlines in the middle of a command are unchanged and stay under E3: 40,000 empty lines between two commands take 3.1 s under bash 5.3.9, past the timeout, and 9.2 s under bash 3.2.57, where 20,000 already take 3.4 s; the same on 0.116.2.

**Other changes.**
- The header of `hook_memfile_was_read` (`hooks/lib/hook-common.sh`) still said a subagent's reads cannot be seen and that round-16 audit 6.1/6.2 was open; 0.92.0 closed it (9a923d4), and the header now says so (897b67f). Comment only.
- Round-16 audit 6.5.2 is now pinned in the corpus: Pattern 3b reads the raw command, and its command-position anchor includes a backtick, so a commit message, echoed text or a quoted heredoc line that quotes a backtick `eval` or `sh -c` of a fetch is denied (`S8-3B1`–`S8-3B3`, `xfp`). Controls: the same text without backticks is allowed (`S8-3B4`, `S8-3B5`) and a backtick substitution that really runs it is denied (`S8-3B6`, `S8-3B7`). In a copy of the hook, dropping the backtick from the anchor flips the three `xfp` rows and releases `S8-3B6` and `S8-3B7`. The narrowing stays deferred, as since 0.89.0; `docs/S8-RESIDUALS.md` registers the family as F27 with the reasons, which until now were in an untracked note (3249197).
- `docs/spec-model-capability-review-2026-10-15.md` §9.4: an interim reading of the skill-usage re-scan due on 10-29 (f177f84).

**Replay.** Every unique Bash command in this machine's transcripts, through 0.116.2's hooks (`git archive v0.116.2 hooks`) and this release's (`git archive 3249197 hooks`; later commits change only a comment in `hooks/lib/hook-common.sh`): all 106,433 under bash 5.3.9 and all 106,433 under bash 3.2.57, each allowed or denied as before (102,130 and 4,303 under each), with the same stdout bytes and exit code. Stderr was not compared.

**Tests.**
- `tests/hooks/pre-bash-safety.test.sh`: the sed shim now also tags calls that carry `unwrap_indirect`'s command-word anchors, and an allowed command and a denied `bash -c` are each one call (9 on 0.116.2); `trailing-newline-time` requires the 40,000 newlines to be denied inside the hooks.json timeout (15.6 s on 0.116.2); the seven corpus rows above.
- `tests/hooks/hook-common.test.sh`: four cases pin the result of the stripping (every trailing newline goes, the inner ones stay); they pass on 0.116.2 and on this release.
- On the tagged commit (its code and tests differ from df87472's only in comments): `npm run check` exits 0, the node suite 1686 of 1688 passed with 2 skipped and every shell suite passed; `pre-bash-safety.test.sh` passes 1224 of 1224, also with SIGPIPE ignored; the bash 3.2.57 runtime gate passes 44 of 44 suites; `npm run smoke` passes 2 of 2. PR CI on df87472: 7 of 7 jobs green (run 40033696419).

**Pre-tag review.** One round by a fresh reviewer (09bc647..df87472): 0 Critical, 0 High, 0 Medium, 5 Low; four were text, repaired in the tagged commit, and the fifth needed no change. A test comment gave 38 programs as the current count (30 now). A `hook-common.sh` comment pointed at a sentence of its header that 897b67f had rewritten. This entry said 40,000 inner newlines take about 2.9 s, close to the timeout; they take 3.1 s and 9.2 s, as corrected above and in E3. A run of thousands of spaces, tabs or CRs in one segment costs time with the square of its length: `rm -rf $X/y` followed by 10,000 spaces takes 9 to 10 s on 0.116.2 and on this release, under bash 5.3.9 and 3.2.57, and about 6 KB passes the timeout. E3 did not list that cause and now does, as (d), unfixed; the longest such run in the 106,433 real commands is 83. The fifth: the seven corpus rows also pass on 0.116.2's hook, since they pin its verdicts rather than change them (the mutations above are what tell them apart), which this entry does not contradict. Behaviour was not falsified: `unwrap_indirect` taken from both versions gave the same output on 6,667 real commands that carry its anchors, 1,500 random inputs and 13 constructed ones, and `hook_read_bash_fields` gave what `$(…)` gives on 71 cases under bash 5.3.9 and 3.2.57.

## [0.116.2] - 2026-10-23

**The §8 gate splits a command into segments once per run instead of three times: 8 fewer `sed` runs each time it checks a command that is not read-only, with the same verdicts, stdout and exit code; spec unchanged (v8.3.0).** `pre-bash-safety-check.sh` runs three checks over a command's segments, for `rm`, for package runners (npx and the like) and for remote runners (deno, pip, cargo, go, nix), and each of them called `s8_split_segments`, which starts four `sed`s, on `SANITIZED_CMD`, which does not change after the escape tokens are stripped. The rm check now keeps its split as `CMD_SEGMENTS` and the other two read it (1ea7aa4). The remote-runner loop used to read the split straight from a process substitution, with any trailing empty lines; it now reads `CMD_SEGMENTS` through `printf '%s\n'`, as the other two loops do; it already skipped empty segments, so it reads the same segments in the same order. A command with a quote inside a `#` comment runs the gate a second time for the comment pass, and each run saves its 8.

**Programs started**, counted with a logging shim on PATH in front of 35 common tools (bash 5.3.9, this machine, rule-hits logging off, an empty `HOME`, a one-commit repository as the working directory): the gate alone 46 → 38 for `ls /tmp >/dev/null` and for `git commit -m '…'`, 53 → 45 for `rm -rf "${D:?}/x"`; the four PreToolUse:Bash hooks that share the read-only fast path (six hooks match `Bash`) 63 → 55, 96 → 88 and 70 → 62. A read-only command (`git log --oneline -1`) starts 4 in those four before and after, one `jq` each. The totals move with the setup: the pre-tag reviewer's (44 tools shimmed) gave 108 → 100 for the four hooks on `git commit -m '…'`, and a written rule-hits row adds programs; the difference of 8 held in every setup measured. Bash's own forks for `$(…)`, `<(…)` and pipeline elements are not in these counts; the reviewer counted 116 → 105 process IDs used per run of the gate on `ls /tmp >/dev/null`. Time was not measured.

**Replay.** Every unique Bash command in this machine's transcripts, through 0.116.1's hooks (`git archive d0d2a80 hooks`) and this release's: all 106,433 under bash 5.3.9, each allowed or denied as before (102,130 and 4,303), with the same stdout bytes and exit code; under bash 3.2.57, the 27,620 that name `rm`, a package runner or a remote runner, the same way (23,318 allowed, 4,302 denied, the same stdout bytes and exit code). Stderr was not compared. With SIGPIPE ignored, a remote-runner match in an early segment of a command over 64 KiB now leaves `printf: Broken pipe` on stderr where `sed` left `couldn't write … Broken pipe`; stderr from a hook that exits 0 reaches the person, not the model.

**Tests.**
- `tests/hooks/pre-bash-safety.test.sh`: a `sed` first on PATH counts the calls given `s8_split_segments`' expression as a whole argument (the curl-sh check's own split, which keeps `|`, has a longer expression and is not counted). An allowed command and a denied one are each split once; on 0.116.1's hook both rows fail with 3 splits, and a count of 0 fails as an unreached shim.
- On the tagged commit (its code differs from 1ea7aa4's only in comments): `npm run check` exits 0, the node suite 1686 of 1688 passed with 2 skipped and every shell suite passed; `pre-bash-safety.test.sh` passes 1214 of 1214, also with SIGPIPE ignored; the bash 3.2.57 runtime gate passes 44 of 44 suites; `npm run smoke` passes 2 of 2. PR CI on f5d3842 (the commit after it changes only comments and text): 7 of 7 jobs green (run 40004731389).

**Pre-tag review.** One round by a fresh reviewer (d0d2a80..f5d3842): 0 Critical, 0 High, 0 Medium, 7 Low, all text, repaired in the tagged commit. The counts are of programs started, not of processes; the setup behind them was not stated; they cover four of the six hooks that match `Bash`; one hook comment said the split runs once per command, though the comment pass is a second run; a test comment described the old code in the present tense; "it skips empty segments" read as new; and "the same output" covered stderr, which differs as the Replay paragraph says. Behaviour was not falsified: 548 constructed commands under bash 5.3.9 and 3.2.57, 17 that take the comment pass, and 18 of 70 to 140 KB, compared on stdout, exit code and rule-hits rows against 0.116.1's hooks, differed in none. One note on a family registered in `docs/S8-RESIDUALS.md` (E3, timeout), unchanged since 0.116.1: a constructed command of tens of thousands of lines still runs the gate past its 3 s hook timeout.

## [0.116.1] - 2026-10-21

**A repository with grafts gets no landed verdict; spec unchanged (v8.3.0).** In a repository with grafts (`.git/info/grafts`, or the file `GIT_GRAFT_FILE` names), a graft on the default branch's side that made a branch commit one of its ancestors took that commit out of 0.116.0's comparison, though no tree on the default branch held its change: with the branch's other commits copied there, the branch read as landed and branch-prune printed its `git branch -D` command. 0.116.0 listed this under "Not changed". Every git command follows grafts, and `--no-replace-objects` does not turn them off, so such a repository is no longer judged: each branch `-d` refused is kept, as in 0.115.0, and named with the reason. Grafts are deprecated in git: reading a graft file prints a hint that support "will be removed in a future Git version" and to run `git replace --convert-graft-file`, and the replace refs that writes are already not followed here. None of the 15 repositories on the maintainer's machine has a graft file.

**Fixed** (68129eb, 75085c2)
- `housekeeping refused` and `branches`: when `git rev-parse --path-format=absolute --git-path info/grafts` names a path that exists (from a linked worktree too, and the file `GIT_GRAFT_FILE` names), every branch to be judged is kept with reason `grafts` (`no-default-branch` when origin/HEAD names a branch with no local copy, which is checked first). When git gives no absolute path there, every branch is kept as `compare-failed`, a tip already on the default branch included, which 0.116.0 judged `on-main`: a git before 2.31 does not know `--path-format` and prints it back as its first line, before the path, exit 0 (checked on a built git 2.30.9, where a branch whose tip a graft put under the default branch now reads as not compared instead of landed), and an empty `GIT_GRAFT_FILE` makes `rev-parse` fail.
- `branch-prune` names the reason: "the repository has grafts (info/grafts or GIT_GRAFT_FILE), which give commits other parents".

**Not changed.** The `git branch -d` commands in the merged-branch list: the hook lists a branch whose tip is on the default branch as git's own history reads it, grafts included, and `-d` decides by the same reading when it runs, as since 0.93.0.

**Correction to 0.116.0's notes.** Its Tests line on "the 29 deleted branches, each recreated in a clone at the default branch of its deletion time" describes runs whose clones (`git clone` of each local repository) took the default branch from `origin/HEAD`, and `git clone` points that at whatever branch the source checkout has: in the three runs whose output was kept (18:46Z, 20:29Z and 20:58Z on 10-21), the claudemd rows were judged against `release/v0.116.0` and the gsd-lite rows against `fix/0.17.2`, the branches those checkouts had, not against the default branch at the deletion time, and the daagu rows against `master`, which it had throughout. The runs on 2d8faf8, 3ecbd0e and 066b64b kept no output; the claudemd checkout had `release/v0.116.0` from 13:52Z to 22:18Z. Re-run with `origin/HEAD` pinned to that branch: the same verdict on each of the 29, 27 landed and 2 kept, 32 to 265 ms per branch in 3 runs on 0.116.0's code (load 0.7, 12 cores) and 40 to 267 ms on 68129eb (load 2.4). The 27 of 29 in 0.116.0's "Why" paragraph came from a script that set the default branch itself and stands.

**Tests.**
- `tests/scripts/housekeeping.test.js`: a graft on the default branch's side (through `refused` with `GIT_GRAFT_FILE` and with `.git/info/grafts`, and through `branches`; control: without the graft the branch is kept with that commit named), and a git that prints `--path-format` back, as a git before 2.31 does (a tip on main, `-d` refused from elsewhere, a graft file present; the first mock exited 129, which no real git does, and passed while git 2.30.9 still read the branch as landed); the round-3 case of a graft that hides a branch commit now expects `grafts`. Against 0.116.0's code all three fail; removing the exists check, the absolute-path check, the null arm, or the second reason each fails at least one.
- `tests/hooks/branch-prune.test.sh` case 11o: a cherry-picked branch in a repository with a graft file is kept with the reason; against 0.116.0's code it is landed, and with the script fixed but not the hook it reads as not compared.
- `tests/hooks/session-summary.test.sh` Case 15 (69fa66c): jq's `strftime` gives UTC digits and `touch -t` reads local time, so from UTC−2 westward the ref "an hour old" landed in the future and no summary was written (`'24h 1' / ''` under America/Los_Angeles, from 0.112.0 on); it now runs `touch` under `TZ=UTC0` and passes under America/Los_Angeles, UTC, Pacific/Pago_Pago, Pacific/Kiritimati and Asia/Kolkata. The suite no longer needs `TZ=UTC` on this machine.
- On the tagged commit (its code differs from 75085c2's only in a comment): `npm run check` exits 0 in this machine's time zone (America/Los_Angeles, no `TZ=UTC`), the node suite 1686 of 1688 passed with 2 skipped (one is the macOS case) and every shell suite passed; the bash 3.2.57 runtime gate passes 44 of 44 suites; `branch-prune` and `session-summary` pass with SIGPIPE ignored; `npm run smoke` passes 2 of 2. PR CI on abd8a33 (the commits after it change only text and that comment): 7 of 7 jobs green (run 39733938558).

**Pre-tag review.** Two rounds by fresh reviewers. Round 1 (f2efa58..7dda689): 0 Critical, 1 High, 0 Medium, 5 Low. The High: a git before 2.31 does not fail on `rev-parse --path-format` but prints it back, exit 0, so the branch for a git that cannot name the graft file never ran there, and on a built git 2.30.9 a repository with grafts still got `on-main` and the `-D` command; the test's mock exited 129, which no real git does (75085c2). The five Lows were text (04ceea6). Round 2, on the repair (7dda689..abd8a33): 0 Critical, 0 High, 0 Medium, 7 Low, all text; six are repaired in the tagged commit, and one is left as found: with `GIT_GRAFT_FILE=/dev/null` the kept line says the repository has grafts, though git reads none from an empty file (the branch is kept either way).

## [0.116.0] - 2026-10-21

**Upgrade notes — a branch `git branch -d` refuses, whose work is on the default branch byte for byte, is now deleted through a checked `-D` command; spec v8.3.0.** Until now a session kept every branch `-d` refused and named it to the user. A rebase merge, a cherry-pick or a squash lands a branch's work as new commits, so `-d` refuses such a branch forever and they piled up. The branch-prune hook now judges these: when the branch's work is on the default branch byte for byte after the fork point — a copy of each commit (`git patch-id --verbatim`) or one squash commit equal to its whole change, at the same place, which a three-way merge confirms — it prints a `git branch -D` command that first checks the branch tip is still the commit it judged, and spec v8.3.0 core §7 lets a session run `-D` only through that command. A branch with any change that is not there is kept and named to the user as before, now with those commits listed. The judgement needs git 2.40 or later (`git merge-tree --merge-base`); with an older git, or in a partial clone, no branch is judged landed unless its tip is already on the default branch, and the others are kept as in 0.115.0. To keep 0.115.0's behaviour, the user can set `DISABLE_BRANCH_PRUNE_HOOK=1`: with no command printed, the spec keeps the branch (this also turns off the merged-branch list the hook has given since 0.93.0). To go back to 0.115.0 (spec v8.2.0), follow `docs/ROLLBACK.md`. A deleted branch is printed as `Deleted branch B (was SHA)`; `git branch B SHA` brings it back while git still has the commits (`git gc` prunes unreachable objects older than two weeks by default, `gc.pruneExpire`).

**Why.** In one week (2026-10-15 to 10-21, three of the maintainer's repositories) 29 local branches whose tips were not on the default branch, so that `-d` refuses them, were deleted with `-D` after the decision went to the user; 27 of them had every commit on the default branch byte for byte, and the other 2 each had one commit whose content there differs. The gsd-lite session that proposed `-D` judged with `git cherry`, whose patch-id strips whitespace: it calls a Python line moved into an `if` equal to a commit that only added trailing spaces. Design record `tasks/specs/landed-branch-cleanup.md`.

**Landed branches** (3215238, 03a962c, d4fbde7, 11282bf, 6af64e0, d96b668, a44f708, ef5b288, 46f63c4, 2d8faf8, 3ecbd0e, 1da7aa0, 1c369da, 7c51d7e, d04c3b2, 4569fc2, 066b64b)
- `housekeeping branches` lists a gone or `worktree-agent-*` branch whose tip is not on the default branch under `landed` when each of its commits has its own commit on the default branch (one commit there stands for one of the branch's), after the fork point, with the same `git patch-id --verbatim` (binary files compared by blob id, no rename pairing, submodule pointers always shown, one fixed set of diff options on both sides, run from the top level), or its whole change equals one such commit and its tip is not back at the tree of one of its earlier commits (a branch that took something out and put it back has an earlier commit's change as its whole change, and a copy of that commit would read as its squash); and that commit is the same change at the same place: `git merge-tree --merge-base=<the change's base> <that commit> <the branch commit>` is clean and leaves that commit's tree as it is. patch-id ignores line numbers, so without this the same edit to another identical block would match. The merge runs in a throwaway bare repository that reads the repository's objects as an alternate, with no `GIT_*` variable, no user, system or repository config and no attributes: no merge driver runs, and `merge=ours` or `merge=union` cannot decide the result. History is walked and patch ids are read without replace refs (`--no-replace-objects`): a replace ref that gave the branch tip main's copy as its content and the fork point as its parent hid the commit before it, and the branch read as landed. Each commit's change is checked from the parent the walk gave it, grafts (`.git/info/grafts`, `GIT_GRAFT_FILE`) included: the throwaway repository has no grafts, and a commit grafted onto the fork point had only its own change checked. When merge-tree fails (a git before 2.40, a missing object) or no temp directory can be made for that repository, the branch is kept as `compare-failed` and the run goes on. A branch with a merge commit, one that forked more than 500 commits back, a partial clone (judging would fetch from the remote; a promisor remote set to any true value) and any branch on a git before 2.40 are never landed. A landing reverted on the default branch still counts (the commit stays in its history); a match from before the fork point does not (re-adding what the default branch once removed is new work). At most 20 such branches are judged per run (`unjudged` counts the rest). merge-tree's result objects go to that repository (`claudemd-landed.XXXXXX` in the temp directory), removed after the run; one a timeout killed is reaped by the next run after 10 minutes and by `/claudemd-clean-residue`. No object or file is added to the repository, though git may refresh the timestamp of an object it already has.
- `housekeeping refused` (new) reads a shell command on stdin and judges each branch named in its `git [-C DIR] branch -d` / `--delete` segments that is still there and is not the default branch, a worktree checkout or a symbolic ref, at most 20: `landed` (also `on-main`, when the tip is on the default branch and `-d` compared it with something else) or `kept`, with up to 3 commits that have no copy and their count, or the reason it was not compared (among them `shallow`, when a shallow clone holds no commit the branch shares with the default branch, because it stops before the fork point or the branch is unrelated, and `no-default-branch`, when origin/HEAD names a branch with no local copy). A segment that forces, names remote branches, sets a `GIT_*` variable or passes a git option other than `-C`, `-c`, `--no-pager`, `-P`, `--no-optional-locks` and `--no-replace-objects`, a `-d` after a `cd` (also `command cd`, `builtin cd`) without an absolute `-C`, comments, redirections and a name the shell would expand are skipped.
- `branch-prune` adds the landed branches to the list it gives after a merge, pull, fetch or push, each named with what its work is on the default branch as (an identical copy of each of its N commits, one squash commit it names, or its own tip) and given `[ "$(git -C R rev-parse -q --verify refs/heads/B)" = SHA ] && git -C R branch -D -- B`, quoted by jq's `@sh`: `-D` keeps git's refusal for a branch checked out in a worktree (and, checked on git 2.53, one being rebased there), the hook judges nothing while a rebase or bisect is in progress, and a branch that moved since the verdict is left alone. After any command with a `git branch -d` segment, also one that exits 0 (`… || true`), it gives a verdict on each branch the command left: the landed ones with that command, the kept ones with their commits and the reason, to be named under open items. It is now also registered on `PostToolUseFailure`, because a refused `-d` exits 1; a failed merge or pull gets no list. New rule-hits event `branch-prune-refused-advisory` (`{landed, kept}`); `branch-prune-advisory` gains `landed`; `/claudemd-status --verbose` names a hook's other events (`alsoOn`), and the install manifest has 28 entries for the 27 hooks. The hook deletes nothing.
- Spec v8.3.0 core §7 Git: "if `-d` refuses, run the `-D` the branch-prune hook printed for that branch (its work is on the main line byte for byte) exactly as printed; with none, keep the branch and name it under open items, never a `-D` of your own." Was: "… keep the branch and name it under open items, never `-D`."

**Not changed, and why.**
- A branch with no upstream that is not `worktree-agent-*` is still never in the list (a backup made before a rebase looks merged); it is judged only when a `git branch -d` names it.
- No GitHub lookup: a merged pull request whose head equals the branch tip would also prove a landing, but needs the network and a login, and on the 29 branches it found 11 that patch-ids found too. `git merge-tree` against the current default branch (does merging the branch change it?) found 10 of the 29: later edits to the same lines read as conflicts. Here it runs against the matching commit, where later edits do not count.
- A branch with a commit changed on the way in stays the user's decision; the line lists that commit.
- A squash-merged branch whose tip is back at the tree of one of its earlier commits (a debug line added, then removed) is kept: its whole change equals that earlier commit's, and a squash of it cannot be told from a copy of that commit. The line lists the commits that have no copy. A branch whose commits undo each other without bringing the tip back to an earlier tree (`+A`, `+B`, `-A`) is judged by its whole change: a copy of its `+B` commit makes it landed as a squash, since everything its tip holds is on the default branch, and its `+A` and `-A` commits go with it.
- The list path judges on every merge, pull, fetch or push, before the once-per-session check, so a repository with many cherry-picked `worktree-agent-*` branches pays for it each time. In a clone of this repository (533 files) with 20 such branches of 10 commits each, all 200 commits picked onto main, the hook took 1.29 to 1.69 s per push in 10 runs on 7c51d7e, against 0.16 to 0.20 s for 0.115.0 (one machine, 12 cores, load 5 to 11; an earlier batch of 5 took 1.52 to 5.06 s, against 0.42 to 0.58 s). A judgement past the hook's budget (8 s, 4 s each when the command also has a `-d`) is stopped and prints nothing. Caching verdicts is left for later.
- A graft (`.git/info/grafts`) on the default branch's side that makes a branch commit an ancestor of the default branch puts that commit in its history, as `git log` and `git branch -d` read it, even when no tree there holds the commit's change: the branch can be landed with that commit counted as on the default branch, and the commit stays reachable only while the graft does. A replace ref that does the same (`git replace --graft`) is not followed. Grafts are deprecated in git.
- A backtick in a branch name ends the Markdown code span around the printed command; the command stays inert (checked under bash, bash 3.2.57 and dash).

**Tests.**
- `tests/scripts/housekeeping.test.js`: 40 new cases, and one replaced. Before the change the file did not load (the new exports did not exist). `branches`: a rebase merge, a two-commit squash, a commit changed in the pick, a cherry-picked `worktree-agent-*` branch, a merge commit on the branch, a match from before the fork point, the same edit on another identical block, an edit main made to the other block after changing this one, a hidden submodule pointer (diff.submodule=log, `ignore = all`), a partial clone (nothing fetched), the throwaway directory removed and a stale one reaped, a landed branch under a hostile git config judged from a subdirectory, a reverted landing, a mode change, a binary file, more than 20 candidates, more than 500 commits after the fork point, a commit that adds back what the branch removed after the first two landed (with its control: all three landed), merge attributes from `.git/info/attributes`, the user's global `core.attributesFile`, `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/git/attributes` and a `.gitattributes`, a merge driver that must not run, a cherry-picked submodule pointer move under diff settings that hide it, a promisor remote written as `yes`, no temp directory (the merged branch is still listed) and a `GIT_ATTR_SOURCE` that must not reach the merge. `refused`: the segment parser (comments, redirections, `GIT_*`, `cd`, `command cd`, `builtin cd`), a cherry-picked branch, a git without `merge-tree --merge-base`, on macOS the system's own git (`/usr/bin/git`: landed from 2.40, `compare-failed` on 2.39; it prints its version, Apple Git 2.50.1 on the CI runners; skipped elsewhere), a changed one with its commit named, a replace ref that hides a branch commit (also through `branches`), a replace ref that dresses a commit up as main's larger copy, a graft that hides a branch commit (`.git/info/grafts` and `GIT_GRAFT_FILE`, also through `branches`), a branch on main that `-d` compared with HEAD elsewhere, a shallow clone that stops before the fork point and a default branch with no local copy (each kept with that reason, with a full-clone control), the names it leaves out, a revision expression, `-C`, at most 20 names, a rebase in progress and the CLI. The case asserting that a plain squash merge stays in `goneUnmerged` was replaced by one whose squash commit adds a file, which does; a plain squash is now `landed`. Of 23 mutations, 22 fail at least one case: no fork-point filter, no squash probe, no merge-commit check, either cap, no default-branch or branch-name check, no same-place check, no partial-clone check, no reaping, no `--no-ext-diff`, no top-level run, no 20-name limit in `refused`, and each of the parser's nine guards (long options, forcing short options, a dynamic `-C`, dynamic names, other git options, `cd`, comments, redirections, `GIT_*`). The 23rd, a whitespace-stripping patch-id, fails none: the same-place check then rejects the whitespace-only match by itself. Of 10 more for the round-2 repair, 9 fail a case: no one-copy-per-commit rule, no earlier-tree rule, merge-tree run in the repository, no `core.attributesFile=/dev/null`, a merge-tree error read as a conflict, the promisor value read as text, no `command`/`builtin`, and no `--ignore-submodules=none` or `--submodule=short`. The 10th, no `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL=/dev/null`, fails none: `core.attributesFile=/dev/null` already shuts out the user's attributes, and no global setting tried without them (`merge.renormalize` with `core.autocrlf`) changed a verdict. The three replace-ref and graft cases failed before their fixes (the branch read as landed, per-commit); with the check's parent named `${c}^` again the graft case fails, and with patch ids read through replace refs the dressed-up case fails. The no-temp-directory case and the shallow and default-branch case failed before 066b64b (a thrown `ENOENT`; `unrelated` for both); removing the default-branch check or the shallow check alone fails the latter, and with the `GIT_*` strip removed the `GIT_ATTR_SOURCE` case reads a branch whose change is not on main as landed. By hand on 7c51d7e: with Debian bookworm's git 2.39.5 (a real one, not the wrapper) a cherry-picked branch is kept as `compare-failed`, one on main is landed `on-main`, and no `claudemd-landed.*` directory is left; in shallow clones (depth 1, 2 and 4, and a landed branch at 1, 2, 3 and 5) a branch whose fork point lies past the shallow boundary is kept (its reason read "shares no history" there; from 066b64b it is `shallow`), and the landed one is landed at depth 5.
- `tests/hooks/branch-prune.test.sh` cases 11 to 13: 21 new checks — the verdict after a refused `-d` (PostToolUseFailure) and after `-d … || true` (PostToolUse); the `-D` command deletes exactly the landed branch; a branch with a new commit since the verdict is left alone; silence after a `-d` that deleted everything; the kill switch; no list after a failed pull; four commands that are not a refused `-d`; hostile branch names and repository path; more than 3 missing commits; a squash named as one; a partial clone kept with its reason; a git without `merge-tree --merge-base` read as not compared; a shallow clone that stops before the fork point and a default branch with no local copy, each named as the reason; a rebase-merged gone branch in the list; with no temp directory, the merged branch still listed; the `-D` command passes this plugin's own Bash gate. Cases 11 and 12 match the landed sentence whole; the round-2 text, which named `git patch-id --verbatim` before every landed branch, a tip already on the default branch included, fails both. Against 0.115.0's code 14 of the 21 fail; of the 7 that pass, 6 assert silence (11d2, 11e, 11g ×4), with case 11 as their live counterpart, and 12b asserts the list 0.115.0 gives with no temp directory, which this release's code lost until 066b64b. 11l's new text, 11m, 11n and 12b failed before 066b64b. Five mutations of the hook (a list after a failed command, no SHA check, a fixed event name, a verdict on failures only, an unquoted name) each fail at least one case. The suite passes under bash 3.2.57 and with SIGPIPE ignored.
- `tests/scripts/hook-registry.test.js` checks every registration, `alsoOn` included, and requires branch-prune on PostToolUseFailure and 28 registrations (it failed before the registry change). `tests/scripts/install.test.js` and `tests/integration/full-lifecycle.test.sh` compare manifest entries with `HOOK_REGISTRATION_COUNT`; `status.test.js` checks `alsoOn`; `clean-residue.test.js` checks the `claudemd-landed.*` pattern against the mkdtemp template; `tests/hooks/contract.test.sh` and `audit-r11-smallfixes.test.js` R15 cover the new event. Each failed before its change.
- `tests/scripts/spec-structure.test.js`: the whole §7 Git line is pinned with the new sentence; the section, preamble, heading-inventory and Recent-changes hashes were updated after reading the diff.
- The shipped `refused` on the 29 deleted branches, each recreated in a clone at the default branch of its deletion time: 27 landed, 2 kept (each with one commit whose content on the default branch differs), 85 to 642 ms per branch on 066b64b at load 4, with every verdict as on d04c3b2 (111 to 566 ms on 7c51d7e at load 15, 46 to 513 ms on 3ecbd0e, 62 to 426 ms on 2d8faf8).
- On the tagged commit (code as in 066b64b; the commits after it change text): `TZ=UTC npm run check` exits 0, the node suite 1684 of 1686 passed with 2 skipped (one is the macOS case) and every shell suite passed, also with SIGPIPE ignored; under `TZ=UTC` the bash 3.2.57 runtime gate passes 44 of 44 suites; `npm run smoke` passes 2 of 2. PR CI on 2cb1da2 (the commits after it change only text: this entry, README, `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`, `docs/RULE-HITS-SCHEMA.md`, the design record, `housekeeping --help` and a hook comment): 7 of 7 jobs green (run 39678344792). Without `TZ=UTC` this machine (UTC−7) fails `session-summary` Case 15, in the bash 3.2.57 gate too, and on 0.115.0 as well: the case builds a `touch -t` local time from a UTC string. Not changed here.

**Offline comparison.** `claude -p` in sandboxes (Opus 5.5, effort high, 5 runs per arm and task; each arm's hooks and spec from a frozen `git archive`): a cherry-pick of two branches, one landing byte for byte and one changed in a conflict, and a squash merge of a two-commit branch. 0.115.0 deleted the landed branch in 0 of 10 runs; this release (7c51d7e, the spec as tagged; the tagged hook and script differ from it only in the verdicts under grafts and replace refs, in the reason given for a branch that was not compared, and in going on without a temp directory, where 7c51d7e printed nothing; on both tasks' end states, rebuilt by a script, the tagged hook's output is byte for byte 7c51d7e's) in 10 of 10, and 2d8faf8, whose landed line still named `git patch-id --verbatim`, in 10 of 10, each time by running the printed command as printed. No run in any arm ran a `-D` of its own, and in every cherry-pick run the changed branch was kept and named to the user. Two earlier builds were measured as well: one that described a squash as copies of its commits got the branch deleted in 1 of 5 squash runs (the other 4 sessions checked that claim, found it false and asked the user), and 11282bf, before the same-place check, in 10 of 10. With `DISABLE_BRANCH_PRUNE_HOOK=1` (2 runs, on 11282bf) the landed branch was kept and no `-D` ran.

**Pre-tag review.** Four rounds by fresh reviewers. The runbook stops at two; rounds 3 and 4 ran by the maintainer's decision, since each of rounds 1 to 3 found a branch wrongly judged landed. Round 1, two reviewers on 4c7dc67..28638fc (code; published text): 0 Critical, 2 High, 5 Medium (one of them found by both), 18 Low. The Highs: the same-place check applied the change with `git apply`, which moves a hunk to wherever its context matches, and a submodule pointer that diff settings hid was in neither patch id; the Mediums: comments, redirections and `GIT_DIR` read as branch names, a temp directory a timeout left, a partial clone that fetched from its remote, and a squash described as copies of its commits (ef5b288, 46f63c4, 2d8faf8, 069383b). Round 2 (28638fc..9008c6d): 0 Critical, 1 High, 3 Medium, 5 Low. The High: a branch that removes a line and adds it back had both adds matched to one copy; the Mediums: merge attributes and a merge driver decided the merge, and the driver ran; a merge-tree error read as missing commits (3ecbd0e, 9e85bfa). The author then found the replace-ref hole (1da7aa0). Round 3 (9008c6d..d453478): 0 Critical, 1 High, 1 Medium, 5 Low. The High: a commit grafted onto the fork point had only its own change checked, because the throwaway repository has no grafts (d04c3b2); the Medium was the squash sentence under Not changed (3fa16c8); two Lows became 4569fc2 and 066b64b. Round 4 (d453478..2cb1da2): 0 Critical, 0 High, 0 Medium, 5 Low: a graft on the default branch's side (left as found, under Not changed) and four text repairs (3156bb9, 494852d).

## [0.115.0] - 2026-10-21

**Upgrade notes — the §8 gate now denies `rm -rf ~/*` and `rm -rf "$HOME"/*`; nine fixes and a README example; spec unchanged (v8.2.0).** A delete whose target is `~`, `$HOME`, `$TMPDIR`, `$PWD` or `$OLDPWD` followed only by `*` and `/` (`rm -rf ~/*`, `rm -rf "$TMPDIR"/*`, `find ~/* -delete`) is denied, as bare `rm -rf ~` and `rm -rf "$HOME"` already were. To run one on purpose, write the subpath literally (`rm -rf ~/build/*` passes), or the user can allow that one command with `[allow-rm-rf-var]`. To go back to 0.114.0, follow `docs/ROLLBACK.md`; 0.114.0's doctor does not know the two new could-not-evaluate reasons below (`gh-timeout`, `gh-failed`) and counts them as a missing prerequisite, so after going back `hook-fail-open` can be red until those rows leave its 30-day window or are acknowledged with `--ack-fail-open`. Other changes users can see: `housekeeping tmp --apply` exits 3 when it could not delete a target, `/claudemd-status` names an unparseable `settings.json`, install says where an overwritten spec was copied, and the `claudemd-cli` CLI and the scripts that use the shared argument parser accept `--` as the end of options.

**§8: a subpath made only of `*` and `/` bounds nothing** (a304dbd, db827e7)
- The whitelist arm treated a target as bounded when anything but `/` was left after removing the variable, and `*` counted. So `rm -rf $HOME/*` passed while bare `$HOME` was denied, although the glob deletes everything under `$HOME`, and with `HOME` empty it is `/*`: the case the arm's own comment cites, which `rm`'s `--preserve-root` does not catch (it guards `/`, not `/*`). `~/*` passed the same way through the `~` arm. `~` expands to an empty string when `HOME` is empty, too.
- Deny-only by construction: the bare test, and the `${HOME:?}` fold that must agree with it, now treat `*` like `/`, and a target that newly meets the test takes the existing deny. `~*` is unchanged: bash leaves an unknown `~user` prefix literal, so it is a glob in the current directory. A find with a selection primary (`find ~/* -name '*.tmp' -delete`) still passes, as it does for bare `$HOME`. The rm deny says that what follows the variable names no subpath of it and asks for the subpath written out; a find keeps the bare-variable message, which asks for a selection primary.
- Measured before the change: of 105,658 distinct Bash commands in this maintainer's transcripts, 2 had such a target, both text about the gate itself. The 21,791 that mention `rm`, `find`, `unlink` or `shred`, replayed through 0.114.0's gate and this one, gave 0 verdict changes and 0 output differences; the same harness on the two shapes above gives allow → deny.

**Fixes**
- `housekeeping tmp --apply` exited 0 when a deletion failed (the failure appeared only in `errors`). It now exits 3, the convention `clean-residue --apply` already uses; USAGE and CONTRIBUTING.md list it. The tmp-sweep hook runs it in the background and reads no exit code. (ea43b42)
- `housekeeping branches` listed as deletable a branch merged only into `origin/<default>` while the local default branch was behind, and `git branch -d` then refused it. A listed branch is now one `git branch -d` deletes from the checkout: on the local default branch and merged into what `-d` compares against (the branch's upstream if it resolves, else HEAD). One merged only on the remote is listed after the pull that brings it into the local default branch. The branch-prune hint no longer says "(or origin/main)". (d1a9c5f)
- `/claudemd-status` read an unparseable `settings.json` as no settings at all. The output now carries `settings: {settingsError: "unparseable", settingsPath, detail}`, and the command page tells the model to relay it. The detail does not quote the file, whose `env` block can hold tokens. (0fc4ad3)
- Install on a `~/.claude` it cannot write printed only Node's error. For `EACCES`/`EPERM` it now names the nearest existing directory that is not writable and the `chmod` that fixes it, or, when another user owns that directory (a `~/.claude` made by `sudo claude`), says that its owner or root has to change it and offers no command; exit 1 as before. (596842a, 6e010b5)
- Install that replaced installed spec files differing from the shipped ones reported `backupDir: null` and printed nothing, though it had copied them to `spec-backup-<stamp>/`. The result now carries `specBackupDir`, and one stderr line names the directory and any previous file it could not copy (whose earlier text is then not kept, as before). `backupDir` keeps its meaning. (3d4ac33, 6e010b5)
- `ship-baseline-check` let a push through without a trace when `gh run list` timed out (2 s budget) or failed. For a repo with a github.com remote it now records a fail-open row, `gh-timeout` or `gh-failed`; other remotes, GitHub Enterprise hosts and ssh host aliases included, stay silent as before. Doctor counts both as could-not-evaluate, not as a missing prerequisite, so one network failure does not turn it red, and its text says what each reason means. A missing `gh` stays the advisory `gh` row it already was. Measured here, two runs of 8 calls: 13 of 16 took 1.46-1.68 s, one took 2.84 s and two failed after 10 s. (582002e, 4d0519a)
- The session-start ledger banner chose the newest `*-ledger.md` with `ls -t | head -n 1`, so a ledger whose name holds a newline hid the banner. Both session-start and ledger-staleness now pick it with one shared glob-and-mtime function (`hook_newest_ledger`), so they name the same file also for a newline in a name or two ledgers saved in the same second; mtimes are compared in whole seconds and a tie keeps the first name in glob order (0.114.0's `ls -t` compared nanoseconds, so two ledgers saved within one second now go by name); C0 control characters and DEL in the path either hook shows print as `?`. Where `platform_stat_mtime` is unavailable, neither shows a ledger any more: there is no order to pick by and no age bound. Each ledger costs one `platform_stat_mtime` call: 100 ledgers took 355 ms here, against the hooks' 5 s timeout. (e6a463a, bffe16c, 5c42bec)
- The MEMORY.md index parsers disagreed on which link names an entry's file when a line has two (`- [A](a.md) — supersedes [old](old.md)`): the memory gate's hint, doctor's tag scan and `spec-coherence-audit` took the last, `mem-audit` the first, and mem-audit missed a title ending in `]`. All take the first `](….md)` on a line that has one. A line with none is read the way the memory gate read it in 0.114.0, by its rightmost `(….md)` (doctor's tag scan now reads it the same way), except by mem-audit, which reads no file there, as before. So the gate matches the same lines and tags as in 0.114.0, and only the file named for a line with a `](….md)` link and another `(….md)` changes. On the 283 lines of this machine's eight MEMORY.md files nothing changes. (86d64af, 714944c)
- `--` ended no option list: `claudemd-cli lint -- --json` reported `unknown flag '--'`, so text starting with `--` could not be linted. In the shared parser and the CLI a bare `--` now ends the options: lint scans what follows as text and audit takes it as the path; scripts that take no operands accept a trailing `--` and refuse anything after it (`clean-residue -- --apply` exits 2); `toggle -- <hook>` and `doc-check -- <file>` take what follows as their operand; `<script> -- --help` exits 2 instead of printing help. Not every script: `perf-baseline.sh` and the subcommand of `housekeeping` and `statusline-adopt` still refuse a `--`. (e2337d9, 4912fb2)
- README listed GitHub Actions as a use with no example. It now has a workflow that lints each pull-request commit message with a pinned `claudemd-cli`, run against a fixture repository before publishing. (dd49991)

**Not changed, and why.**
- `rm -rf ~/.*` and `$HOME/.*` (every dotfile) still pass: the residue holds a literal `.`, which is a different judgement from "no subpath" (registered as F26). A brace expansion that leaves the bare home, `rm -rf ~/{a,}`, also passes (F25). Positional parameters before a glob (`"$1"*`) stay registered residual F12. Registered from the pre-tag review, none of them in the 105,658 real commands: `?` and bracket globs that match what `*` does (`rm -rf ~/?*`, F24, still allowed), `~+/*` (F22, still allowed), and a quoted star or `~/*`, which is a literal path but is read after quote removal (F20, denied; write the path without the variable to delete such a file).
- `/claudemd-status` still reads an unreadable `settings.json`, a non-object one, and an unparseable project-level `.claude/settings*.json` as no settings; uninstall still prints Node's raw error.

**Tests.**
- `tests/fixtures/bash-safety/corpus.tsv` (run by `tests/hooks/pre-bash-safety.test.sh`): two `pass` rows relabelled `deny`, twelve `S8-GLOB` deny rows and six controls. Before the hook change the fourteen deny rows failed and the controls passed. Twelve residual rows (four F24, three F25, two F26 and one F22 `xfn`; two F20 `xfp`) and one control (`find ~/? -delete`, a one-character name) pin today's verdicts, and four deny-reason rows pin the new texts (three failed on the first wording).
- `tests/scripts/housekeeping.test.js`: `tmp --apply` exits 3 while a target cannot be deleted and 0 once it can (failed `0 !== 3` before); three cases where `git branch -d` refuses a branch (a tip only on `origin/main`, a tip not on the checkout, a `worktree-agent-*` branch whose live upstream lacks its tip) no longer list it (each listed it before). The test that asserted the old listing was replaced. `tests/hooks/branch-prune.test.sh` case 10: no hint while the local default branch is behind.
- `tests/scripts/command-page-behaviour.test.js`: status names an unparseable `settings.json` and the page says to relay it (failed: `settingsError` undefined); the install page names `specBackupDir`. `tests/scripts/install-cli.test.js` (new): three unwritable layouts name the directory and the fix (each printed only Node's message before), a control keeps Node's text when the target is writable, and an overwritten edited spec reports `specBackupDir`. From the pre-tag review: a directory owned by another user gets no chmod advice (`/` as HOME; failed before), and a previous file the backup could not read is named (failed before).
- `tests/hooks/ship-baseline.test.sh` cases 53-62 with fake `gh` binaries (exit 1, an empty list, empty output, a slow single process) and github.com and other remotes: 57 of 62 passed before. `doctor.test.js` and `rule-hits-parse.test.js` check that both reasons are could-not-evaluate (4 cases failed before).
- `tests/hooks/session-start.test.sh` 41h-41k (41h and 41k failed before; 41i and 41j pin the tie rule and an ordinary banner's bytes) and `tests/hooks/ledger-staleness.test.sh` 10d-10h, which hand one `tasks/` directory to both hooks and require the same file (10d, 10f and 10h failed before); 10d also requires the path ledger-staleness prints to show the newline as `?` (failed before that change).
- `tests/fixtures/memory-index-links.json` (new) runs through `tests/scripts/memory-index-links.test.js` (new; 2 of 3 failed before), `memory-tags-parity.test.sh`, `mem-audit.test.sh` case 15, `memory-read-check.test.sh` case 72 and `memory-prompt-hint.test.sh` case 29, each of which failed before. From the pre-tag review: lines with no `](….md)` link give the same files and tags as the 0.114.0 rule (`memory-tags-parity.test.sh`, against that rule whole; failed without the fallback), and `indexEntryFile` names their rightmost `(….md)` (failed without it).
- `tests/scripts/argv.test.js` (3 cases) and `tests/scripts/lint-cli.test.js` (4 cases, one running the `--help` example) for `--`; all seven failed before. The README workflow was run against a fixture repository with `claudemd-cli@0.113.4`: exit 0 for clean messages and an empty range, 1 for a hit, 128 for an unknown base commit.
- On the tagged commit (code as in aa00336; the commit after it changes this entry only): `npm run check` exits 0, the node suite 1643 of 1644 passed with 1 skipped and every shell suite passed. PR CI run 39566119854 on aa00336 passed all 7 jobs. Earlier PR CI runs failed on three tests: the errno macOS reports for a directory `tmp --apply` cannot delete (run 39557203873, fixed in cd05471), the another-user install case on macOS's read-only `/` (run 39562053656, second attempt, fixed in aa00336), and `session-end-check` Case 33e under bash 3.2 (run 39562053656, first attempt), which passed with the same hook code on 244c2f8 and aa00336; its git call has a 0.5 s timeout, and the case is noted for a separate fix.

**Pre-tag review.** Round 1, two fresh reviewers on cd68796..244c2f8 (hooks; scripts, CLI and published text): 0 Critical, 0 High, 4 Medium, 20 Low. The Mediums: the memory gate's tag set shrank on lines with no `](….md)` link (fallback, 714944c); `?` and bracket globs bypass the new §8 rule (registered as F24, not patched); the rollback note did not say 0.114.0's doctor reads the new reasons as missing prerequisites; the install advice offered chmod for another user's directory (6e010b5). Round 2, a fresh reviewer on 244c2f8..cd05471: 0 Critical, 0 High, 0 Medium, 10 Low, all repaired as text, comments, residual rows and design record (9f5e7ef).

## [0.114.0] - 2026-10-21

**Upgrade notes — one new `/claudemd-doctor` flag and one hook fix; spec unchanged (v8.2.0). Plugin users have nothing to do.** `/claudemd-doctor --ack-fail-open` clears a `hook-fail-open` row once its cause is fixed. Until the flag has written an acknowledgement, doctor computes its checks, verdicts and exit codes from the log as 0.113.4 did; the red row's text now also says how to clear it. The hook fix can add rows 0.113.4 dropped: a fail-open rate-limit marker dated in the future (left by a clock that ran ahead) no longer holds rows back, so on a machine with such a marker and a cause still present, doctor can report a fail-open 0.113.4 did not. To go back to 0.113.4, follow `docs/ROLLBACK.md`; 0.113.4 does not read the acknowledgement file, so its row is red again until the window passes.

**Why.** A hook that skips enforcement because a prerequisite is missing (`jq`, a patterns file, a writable state directory) writes a `fail-open` row, and `/claudemd-doctor` reported `hook-fail-open` red, exiting 3, for the 30 days that row stays in its window, also after the user had fixed the cause (0.113.3 and 0.113.4 "Not changed"). Design record `tasks/specs/doctor-fail-open-ack.md`.

**`/claudemd-doctor --ack-fail-open`** (25d80cc, f1a89e6, 5adebf6, c412116, 6d6b0fa)
- With at least one unacknowledged prerequisite row in the window, it writes `fail-open-ack.json` in the state directory (`~/.claude/.claudemd-state/`, or where `CLAUDEMD_STATE_DIR` points), listing every prerequisite fail-open row in the window by its `ts`, `hook` and reason, and the same run reports the row green. `failOpenAck` gives `written` and `acknowledged`, the number of rows it newly covered.
- A row counts as acknowledged only when an entry matches all three parts. Entries are counted, so a second row identical to an acknowledged one (a hook that cannot write its throttle marker records every run) is not covered by it. No clock is compared, when writing or when reading: a clock that was ahead or was set back cannot make the file cover a row it did not list. Any other prerequisite row turns the check red as before, and the detail also counts the acknowledged ones. No per-reason recovery test is needed: a prerequisite that is still missing writes a new row the next time its hook records one.
- A file that does not parse, has no `acknowledged` array, or is not a regular file counts as none, and the flag then writes a new one. It is written to a temp file beside the path and renamed over it, so a symlink or FIFO there is replaced, not written through or blocked on. With nothing to acknowledge it writes nothing and returns `failOpenAck: {written: false, reason}`. When it cannot write (a directory at the path, a state directory that is not writable) it exits 1 naming the file and the error code, with no check list, no `--prune-backups` run and no temp file left; the page says what the common error codes mean.
- The command page tells the model to run it only after the user says the cause is fixed, never to turn the check green on its own, and what the transcript reasons can come from. `doctor --help`, README and `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` list the flag; `--help` now says doctor is read-only apart from its two flags and the one manifest relocation, and that exit 1 includes an acknowledgement it cannot write.
- `hook_record_failopen` (`hooks/lib/hook-common.sh`): a rate-limit marker dated after the current time, left by a clock that ran ahead, now counts as stale. It held every later fail-open row of that hook and reason back until the clock passed it, so after an acknowledgement a cause still broken stayed green that long.
- `clean-residue` reaps neither the file nor its temp: they match none of the state directory's ephemeral name patterns. `CLAUDEMD_PURGE=1` uninstall removes both with the rest of the state directory, also from one that `CLAUDEMD_STATE_DIR` moved.

**Not changed, and why.**
- Between an acknowledgement and the next row the affected hook records, doctor reports green even if the cause is not fixed. While a hook can write to `~/.claude/.claudemd-state/` (hooks do not read `CLAUDEMD_STATE_DIR`) it records at most one row a minute per hook and reason, so that row comes on its first run a minute or more after its last recorded one.
- The other two classes of fail-open row (an empty event from a manual run, a gate with nothing to evaluate) are as before; there is nothing to acknowledge in them.

**Tests.**
