- `tests/scripts/doctor.test.js`, thirteen cases: acknowledging turns the row green in the same run and writes each row's three parts; a row differing in any one part, or identical to an acknowledged one, is red, and the detail counts the acknowledged ones; an entry covers only a row equal in all three parts, also once its own row has left the log; rows dated a day ahead of the clock are acknowledged and stay so when the clock passes them, while a row recorded after the clock is put back is red; nothing to acknowledge writes nothing and leaves an existing file as it was; a second acknowledgement keeps the rows still in the window, drops the others and counts only the new; seven damaged or foreign shapes count as none and are replaced; a numeric `ts` is acknowledged like any other; the red row's new sentence and the advisory clause on the green row; a symlink at the path is not followed when reading or writing; a FIFO there blocks neither doctor nor the flag; a missing state directory is created; the CLI exits 1 with `EISDIR` for a directory at the path and `EACCES` for an unwritable state directory, leaving no temp file. 9 of the first twelve failed on the draft this release replaced, which acknowledged a single moment (the FIFO case by hanging until its 60 s timeout). Each of fifteen mutations fails at least one case: a set instead of counts, `stat` for `lstat`, no `mkdir`, a direct write instead of the rename, writing only the new rows, counting all rows, no temp cleanup, each of the three parts dropped from the comparison alone and dropped from the comparison and the file, no same-run green, and dropping the nothing-to-acknowledge guard.
- `tests/scripts/command-page-behaviour.test.js` gains a doctor case: it pins the new page paragraph and runs the flag twice in a sandbox HOME, checking the fields and the file the paragraph names. It fails on 0.113.4's page.
- `tests/hooks/fail-open.test.sh` T24 and T24b: with a rate-limit marker dated a day ahead, a fail-open is recorded and the marker reset to now, and the next call within a minute is throttled; a marker 30 s ahead is stale too. Both fail without the `hook-common.sh` change.
- `tests/scripts/uninstall.test.js`: the purge case also removes the file and its `.<pid>` temp from a moved state directory and keeps a user's `fail-open-ack.json.bak`.
- On the tagged commit (code as in aac4226; the commits after it change tests and text): `npm run check` exits 0, the node suite 1617 of 1618 passed with 1 skipped and every shell suite passed. PR CI run 39552977297 on aac4226 passed all 7 jobs (static, and the test job on ubuntu and macOS with node 20, 22 and 24).

**Pre-tag review.** Two rounds by fresh reviewers on this design, after two on a draft that acknowledged a single moment, which this release replaced. Round 1 (a66c6c1..7a2b6c8): 0 Critical, 0 High, 2 Medium, 8 Low. The Mediums were a fail-open rate-limit marker dated in the future that held rows back (fixed in `hook-common.sh`, c412116) and a three-part match the tests did not pin for `ts` and `hook` (case added, 6d6b0fa); the Lows were text. Round 2 (7a2b6c8..aac4226): 0 Critical, 0 High, 0 Medium, 6 Low, all text or tests (cb07cf8). Left as found: `docs/RULE-HITS-SCHEMA.md` lists `transcript-missing` and `event-fields-missing` as could-not-evaluate, which the code has not done since 0.76.2.

## [0.113.4] - 2026-10-20

**Upgrade notes — command pages and one module trigger: fixes only, spec unchanged (v8.2.0). Plugin users have nothing to do.** Six slash-command pages now describe what 0.113.3 made their commands do, so the model running them reads the current exit codes, fields and recovery steps. The `ship` module now also loads on 打个tag. No command, hook verdict or exit code changes. To go back to 0.113.3, follow `docs/ROLLBACK.md`.

**Why.** 0.113.3 changed six commands and listed their pages under "Not changed": the pages are model-visible text, which this project changes only in a release that is reviewed for it. The same list named two trigger misses; one is fixed here and the other is not (see "Not changed"). Design record `tasks/specs/text-release-0.113.4.md`.

**Command pages** (23cf62f)
- `/claudemd-bypass-audit`: the audit scores every project's suggest rows, each against the transcript in the project the row names; `--cwd` is where a transcript is looked for when that fails or the row names no project, so it does not narrow the numbers. The missing-transcript hint now says when re-running with `--cwd` helps. The page said `--cwd` is "what you want when auditing the project the agent is running in".
- `/claudemd-analyze`: without `--strict` it exits 0 whatever it finds; it exits 1 when the audit cannot run (a `--project`/`--cwd` that is not a directory, the two given different values, a file it reads that cannot be read) and 2 on an argument it does not accept. The page said "exits 0 by default", and `spec-coherence-audit --help` said "Default: always exit 0"; both are corrected.
- `/claudemd-uninstall restore`: a file that was a symlink when it was backed up comes back as a link to the file it pointed at (an absolute link, also when the original was relative); a link whose target is gone is skipped.
- `/claudemd-refresh`: when the install fails after the uninstall succeeded, the model relays the reinstall commands the script prints instead of reporting the refresh as done.
- `/claudemd-status`: `plugin.manifestError: "unparseable"`, `detail` and the `manifest-unparseable` hint now have a branch on the page; with the cache hint present, both are given.
- `/claudemd-statusline`: `remove` restores the whole saved statusLine object, `padding` included; `adopt` refuses a `settings.json` that parses to `null`, an array, a string, a number or a boolean (which `detect` reads as `absent`), and the page says what to tell the user.

**Module trigger** — `ship`: `打\s*tag` becomes `打\s*(个|一个)?\s*tag`, so 打个tag and 打一个 tag load the ship module. Replayed on this machine's 663 human prompts against 0.113.3's triggers, no prompt gains or loses a match (打个tag itself occurs in none). The `debug` module's trigger is byte-identical to 0.113.3's.

**Parallel `housekeeping tmp --apply` count (D#494)** — 0.113.3 backed out a fix that counted a directory only when this process's `rmdir` of it succeeded, because it failed on macOS CI alone. A probe in this release's PR, removed before the tag, raced four processes over 800 directories on every CI leg. On macos-latest, two processes' `rmdir(2)` of the same empty directory both returned success: 801, 897 and 885 successes for 800 directories on Node 20, 22 and 24 (PR CI run 39461011037). On Linux the sum was exactly 800. That design cannot count on macOS, so the count stays as in 0.113.3; `scripts/housekeeping.js` records why beside the removal. No behaviour changes.

**Not changed, and why.**
- 发个版 and a failing test in Chinese (测试挂了, CI 红了) still draw no module. This release added arms for both and backed them out after two pre-tag review rounds: each narrowing left new false matches beside it (开发一个版本, 发一个版权声明, 写测试覆盖登录失败的情况, 测试没过期的缓存), while on this machine's prompts the debug arm matched none and the 发个版 arm matched one, in a session that had already drawn ship. Case 21 of the inject suite keeps both as RESIDUAL rows.
- `ship` still matches `ship-baseline` and `pre-ship`: of the 2 prompts that matched only through a hyphenated form, 1 was followed by a release, so a tighter bound trades a hit for a miss.
- English "test failed" / "tests fail" still draw no debug module (`failing` does).
- `/claudemd-doctor`'s `hook-fail-open` row still stays red for 30 days after a real fail-open: making it clear on recovery changes doctor's exit-code semantics, outside this release.
- Parallel `housekeeping tmp --apply` runs can still report more deletions than directories they removed, a number only tmp-sweep's result file reads. A claim that holds on macOS needs a rename or a claim file, and either one leaves an orphan no sweep matches after a kill mid-removal.
- The other items under 0.113.3's "Not changed" stand.

**Tests.**
- `tests/scripts/command-page-behaviour.test.js` (new) pins each new page sentence as a whole line and, in a sandbox HOME, runs what it names: `spec-coherence-audit` exits 1 on a missing `--project` and 2 on an unknown flag, its `--help` no longer says "always exit 0", `lesson-bypass-audit --help` says `--cwd` does not narrow the audit, `status` returns every field and value the page names (without and with a plugin cache), `statusline-adopt` refuses a `null` settings file and leaves it as it was, and `refresh-plugin.sh`, run against a `claude` shim whose install fails, exits with that status and prints the commands the page quotes. Before the page edits 0 of 6 passed; after, 6 of 6. Putting the old `--help` line back fails the analyze case.
- `tests/hooks/spec-module-inject.test.sh` case 21: 打个tag and 打一个 tag inject ship (both missed on 0.113.3's module); 9 RESIDUAL rows (发个版, 发一版, 发一个版本, 测试挂了, 用例跑挂了, 单测失败了, CI 红了, ci没过, 测试又挂掉了) draw nothing and flip the case if an arm for them returns; 22 neighbours from the two review rounds (开发一个版本, 发个版本号, 发一个版权声明, 测试失败率, 写测试覆盖登录失败的情况, 测试没过期的缓存, `CI_1挂了` and others) must keep drawing nothing.
- `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`'s suite count follows the new node suite (95).
- On the tagged commit (code as in 41402d5; the commits after it change one test file and text): `( trap '' PIPE; npm run check )` exit 0 (node 1,603 of 1,604 passed, 1 skipped because `SHFMT` is not set; every shell suite passed); the bash 3.2 runtime step, all 44 suites with the hooks run under bash 3.2.57, leaving no `/tmp/sh-thd*` file; `npm run smoke` 2/2. PR CI: run 39456034935 on d976236 failed the architecture-drift suite count (94 against 96 node suites, fixed in e26828b) and run 39458386087 on 08b773d the spec-edit version gate (answered by the bump in 2334033), each cancelling the macOS legs; runs 39461011037 (2334033), 39464930475 (bad2e0f) and 39471198117 (82a4399) passed 7/7.

**Pre-tag review.** Round 1, one fresh reviewer on 575ce92..bad2e0f: 0 Critical, 0 High, 2 Medium, 11 Low. Both Mediums were on the new trigger arms: 发个版 drew ship on 开发一个版本 and 发个版本号, the debug arm drew on prompts about writing tests for a failure case, and case 21 pinned none of the arms' bounds. The arms were narrowed with a row per bound that failed first (d2764b1); the Lows, page and test text, were fixed in 82a4399, except a JS/jq divergence on emoji and `ſ` in the calibration copy. Round 2, one fresh reviewer on bad2e0f..82a4399: 0 Critical, 0 High, 2 Medium, 8 Low, both Mediums new false matches beside the narrowed arms. At the depth limit both arms were backed out (41402d5) instead of patched a third time, since on this machine's prompts they had gained nothing; the 打个tag arm drew no finding in either round. The Lows on what remains were fixed in f8a9aab. The commits after 82a4399 restore released bytes and change a test file and text; they were checked by the author's claim-drift pass. Published commit messages f68f3e5 and d2764b1 describe the arms as they were before the revert, and d2764b1 calls its rows one per bound, which two of them are not.

## [0.113.3] - 2026-10-20

**Upgrade notes — a backlog sweep: fixes only, spec unchanged (v8.2.0). Plugin users have nothing to do.** Where an output was wrong it now says what happened, so these verdicts and exit codes move:
- the §8 gate denies `rm -rf ~`, `rm -rf ~/`, `find ~ -delete` and `npm x <unpinned package>`;
- ship-baseline, banned-vocab and memory-read-check read a command inside `( )`, `$( )` or backticks as they read it bare, so a wrapped push, commit, `gh pr|release create`, `npm publish` or `cargo publish`, and for memory-read-check any of its trigger words, meets them;
- `claudemd-cli lint`: text that is not UTF-8 exits 2 instead of 0 or 1, and a `-h` inside the text is scanned instead of printing help;
- `/claudemd-clean-residue --apply` exits 3 on every run while a probe dir it cannot fully delete remains (a second run exited 0 and called the rest kept);
- `sampling-audit` exits 1 when its report cannot be written, and `spec-coherence-audit` exits 1 on a `--project` that is not a directory (0 before);
- `/claudemd-doctor` reports `hook-fail-open` and exits 3 while hooks fail open because the state directory cannot be written (no row was written before);
- maintainer tools: `housekeeping tmp --root` that is not a directory exits 2 (0), `claim-drift` refuses a range starting with `-` (2), `doc-check` given a directory exits 2 (1, with a stack trace), and `build-spec-modules` fails on a line that looks like a module marker but is not one.

To go back to 0.113.2, follow `docs/ROLLBACK.md`.

**Why.** The converge backlog (`.converge/BACKLOG.md`, local) held 75 P2 entries written between 09-13 and 10-17 that nobody had re-checked against the code. A read-only triage against 80cdce4 found 40 still real (31 were not defects, 3 already fixed, 1 stale). This release fixes 31 of the 40 in full and parts of 6 more. It also fixes three backlog entries the triage was not asked about (the 128 KiB prompt limit in spec-module injection, the node-absent install banner, `perf-baseline.sh`'s here-strings), D#455 (the status line's here-string), and the stderr class under "Every hook" below, which three of the triaged fixes each met at one site. Design record `tasks/specs/residuals-0.113.3-2026-10-20.md`.

**What changes for you.**

*§8 safety gate (`pre-bash-safety-check.sh`, on by default)*
- **`rm -rf ~` is denied like `rm -rf $HOME`** (941ecfd). A target with no `$` never reached the arm that denies a bare `$HOME`, so `rm -rf ~`, `rm -rf ~/` and `find ~ -delete` were allowed. `~/x`, `~/*`, `~user` and a find with a selection primary keep their verdicts, as `$HOME/x` and `$HOME/*` do. The gate reads targets after quote removal, so a quoted `"~"` (a directory literally named `~`) is denied too; the message names `./~`, which passes (xfp family F20).
- **`npm x`, npm's alias of `npm exec`, goes through the npx check** (9f86289): lockfile, then local install, then a pinned version. Only `npm x` right after `npm` joins: with flags allowed in between, a flag's argument named `x` (`npm --prefix x run build`) would read as the subcommand. `npm --yes x pkg` stays allowed (xfn family F19).
- Replay for both: of the 101,102 distinct Bash commands in this machine's transcripts, the 4,519 that hold rm or find followed by a `~`, or npm, were run through the 0.113.2 gate and this one; none changes verdict.

*Push and release gates (on by default)*
- **A command inside `( )`, `$( )` or backticks reaches the gates** (77dd706 ship-baseline, 8501c0e banned-vocab, 2ebd90d memory-read-check). `(git push origin main)` and `out=$(git push … 2>&1)` passed all three; so did a wrapped commit (banned-vocab's message scan) and, for banned-vocab and memory-read-check, a wrapped `gh pr|release create`, `npm publish` or `cargo publish`. Replays over this machine's transcripts changed 1 of 1,150 (ship-baseline), 2 of 4,203 (banned-vocab) and 1 of 8,589 (memory-read-check) verdicts, each from allow to deny and each a real push or commit inside `( )` or `$( )`. New denies on red CI: `(git push --help)`, `echo $(git push -h)` and `` echo `git push --help` `` (a bare `git push --help` is still exempt); none in the replay.

*Session end and session start (on by default)*
- **Session-end checkpoint** (`session-end-check.sh`): a command with 17 or more `A="x"` assignments made jq give up and dropped the checkpoint (e089374); a command ending in `tsc` (`npx tsc`, `tsc`) counts as a typecheck (5d58c44); a `HOME` with a trailing slash still keeps memory edits out of the checkpoint (cd69a35); a cwd whose name holds a newline is still a project root (f77f083).
- **Failed-install banner without node** (9152ba8): with no `node` on the PATH the hooks run with, nothing retries the install, but the banner said "Retrying this session" (or "The install retries at every session start") and deleted its sentinel. It now says that nothing retries until node is reachable and keeps the sentinel, so the next session shows it again (`DISABLE_BOOTSTRAP_FAIL_BANNER=1` still turns it off). With node nothing changes.
- A ledger with CRLF line endings yields its banner again (04b87a7); the `auto-upgrade` line reaches the bootstrap log on a machine with no `~/.claude/logs` yet, instead of a shell error on stderr (24d5778).

*Spec-module injection (`spec-module-inject.sh`, on by default)*
- A prompt of 128 KiB or more reached jq as one argument, past Linux's per-argument cap, so ship's whole-prompt trigger never matched; the text now goes on stdin (c907e25).
- The 300-character head window counted bytes under `LC_ALL=C`, so a CJK prompt could miss its module; it counts characters now (d59f234).
- When the per-session list of injected modules cannot be written, a module is no longer injected on every prompt with "Permission denied" on stderr: it is skipped and a `prereq-missing` fail-open row says why (d1e93ec).

*Hooks: the file writes found printing "Permission denied" to the user*
- bash applies redirections left to right, so `cmd > "$f" 2>/dev/null` reports a file it cannot open on the hook's own stderr, which Claude Code shows the user. c07e25e, d1e93ec and 24d5778 fixed that order where their own defects met it; 737d6d7 fixes the 13 other sites a new source rule reported across 9 files, and five writes that discarded nothing, among them the shared rule-hits row append. All now write `2>/dev/null` first. Two writes of other shapes did the same: sandbox-disposal's bare `touch` of its session ref and tmp-sweep's `> result 2>&1` for its detached sweep (42d8031). `hook_record_failopen` still writes its row when its 60 s throttle marker cannot be written, so those rows are unthrottled in that state (c07e25e). tmp-sweep's lock and stamp reads print nothing when a concurrent run removes the file mid-read (fd7f139).

*Other default-on advisories*
- **branch-prune** names at most 10 branches (`(+N more)`; the command deletes the 10 named) and gives the line once per session for an unchanged set (13c988e); a claim it cannot write does not silence it: it then gives the line on every call (8ec325e). It used to send over 10,000 characters with 150 merged branches, on every merge, pull, fetch or push. The claim files (`branch-prune-*` in `~/.claude/.claudemd-state`) are a new class `/claudemd-clean-residue` reaps.
- **memory-prompt-hint** suggests a memory again after a compaction when it was suggested before it and never read (43053b9).
- **mem-audit** accepts `**Why**（…）：` and the other parenthetical forms before the colon, and takes the first `](` on an index line as its link, so neither a title holding `(x.md)` nor a description linking another memory reads as the line's file (670ed1b, be8fa47).
- **residue-audit** writes a zero baseline when `~/.claude/tmp` does not exist yet, so that directory's later growth alarms, and its remedy lists the newest entries instead of a `find -mtime +7` that matched none of them (d1ec361).
- **tmp-sweep**'s `du` suggestion quotes a `TMPDIR` with a space, and the advisory no longer claims "nothing is reclaiming" (4de4f2d).

*Opt-in hooks*
- evidence-gate and transcript-structure-scan: a full-width colon or dash reads the same under `LC_ALL=C` (a76ace0).
- cross-repo-write: when its per-session claim cannot be written the advisory still goes out, and the row says `claim_failed` (42cdc56).
- test-failure-debug: one non-empty injection per session, also under concurrent calls, with a reason that holds for a chained command (c19629d); a lock left by a hook killed mid-injection stops blocking after a minute (270847c), though concurrent failures that meet such a lock can each take it over and inject the module twice in that session.
- ledger-staleness names only the window it read (2f439e2); verify-log keeps its row when `session_id` is empty or the cwd has a backslash (e3f5e17); deferred-notice delivers when `HOME` contains `[` (30d3cf6).

*Status line, commands and scripts*
- The status line read its input from a here-string, so with no file writable (bash 5.0 and earlier, or 5.1+ past a pipe buffer) it showed only `user@host:` (be1a7ec, D#455). `/claudemd-statusline` refuses a `settings.json` that is `[]` or `null` instead of reporting `set` or crashing, and `remove` restores the whole prior statusLine object, `padding` included, whatever its command's shape (258ce3b, 107b61e).
- `/claudemd-uninstall restore` puts a symlinked `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` back as the same link instead of a copy (5e06a1d); uninstall leaves `settings.json` untouched when it removed nothing (15ead1a).
- `/claudemd-clean-residue` keeps reporting a probe dir it cannot fully delete instead of deleting part of it and calling the rest kept (73abe09).
- `/claudemd-status` reports an unparseable manifest in new fields (`manifestError: "unparseable"`, `manifestPath`, `detail`); with the plugin cache present its `cache-present-bootstrap-pending` hint and `cacheVersions` stay as they were, and without it the hint is `manifest-unparseable` instead of none (5ce45a6, 0974a1e); `/claudemd-refresh` prints how to reinstall when the install fails after the uninstall succeeded (fb0f669).

*`claudemd-cli` and the audit scripts*
- `claudemd-cli lint`: text that is not UTF-8 (a UTF-16 byte-order mark, NUL bytes, or bytes a strict UTF-8 decoder rejects, as BOM-less UTF-16 CJK is) exits 2 saying it is not UTF-8 instead of "OK: no hits" (the message names UTF-16 or NUL bytes, also for other bytes such as Latin-1); an unreadable file names its real error instead of "file not found" (still exit 2) (91e74f5, 8d24040). `-h` inside lint's text is text, while `audit <path> -h` still prints help; `--file` given twice says so; `--help` names the repository (3cd1fbd, 8d24040). CONTRIBUTING.md's exit-code table now names the use of 2 for input a tool cannot use, which claudemd-cli, doc-check and housekeeping already made.
- `sampling-audit`: a report that cannot be written exits 1 with the reason on stderr, the summary still printed (24b92a3); a tool-only transcript gets a report (88f336f); a skill named `constructor` counts as a number, and `--until` rejects a date that does not exist (exit 1) (892c6b4); one transcript with very many distinct paths no longer overflows the stack (5003ace).
- `spec-coherence-audit`: a `--project`/`--cwd` that is not a directory exits 1 naming it (ff91c3d). `lesson-bypass-audit`: USAGE says `--cwd` is a lookup fallback, not the audit's scope; the numbers do not change (15a8364).

*Maintainer tools* — `build-spec-modules` fails on a line that looks like a module marker but is not one, with or without the colon (48eca7c, 37829d4; the built modules are byte-identical); `housekeeping` reports `truncated` past 200 branches and makes a bad `--root` a usage error (529ad53); `claim-drift` refuses a range that starts with `-` and reads names under `diff.noprefix` and non-ASCII paths (b8e0d52); `doc-check` handles CRLF, unclosed blocks, a directory argument and long digit runs, and still checks a ratio right after a comma (fc43648, 7ddcdb4); `baseline-metrics --skip-dup` says it skipped (efcdba8); `perf-baseline.sh` has no here-string left (8c19e8f).

**Tests.**
- Every fix above carries a case in the suite of the file it changes that failed on the code before its fix and passes now; the few controls beside them (a restore record written by 0.113.2, the unwrapped push) pass on both by design. Hook suites ran under bash 5.3.9 and under 3.2.57 first on PATH.
- Two new source rules in `tests/scripts/hook-full-disk-source.test.js`: no here-string in any `scripts/*.sh`, and no file redirection before `2>/dev/null` under `hooks/` (13 sites before 737d6d7, none after).
- Test-only: `hook-budget` keeps its sandbox off `/tmp/claude-*` (0a337e0, already on `main`); the sigpipe source check finds the line its mutation inserts instead of pinning 2220 (6b484e5).
- The corpus gains residual families F19 (`npm --yes x`), F20 (a quoted `"~"`), F21 (`find ~/. -delete`), F22 (`~+`/`~-` targets) and F23 (`pnpx`), rows S8-NPMX1, S8-TILDEQ1, S8-DOTSTART1–3, S8-TILDEPWD1/2 and S8-PNPX1 (6fdb653, 39d9d9e). None of these shapes occurs as a command in this machine's 101,102 distinct real ones.
- The UTF-16 CJK row of lint-cli D#174a holds no NUL, so it exercises the strict UTF-8 decode (95b9a89).
- On the tagged commit (code as in 0a66408; the commits after it change one test fixture, corpus rows and text): `( trap '' PIPE; npm run check )` exit 0 (node 1,597 of 1,598 passed, 1 skipped because `SHFMT` is not set; every shell suite passed); the bash 3.2 runtime step, all 44 suites with the hooks run under bash 3.2.57; `npm run smoke` 2/2. PR CI: run 39426400220 on 164ad9b failed on macos-latest with Node 24 (the housekeeping parallel count, backed out in 864b03a; the other two macOS legs were cancelled by fail-fast); run 39434886152 on 0a66408 passed 7/7.

**Not changed, and why.**
- cross-repo-write still splits a command on a `;` or `&&` inside quotes, which can spend its once-per-repo advisory on a commit message. A fix was written and left out: quote handling in that hook was reverted to the simple form by user decision in its design record (`tasks/specs/cross-repo-write.md` r7).
- banned-vocab still denies a commit message that quotes a banned word to remove it, and `npm --prefix dir exec pkg` is still judged from the event's cwd: the first needs a decision on how much quoted text may pass, the second would turn a deny into an allow on the §8 gate.
- The ship and debug module triggers still miss "打个tag" and "测试挂了": the trigger lines are model-visible text.
- `/claudemd-doctor`'s `hook-fail-open` row stays red for 30 days after a real fail-open: making it clear on recovery changes doctor's exit code semantics.
- tmp-sweep can still remove a long-running `vitest run`'s directory: listed since 0.93.0, and reproduced in 0.107.1 with the age floor lowered to a minute, not at the default 60. Telling a live run's directory apart needs the run's start time, which the sweep does not read.
- evidence-gate still counts a backgrounded test run as verification before its outcome is seen. A fix was shipped in this release's first draft and backed out (573528b): the outcome usually reaches the session as a task notification, which the hook does not read, so a passing background run read as unverified (64 of 88 such runs in this project's 40 latest transcripts arrived that way).
- `make install-latest` and `make checkout-deps` still count as checks at session end. The fix (ad5605f, backed out in 7f1a749) also stopped `make typecheck`, `make tests` and other check targets from counting, each costing a checkpoint the user has to approve deleting.
- `find ~/. -delete`, `find $HOME/. -delete`, `rm -rf ~+`, `find ~- -delete` and `pnpx <unpinned package>` are still allowed: registered as residuals F21–F23 in `docs/S8-RESIDUALS.md`, found by the pre-tag reviewers and not patched in a review round.
- `/claudemd-status` names `~/.claude/.claudemd-manifest.json` as `manifestPath` when the file that did not parse is the legacy `.claudemd-state/installed.json`, and says "not valid JSON" for a manifest holding JSON `null`.
- Push gates still miss `{ git push; }`, `! git push`, `time git push` and a push after `then`/`do` on the same line, and ship-baseline's `--help` exemption reads the first push segment only: unchanged from 0.113.2.
- branch-prune's once-per-session claims survive a compaction, so a set already hinted is not hinted again in that session.
- Parallel `housekeeping tmp --apply` runs can still report more deletions than directories they removed (tmp-sweep's result file only). The fix held on Linux and failed its own test on macOS in PR CI; it is backed out (864b03a) until the macOS behaviour is understood.
- Command pages that now say less than the code (`/claudemd-bypass-audit`'s `--cwd`, `/claudemd-analyze`'s exit code, `/claudemd-uninstall restore`, `/claudemd-refresh`, `/claudemd-status`, `/claudemd-statusline`'s restore and its refusal of a `[]` or `null` settings file) are model-visible text and wait for a text release. `/claudemd-clean-residue`'s page does change: its class count follows the new branch-prune claims, which a drift test checks.

**Pre-tag review.** Round 1, three fresh reviewers in parallel on b3f7169..164ad9b (the published text, the hooks, the scripts): 0 Critical, 0 High, 7 Medium, 25 Low. Repairs 573528b..0a66408: two components backed out (evidence-gate's background runs, session-end's make targets) and a third after the macOS CI failure (housekeeping's parallel count); the Mediums fixed with a case that failed before (status stays additive, branch-prune's unwritable claim, sandbox-disposal's and tmp-sweep's stderr, lint's exit codes and `audit -h`) or in this entry's text; the Lows fixed where small, the rest listed under "Not changed". Round 2, one fresh reviewer on 164ad9b..0a66408: 0 Critical, 0 High, 1 Medium (a test row that passed before its fix; fixed in 95b9a89), 8 Low (text repaired; three reviewer-constructed §8 shapes registered as F21–F23). Round 2 is the depth limit: its repairs change one test fixture, corpus rows and text only, and were checked by the author's claim-drift pass. Several published commit messages cite the SHA a change had on its branch before it was picked into this release (b1732d3, 7d92c78, b654165) or overstate a test (8d24040); the commits themselves are as described here.

## [0.113.2] - 2026-10-19

**Upgrade notes — three fixes, mostly for stock macOS (`/bin/bash` 3.2); spec unchanged (v8.2.0). Plugin users have nothing to do.** One of them (D#417) also reaches Linux: when no file can be written, bash 4.x and 5.0 need a temp file for every here-string, and 5.1 and later for one longer than a pipe buffer (64 KiB on Linux), so a hook given such input now behaves as it does with a writable disk. Otherwise the hooks behave as before, with a few more short-lived subshells (one for each file a hook writes, one for most former here-strings). To go back to 0.113.1, follow `docs/ROLLBACK.md`.

**Why.** Two of the items 0.112.0 deferred (D#417, D#421) need the same condition, a file the shell cannot write (a full disk, a quota, a file-size limit), and show first on macOS's bash 3.2; D#421 can cost a gate its deny. The third (D#444) is the fallback time limit's wait that 0.113.1 deferred. Design records `tasks/specs/macos-full-disk-2026-10-19.md` and `tasks/specs/d444-watchdog-disown-2026-10-19.md`.

**What changes for you.**
- **A deny stays one JSON object when the log cannot be written** (D#421): bash's `printf` and `echo` builtins write through the shell's stdio buffer, and when the write to a redirected file fails, macOS's libc keeps the bytes and the next flush sends them to the hook's own stdout. 0.112.0's PR CI showed banned-vocab's deny followed by its log row on the macOS leg: two objects, which Claude Code cannot read as one decision. The §8 gate in `pre-bash-safety-check.sh` also writes its log row before it prints its deny. Every place a hook writes a file from a builtin, a shell function or a `{ }` group now writes from a subshell whose stdout is that file for its whole life (24 places in 14 files), so a failed write's bytes can only go to that file. The one write left as it was runs inside a detached subshell whose stdout is already `/dev/null`. Linux's glibc drops the bytes, so Linux never showed this.
- **No here-strings** (D#417): bash 3.2 to 5.0 write every here-string (`<<<`) to a temp file, and 5.1 and later one longer than a pipe buffer; when the file cannot be written, the command it feeds does not run. Under bash 3.2, `verify-log.sh` and `test-failure-debug.sh` then ended on `TOOL: unbound variable` with exit 1, `spec-module-gate` in deny mode read no words and let a release through, and `cross-repo-write-check` read no command and gave no advisory (all four are opt-in). Under bash 5.3.9 the same gate let a 70,000-character release command through. All 13 here-strings under `hooks/` are gone. The two that split a command segment into words on the PreToolUse path use `set -f` and an unquoted expansion, as the §8 gate's `rm` check does; the other eleven, seven of them in `sandbox-disposal-check`, `mem-audit`, `memory-prompt-hint`, `session-start-check`'s update check and `transcript-structure-scan`, read from a process substitution: `< <(printf '%s\n' …)` in eight, `<(printf …)` as a file argument in `mem-audit`'s two, and the `awk` pipeline itself in `transcript-structure-scan`'s first.
- **Fallback time limit** (D#444): on a machine with neither `timeout` nor `gtimeout` (stock macOS), a call whose command finished before the watchdog started waiting returned only after the whole limit under bash 3.2. With a 1 s limit that was 17 of 500 `/bin/true` calls, 13 of 500 calls of the builtin `true` and 12 of 1,000 calls of a missing command; none under bash 5.3. The caller no longer waits for the watchdog (it is disowned, then killed): 0 of 1,000 for each of the three under bash 3.2 and 5.3, and no `Terminated` line. Correction to 0.113.1's entry, which said the `sleep` "is now killed with the watchdog" and that "a reader of an inherited descriptor no longer waits out the limit": when the command finishes inside that window under bash 3.2, the watchdog and its `sleep` still run in the background until the limit and keep the caller's descriptors past 2 open. Only the caller itself returns at once.

**Tests.**
- 1ff0058 and 09fbc11 (D#444): `platform` case 15 runs 300 calls of the external `true` and fails at the first one to take a second. Under bash 3.2.57 it failed 3 of 3 runs before the change (calls 11, 13 and 29 took 2.003 s); after it, 3 of 3 runs pass under 3.2.57 and 15 of 15 under 5.3. c13f6d0 and 8d1fa16: `session-end-check` 33i (the other five markers of a stopped operation) and 33j (the 2 s guard, with a slow `jq` that is shown to answer), the gaps 0.113.1's review named; deleting any one of the six guards turns only its own case red, and three mutations of 33j's slow-`jq` wrapper are caught.
- f16df43 (D#417): `spec-module-gate` 17, `verify-log` 12, `test-failure-debug` 10 and `cross-repo-write` X23 run the hook with no file writable (a zero file-size limit with SIGXFSZ ignored). Each failed on the previous hooks under bash 3.2.57 and passes now. Their inputs are short, so they discriminate only where every here-string needs a temp file (bash 5.0 and earlier): the bash 3.2 runtime step, and the macOS legs, whose `bash` is `/bin/bash` 3.2. `spec-module-gate` 17b, added after the pre-tag review, sends a 70,000-character release command and failed on the previous hooks under bash 5.3.9 too.
- 341a252 and e939d6e (D#421): `tests/scripts/hook-full-disk-source.test.js` reads `hooks/` with the shell reader the SIGPIPE check uses (moved to `tests/lib/shell-scan.mjs`). It fails on any here-string, and on any redirection of stdout to a file from a printing builtin on its list (33, among them `printf`, `echo`, `eval` and `source`), a function, a `{ }` group or loop, or a bare `exec`, also inside a subshell. On the hooks before f16df43 it reports 13 here-strings and 24 such writes (a 25th, the one it keeps, is listed instead); six mutation rows each produce the problem they name. Since the pre-tag review it also reads `eval`, `source`, `.`, `pushd`, `popd`, `cd`, `kill`, `help`, `history`, `fc`, `bind`, `enable`, a `1<>` redirection and functions written `function name {`; none of them writes a file under `hooks/`. Three rows discriminate on macOS only, since glibc drops the bytes: `rule-hits` STDOUT-1, a `pre-bash-safety` §8 deny for a logged session is the only object on stdout, and `banned-vocab` 26n runs with the log on again (it ran with it off since 0.112.0). The log append's subshell, 200 rows, best of 3: bash 5.3.9 1.54 s → 1.54 s, bash 3.2.57 1.61 s → 1.67 s.
- On the tagged commit (code as in e939d6e plus the round-1 repairs, which change tests, one comment and this entry): `npm run check` with SIGPIPE ignored, exit 0 (node 1,557 of 1,558 passed, 1 skipped because `SHFMT` is not set; every shell suite passed; shellcheck clean on 94 files); `npm run smoke` 2/2; the bash 3.2 runtime step, all 44 suites with the hooks run under bash 3.2.57. PR CI: run 39307188194 on 42178ab failed on macos-latest with Node 22 on two test rows (`platform` 15 called `/bin/true`, which macOS does not have; `cross-repo-write` X23 expected the logical `/var/folders` path where the hook names the physical one), and fail-fast cancelled the other two macOS legs, which had printed the same two failures, and ubuntu-latest with Node 20; repaired in 59d1477; run 39309522043 on 59d1477 and run 39314681157 on 6db20b1, the last code commit, 7 of 7 jobs green each.

**Not changed, and why.**
- `session-end-check.sh` still writes its checkpoint through a here-doc (`cat > … <<EOF`). Under bash 5.0 and earlier that needs a temp file; when none can be written, `cat` does not run and the hook takes it as a failed write: no checkpoint, as when the project itself cannot be written. For a project on the volume that holds `/tmp` the two fill up together; for one on another volume, a full `/tmp` costs the checkpoint.
- The source check does not read inside `$( )`, `<( )` or backticks. A failed write's bytes there would end up in the captured value or in what the reader reads, not on the hook's stdout; no file is written inside one under `hooks/`.
- `scripts/statusline.sh`, the status line rather than a hook, still reads its input from a here-string, so under the same condition it gets no input (D#455).
- Closed without a change, on measurements: D#420 (a memory file written with `cat >> F` counts as read: over 225 transcripts, at most 2 sessions where it could change a deny, likely none) and D#144 (sandbox-disposal on macOS does not scan `$TMPDIR`; its `/tmp` arm there only ever matched this repository's own test directories).
- 0.113.1's "Not changed" list stands otherwise.

**Pre-tag review.** Round 1, a fresh reviewer on 41b675c..42178ab: 0 Critical, 2 High, 1 Medium, 8 Low. The two Highs were the two macOS test rows above. The Medium: this entry said the hooks behave as before under bash 4 and later; it now says what reaches bash 4.x and 5.x, and `spec-module-gate` 17b covers it. Of the Lows, the round-1 repairs fix a stale comment in `cross-repo-write-check.sh`, the shapes the source check did not read, this entry's write count and two "Not changed" reasons, and its placeholders; `scripts/statusline.sh`'s here-string is deferred (D#455). One pushed commit message, e939d6e's, keeps a write count that includes the kept write (25) and the pre-rebase SHA 1b0e6d5 for f16df43: rewording it would rewrite the branch under review. Round 2, a new reviewer on the repairs 42178ab..6db20b1: 0 Critical, 0 High, 0 Medium, 8 Low. Seven are text: test comments that still said "bash 3.2 only", a wrong comment about `>( )` (its stdout is the hook's; there is none under `hooks/`), this entry's account of the builtins the check reads, of the round-1 CI run, of the eleven here-string replacements and of the commit messages, and the design record; they are repaired in text-only commits, which got this repository's claim-drift pass instead of a third review; that pass also found the new gate's row in `docs/ADDING-NEW-HOOK.md` still naming bash 3.2 only, now repaired. The eighth is left as it is: the check's function pattern also collects `awk` function names inside quoted programs, which can only make it report more, and reports nothing today.

## [0.113.1] - 2026-10-17

**Upgrade notes — four fixes left by 0.113.0's pre-tag review; spec unchanged (v8.2.0). Plugin users have nothing to do.** What you can see: the session-end checkpoint (`tasks/session-end-<session>-paused.md`) and the line the hook prints at exit no longer count a file that git reports unchanged from the commit at HEAD by the end of the session, such as a file a `git checkout` of a committed branch rewrote, and when every open change is such a file the hook writes no checkpoint and no rule-hits row. Such a session counts toward the §13.2 batch-review reminder, which counts `deny`, `warn` and `deny-repeat` rows, only if it wrote another one. To go back to 0.113.0, follow `docs/ROLLBACK.md`, which now says how to make Claude Code load an older version.

**Why.** 0.113.0's pre-tag review took D#404 (the same checkpoint fix) out of that release because it dropped real edits, and left three Lows open (D#434). This release is the redo (D#433) and those three. Design record `tasks/specs/d433-d434-2026-10-17.md`.

**What changes for you.**
- **Session-end checkpoint** (D#433): when changes are still open at the end and the session's directory is in a git repository, the hook asks git about those files and leaves out the settled ones. A change is settled only when its file is tracked (the plain `H` tag of `git ls-files -v`), `git status` does not list it, it is not a symlink, and its directory resolves to a path inside the repository. Everything else stays on the checkpoint: a symlink (an edit through `AGENTS.md -> CLAUDE.md` changes the target), a file reached through a directory link out of the repository, every absent file (git cannot tell a file a switch removed from a deleted untracked or ignored file, or one in a submodule or under `.git`), anything untracked, ignored, assume-unchanged, skip-worktree or unmerged, and anything `git status` lists as changed, staged, deleted or renamed. The check is off while a rebase, merge, cherry-pick or revert is stopped (an autostash is kept in the git directory, not in `refs/stash`), when a stash exists that is newer than the prompt (or a stash exists and the prompt has no time to compare), and past 1,000 open changes. Each git call is bounded at 0.5 s and none starts once the hook has run for 2 s, inside its 3 s limit; a failure or timeout leaves every change counted. Where neither `timeout` nor `gtimeout` exists, the bound stops a direct git call, not a git that is a wrapper script. The checkpoint says that settled files are not counted whenever git answered, and the `open` field of its rule-hits row counts the same way (`docs/RULE-HITS-SCHEMA.md`).
- **Log rotation** (D#434): a FIFO at `~/.claude/logs/claudemd.jsonl.rotating` no longer blocks an append to an over-cap log until the hook's time limit (0.113.0 listed it under "Not changed"). Anything at that path now counts as a held lock; the stale-lock reap removes a FIFO there after 60 seconds, as it does a lock file.
- **Time limits without coreutils** (D#434): on a machine with neither `timeout` nor `gtimeout` (stock macOS), the hooks' fallback time limit left a `sleep` running for the rest of the limit after the command finished, holding every file descriptor of its caller past 2. It is now killed with the watchdog. Nothing in a hook's output changes; a reader of an inherited descriptor no longer waits out the limit, which made `tests/hooks/session-start.test.sh` take 58 s instead of 15 s on such a PATH.
- **`docs/ROLLBACK.md`** (D#434): "Local machine needs the previous version back" now gives the steps: close the other Claude Code windows, remove the `claudemd` marketplace (this uninstalls the plugin), add `sdsrss/claudemd#vX.Y.Z`, install, run that version's installer with `CLAUDEMD_ALLOW_DOWNGRADE=1`, restart; what the two `/claudemd-refresh` banners mean while the marketplace is pinned and how to turn them off; and the way back to the newest release. The steps were run on Claude Code 2.1.302 with `HOME` pointed at a scratch directory.

**Tests.**
- 29bd8ef and 902c86f (D#433): `session-end-check` cases 32a–h and 33a–h. On 0.113.0's hook 32a, 32b, 32d, 32f, 32g, 32h, 33d, 33e and 33g fail (32d: 'kept kept kept' for 'kept dropped kept'; 33e: 1,000 kept of 1); 32c, 32e, 33a–c, 33f and 33h are the keep-side cases. Of thirteen mutants of the check, twelve turn a case red; dropping the rename-source parse does not, because a renamed-away file is absent and stays anyway. PASS under bash 5.3 and 3.2.57, with and without `timeout` on PATH. Hook time, best of 3 at load ~4.8: 1,000 open files of which 999 settle, 0.17 s → 0.49 s under bash 5.3 and 0.16 s → 0.85 s under bash 3.2; 12,530, past the cap, 2.27 s → 2.28 s and 2.82 s → 2.67 s. `hook-budget` now reads a fractional time limit (it read `platform_timeout 0.5` as 0).
- d08a0e4: `rule-hits` ROT-10 red before (the append blocked on the FIFO), green after. 33eb8ee and a3c14be: `platform` case 14 red before (a reader of fd 3 blocked the full 5 s), 14/14 after, also under bash 3.2.57; the watchdog left its sleep running in 52 of 300 calls under bash 5.3 and 10 of 300 under 3.2 after 33eb8ee, 0 of 300 after a3c14be. a1395d1: `contributing-rollback-drift` joins the recipe's command lines to `marketplace.json` and `package.json`, red on the old text, 11/11 after.
- On the tagged commit (code as in 902c86f and a3c14be, plus the round-2 comment and text repairs): `npm run check` with SIGPIPE ignored, exit 0 (node 1,546 of 1,547 passed, 1 skipped because `SHFMT` is not set; every shell suite passed; shellcheck clean on 94 files); `npm run smoke` 2/2; the bash 3.2 runtime step, all 44 suites with the hooks run under bash 3.2.57. PR CI: run 39074155046 on 8076ee1 (before the round-1 repairs) and run 39078288382 on 5b8d2ef (after them), 7 of 7 jobs green each.

**Not changed, and why.**
- The session-end hook's `git rev-parse --show-toplevel`, there since before 0.113.0, still has no time limit. With one, a slow git would drop the repository's top level from the project roots, and a missed root stops counting real edits.
- "Unchanged" is git's answer: with `core.fileMode=false` a mode-only change is settled, and a stash whose commit date is pinned in the past (`GIT_COMMITTER_DATE`) reads as older than the prompt, so its work is not counted.
- The fallback time limit (no `timeout` or `gtimeout`) under bash 3.2 can wait out its whole limit when the wrapped command ends before the watchdog has started waiting: `platform_timeout 0.3 /bin/true` did so in 16 of 300 calls (0 under bash 5.3, 0 of 300 for `git rev-parse`, 0 of 200 session-end runs). 0.113.0 never did, and left the sleep running on every call instead. Deferred.
- A symlink or a non-empty directory at the rotation lock path can keep the log from rotating until it is removed: the reap leaves a dangling symlink and a non-empty directory alone, as in 0.113.0, and a symlink to a device such as `/dev/null`, which 0.113.0 took as its lock and removed, now counts as held.
- 0.113.0's "Not changed" list stands otherwise: core §11's premature-stop line goes into the planned ablation (D#328); D#420, D#421, D#417, §8 family F18, D#418 and D#349 for the reasons given in 0.112.0.

**Pre-tag review.** Round 1, two reviewers in parallel on 73d40cc..8076ee1, one on this entry and every place its subjects are described, one on the code: 0 Critical, 0 High, 4 Medium, 17 Low. Three Mediums were in the D#433 check (absent files in a submodule, under `.git`, ignored or untracked were dropped; a rebase stopped with an autostash was dropped; past the cap the hook ran 3.4 s under bash 3.2), repaired in 902c86f; the fourth was this ROLLBACK recipe ending in banners it did not mention, repaired in the text. Of the Lows, 902c86f also repaired an unrepresentable file name being dropped, the checkpoint claiming filtering that did not run, the stale header comments and a test comment; a3c14be the watchdog's remaining orphan sleep; 6020ccc the text ones; the git-semantics ones and the lock-path symlink are under "Not changed". Round 2, a new reviewer on the repair range 8076ee1..5b8d2ef: 0 Critical, 0 High, 0 Medium, 13 Low. The text ones are repaired in a text-only commit before the tag, which got this repository's claim-drift pass instead of a third review: the `DISABLE_UPSTREAM_CHECK` descriptions in README and `/claudemd-status` now say it also turns off the stale-registration banner, ROLLBACK says the two banners come one after the other, and this entry states the stash and batch-reminder conditions exactly. The watchdog wait above is deferred; untested markers of a stopped operation and of the 2 s guard (each checked by the reviewer's probes) and a commit spelled `git -c k=v commit` that the validation pattern does not read are left as they are.

## [0.113.0] - 2026-10-17

**Upgrade notes — spec v8.2.0: the spec no longer sends UI checks to gstack, and reviews are spawned unnamed so their reports arrive whole. Plugin users have nothing to do.** Spec v8.2.0 changes several rules (all listed below); two of them change what an agent does by default. Core §2.1 said "UI/visual verify → gstack's `browse`, through the `gstack` skill when `browse` is not listed on its own; not `mcp__claude-in-chrome__*` / computer-use"; that line is gone, so an agent checking a UI uses whichever browser tool its session lists and no longer avoids the Chrome tools. To keep the old routing, put that line in a file under `~/.claude/rules/`, which every project on the machine loads, or in one project's `CLAUDE.md`. And §EXT §11-O's "Report by file" (an output path for every review and audit, a final message of at most 1,500 characters) becomes "Long reports": reviews and audits are spawned without a `name` and put the whole report in their final message, because a named spawn's report reaches the parent cut at 4,000 characters and an unnamed one's arrives whole. To take the plugin and spec back to 0.112.0 (spec v8.1.0), follow `docs/ROLLBACK.md`: its installer refuses a downgrade unless `CLAUDEMD_ALLOW_DOWNGRADE=1` is set. If 0.112.0's log stops rotating after that (a 0.113.0 hook killed mid-rotation can leave a `~/.claude/logs/claudemd.jsonl.rotating` file, which 0.112.0 neither reaps nor gets past), delete that file. Of the hooks, only the log writer's rotation lock changes (no change to any hook's output), and `spec-module-inject` injects the modules' v8.2.0 text.

**Why.** The spec half comes from a `/doctor prompt-audit` of everything this project's sessions load (`docs/prompt-audit-2026-10-17.md`; decisions in `tasks/specs/prompt-audit-fixes-2026-10-17.md`). The maintainer is removing gstack and asked that the spec stop calling it. The fixes waited on `next-release` after 0.112.0 (`tasks/specs/post-0.112-residuals.md`); each was reproduced first with a test that failed, and each commit message names the test and the failure.

**What changes for you.**
- **Spec v8.2.0** (full entry in `spec/CLAUDE-changelog.md`; no HARD rule added or removed): core §2.1 drops the gstack line and "unfamiliar module → module overview before 3+ Reads". §7's push-onto-red rule (HARD) gains its reason. §8's ban on deep traversal of `~/.claude/` keeps its scope and gives the right reason (tool output is saved under `~/.claude/projects/<project>/<session>/tool-results/`; `~/.claude/tmp/` holds scratch files only when the sandbox sets TMPDIR there) and no longer recommends a Grep tool that some sessions do not have. §11-O Long reports, above. §11-EXT-MEM gives the memory directory as `~/.claude/projects/<encoded-git-root>/memory/`, where Claude Code keeps it. `OPERATOR.md` and `hard-rules.json`'s `_doc` say demotion reads 0 fired rows (`hits.fired`) for hook-enforced entries outside §8, as `/claudemd-rules` has since 0.111.0 (D#399). Smaller edits: §12 Blind brief, which `review.md` carries, points at Long reports; §11-O's polling fallback names the output path it polls; §6 and §7-EXT lose their "(core §2.1)" pointers.
- **Log rotation** (the log writer every hook shares): the lock that keeps two rotations from losing each other's archives was `mkdir`, and with Ubuntu's default coreutils (uutils 0.10.0) four racing processes got more than one winner in 750 of 1,000 trials on tmpfs and 780 of 1,000 on ext4. It is now a noclobber file claim: 0 of 1,000 on both. An older hook's lock (an empty directory) still excludes this one and is still reaped (D#357).
- **Commands**: `/claudemd-rules` states its 30-day window without version history; `/claudemd-clean-residue` cites the retention policy's own section, §7-EXT-TMP.
- **`scripts/perf-baseline.sh`** (maintainer tool): the probed hooks get a sandbox `TMPDIR`, so a run no longer leaves a `claudemd-sync-perf-baseline-session` sentinel in the real `/tmp`, where it also sent the next run's version-sync probes down an early exit.

**Tests.**
- 731e858 (D#357): rule-hits ROT-8 red before for both lock forms, ROT-9 new; five mutants each turn the suite red; all 44 shell suites under bash 3.2.57. 59b64df: perf-baseline-hermetic case 7 red before (the sentinel), 7/7 after. 358d4af (D#405): the SessionStart case 20b race reproduced 3/3 with a shim, 3/3 green after; 88/88 under bash 5.3 and 3.2.57.
- The spec edits: `spec-structure` re-pins the two §11-O lines and the §12 Blind brief line, rewords the `_doc` window assertion to the fired-row wording (still 30d), and updates the section hashes of every edited section, and no other section's hash moved; `version-cascade-check` and `spec-coherence-audit --strict` (coreDelta=0, extendedDelta=0) clean.
- On 2c6732b, the code this release tags (D#404 reverted, round-1 repairs in): `npm run check` with SIGPIPE ignored, exit 0 (node 1,545 of 1,546 passed, 1 skipped because `SHFMT` is not set; every shell suite passed; shellcheck clean on 94 files); `npm run smoke` 2/2. The local bash 3.2 runtime step was skipped (`BASH32_BIN` unset): `session-end-check.sh` is back at 0.112.0's bytes, and `rule-hits.sh` has not changed since 731e858's run of all 44 shell suites under bash 3.2.57. After 2c6732b only this entry, `spec/CLAUDE-changelog.md` and the two design records under `tasks/specs/` changed. PR CI run 39062343641 on 2c6732b, 7 of 7 jobs (the first run, 39060803291 on 58a1599, also 7 of 7).

**Not changed, and why.**
- Core §11's "a progress summary, a next step announced instead of taken … = confirmed premature stop" stays as it is. The audit rated it Medium (current models keep going without it), but the official migration guide says Sonnet 5.5 and Haiku 5.5 at `low` effort stop early more often, and this spec loads into those models' subagents too; it goes into the planned ablation (D#328) instead.
- **Session-end checkpoint (D#404)**: a `git checkout` of a committed branch still leaves a checkpoint that counts the files it rewrote (and lists the last three). A fix was in this release's branch (8d88816) and was taken back out before the tag (4d73f7b): it dropped a file once `git status` no longer listed it, and the pre-tag review found entries it dropped that should stay, among them an edit made through an in-repo symlink (`AGENTS.md -> CLAUDE.md`: git lists the target), a file under an in-repo symlink to a directory outside the repository, and files marked assume-unchanged; two of its git calls also ran without a time limit. It fixed noise (1 of the 10 checkpoints the hook would write over the maintainer's 225 transcripts), and those holes are silences in the hook that reports unvalidated changes.
- **Log rotation**: a FIFO planted at `~/.claude/logs/claudemd.jsonl.rotating` makes the next append to an over-cap log block until the hook's time limit, so a gate's deny is lost for that call; 0.112.0's `mkdir` failed on it instead. Planting it takes write access to `~/.claude/logs`, the stale-lock reap removes it after 60 seconds, and the shape is in §8's registered timeout family (E3 in `docs/S8-RESIDUALS.md`).
- The rest of 0.112.0's "Not changed" list stands: D#420 (a memory file written with `cat > F <<'EOF'` counts as read), D#421 and D#417 (macOS bash 3.2 with a full disk), §8 family F18, D#418 and D#349, each for the reason given there.

**Pre-tag review.** Round 1, two reviewers in parallel on 7af6322..58a1599, one on this entry and every place its subjects are described, one on the code: 0 Critical, 1 High, 2 Medium, 21 Low. The High and one Medium were the same sentence (this entry said the checkpoint lists every open file). The other Medium was D#404 dropping an edit made through an in-repo symlink, one of seven code-review findings on that component, which went back to 0.112.0's bytes (4d73f7b) instead of getting a patch; its Lows left with it. The other Lows were text, repaired in 2c6732b, except three now deferred (D#434): a FIFO at the rotation lock path (disclosed above), `docs/ROLLBACK.md` not saying how to make Claude Code load the old version, and a session-start test suite 38 seconds slower on a PATH without `timeout`. Round 2, a new reviewer on 58a1599..2c6732b: 0 Critical, 0 High, 1 Medium, 7 Low, all text (the Medium: the Tests line cited a tree from before the revert), repaired in 3e02fdf, which got this repository's claim-drift pass instead of a third review.

## [0.112.0] - 2026-10-17

**Upgrade notes — the memory gates now cover sessions started in a repository subdirectory or a linked worktree; spec unchanged (v8.1.0). Plugin users have nothing to do.** The default most users can meet changes in one place. `memory-read-check` (the §11 gate that blocks a ship, push or release command until the memory files whose tags it matches have been read) and `memory-prompt-hint` now look for `MEMORY.md` under the repository's git root, which is where Claude Code keeps `memory/` for every subdirectory and linked worktree of a repository. A session started below the root used to look under its own start directory, usually found no index there and was never blocked; it now is, as a session started at the root always was. One case goes the other way: if the start directory has an index of its own (written by a Claude Code that keyed memory by the start directory) and the root has none, 0.111.0 used that index and could block, and 0.112.0 reads the root's, which Claude Code loads, and does not. To turn the gate off, set `DISABLE_MEMORY_READ_HOOK=1` (and `DISABLE_MEMORY_HINT_HOOK=1` for the hint) in the `env` block of `~/.claude/settings.json`. To take the plugin back to 0.111.0, follow `docs/ROLLBACK.md`: its installer refuses a downgrade unless `CLAUDEMD_ALLOW_DOWNGRADE=1` is set. Every session start directory in the maintainer's transcripts (8) resolves to itself, so no verdict there moves. Other default output changes too, listed below: sparkline arrows, `/claudemd-toggle`'s notes, `/claudemd-status`'s `persisted`, the memory directory `/claudemd-doctor` and `/claudemd-analyze` read, and which files a no-manifest uninstall deletes.

**Why.** 0.111.0 left deferred issues (D#390–D#392), four decisions its review kept, and design questions from the converge backlog. Four fixes (D#213, D#288, D#301, D#390) had been held until 2026-10-26 because two pre-registered evaluations (`tasks/g1b-g2-eval`, `tasks/lang-drift`, local) required their files unchanged; both evaluations closed their analysis segment at the 0.109.0 exposure and were read on 2026-10-16, so the hold no longer applied. Each fix below was reproduced first with a test that failed; each commit message names the test and the failure. Decision record: `tasks/specs/post-0.111-residuals.md`.

**What changes for you.**
- **`/claudemd-doctor` and `/claudemd-analyze`** look for `MEMORY.md` under the git root as well. From a subdirectory or a worktree, `/claudemd-analyze` reported "no index" and skipped, and the doctor's memory maintenance scanned the start directory's `memory/` and had nothing to report.
- **macOS `/bin/bash` (bash 3.2) with no writable temp directory** (a full disk, an unwritable `TMPDIR`): four programs the hooks read at load time (three in their libraries, one in `pre-bash-safety`) came from here-docs, which bash 3.2 writes to temp files. With none writable the programs were unset, and `banned-vocab`'s scan of the last turn's prose let a push through. They are string literals now, with the same bytes (D#213). Under that condition `pre-bash-safety` now gives its normal verdicts: 0.111.0's empty program passed every here-doc body through, so any here-doc with a `/dev/tcp` line in its body was denied there, data (`cat <<EOF`) and a body a shell runs (`sh <<EOF`) alike; both are allowed now, as they always are otherwise (the second is family F18 below).
- **`/claudemd-toggle`**: re-enabling a hook inside a session that has its `DISABLE_*_HOOK=1` set adds a note that the hook stays off until Claude Code is relaunched, and that a shell export of the variable has to be removed there too. A running session applies a saved settings value at once but does not unset a removed one (Claude Code docs, environment variables). The note also names a project's `.claude/settings.json` or `.claude/settings.local.json` that sets the same variable and so decides, reads the opt-in value through those files, and names `DISABLE_CLAUDEMD_HOOKS=1` under an "enabled".
- **`/claudemd-status`**: `persisted` is the layered value, a project's settings files over the user file, and a JSON number `1` counts as `"1"`, so a kill switch set by a project file no longer shows as a pending change.
- **Uninstall without a manifest** keeps more files: a spec file counts as claudemd's only when its first line starts with `# AI-CODING-SPEC `, and a `spec-modules/` file only when its module heading is the second line after the frontmatter's closing fence, after a blank line. Every shipped file still reads as claudemd's: 1,013 spec and 211 module files across all 278 tags.
- **`/claudemd-sparkline`** draws a trend arrow only when the two buckets' counts would be unlikely under equal rates (an exact two-sided 5% test), on top of the ±20% rule; 3 events against 1 read ↗ before. On the maintainer's log 9 of 28 rows went from an arrow to ≈ when this was written, none the other way.
- **Session summary** on busybox (Alpine): busybox `date` has no form for "24 hours ago", so the summary's 24-hour fallback started at 1970 and counted the whole log as "last 24 h". Both window starts now come from `jq`.
- **`evidence-gate`** (opt-in, `EVIDENCE_GATE=1`): a Done label followed by more than about 128 KiB of text was missed, a pipe race (D#288). The "not run" exemption reads the same in every locale: `→ not run原因…` was exempt under `LC_ALL=C` and not under a UTF-8 locale (D#342). Its verification patterns count `bash tests/…` as a runner and an upper-case `OVERALL:` line as a verdict (D#390). Replaying the 1,969 real turn ends on disk at the time, that pattern change moves 0 verdicts.
- **`verify-log`** (opt-in, `EVIDENCE_WTREE=1`): a run whose output ends in run-all's or bash32-runtime's `OVERALL: 1 suite(s) failed` or `OVERALL: 2 of 44 suite(s) failed` behind a `tail` was logged as a passing run; it is now kept out. Only that `OVERALL:` line with a nonzero count counts, so `0 of 44 suite(s) failed` stays a pass. Replaying 3,759 candidate runs against 0.111.0's patterns: 385 newly logged (read by hand as passes), 70 newly kept out as failures, 6 moved from logged to kept out, each with a real `OVERALL: 1 suite(s) failed` line.

**Tests.**
- Every fix came with a test that failed before it; the commit messages name each test and the failure it showed.
- `pre-bash-safety` (the §8 gate) changed only in how the two here-doc programs it uses are assigned, its own and the one it takes from `hook-common.sh` (D#213), and in neither's bytes. The 15,518 real commands with a `<<` in the maintainer's transcripts, replayed through the 0.111.0 hooks and these: 13,963 allowed and 1,555 denied by both, 0 verdicts and 0 bytes of output different.
- On c26019e, the code this release tags: `npm run check` exit 0 with a built bash 3.2.57 (node 1,545 of 1,546 passed, 1 skipped because `SHFMT` is not set; bash 2,378 of 2,378; all 44 shell suites, 40 hook and 4 integration, also executed under bash 3.2; shellcheck clean on 94 files); the same with SIGPIPE ignored, exit 0; `npm run smoke` 2/2; PR CI run 39040045565, 7 of 7 jobs. The first full run had failed 3 node tests, all caused by the new `memory-root.test.js`: `home-sandbox-consumers` (it set `HOME` by hand) and two in `architecture-drift` (the suite count in `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`); fixed in 555480f.
- The first CI run on the release branch (run 39029952035) failed on macOS in two places, both repaired: `banned-vocab` case 26n, whose zero file-size limit also failed the hook's log write, printed that log row on stdout after the deny JSON (macOS's bash 3.2 keeps a failed builtin write's bytes; not fixed in this release, see below; 26n now writes no log), and `memory-read-check` case 66 built its worktree fixture on a logical path under macOS's `/var` symlink while git writes worktree pointers physically (eeb3cc5; reproduced on Linux with a symlinked `TMPDIR`). The next run (39031306082) passed all 7 jobs.

**Not changed, and why.**
- Memory gates: writing a memory file with `cat > F <<'EOF'` or `cat >> F <<'EOF'` still counts as having read it (D#301, reopened as D#420; 43 such segments in the 714 Bash commands that name a memory file on the maintainer's machine). A fix was in this release's branch and was taken back out before the tag: skipping write targets kept the scan going over every occurrence of the path, and the pre-tag review timed a constructed 81 KB command naming three memory files at 3.4–4.2 s under bash 3.2, past the hook's 3-second limit (0.111.0: 1.9 s).
- macOS, a full disk: when a hook's file write from a shell builtin fails, bash 3.2 can later print the lost bytes on the hook's stdout after its JSON, so a gate's deny reaches Claude Code as two objects (found by this release's CI; about 21 places, D#421). A fix that covered 5 of them was taken back out before the tag.
- `spec/OPERATOR.md` still says rules are demoted on "0 hits in 30d", while the demote lists read `hits.fired` since 0.111.0 (D#399); and `spec/CLAUDE-extended.md` and the `memory` spec module still describe the memory directory as `<encoded-cwd>/memory/`, where Claude Code keys it by the git root. Spec text changes ride the next spec release with its version cascade.
- §8 family F18, registered in `docs/S8-RESIDUALS.md`: a here-doc body that its consumer runs (`bash <<EOF`, `cat <<EOF | sh`) is read as data, so a `rm -rf $X/y` or a reverse shell inside it is not denied, as in 0.111.0. Of 93,372 distinct real commands, 19 feed a here-doc to a shell, and none of them runs a §8 shape.
- The `evidence-gate` "not run" exemption still accepts prose such as "does not run on Windows". Across the 1,967 real turn ends on disk when this was checked, the 9 English hits were discussion of this hook or honest partials ("was not run end to end"); requiring a verdict shape would nag those and catch nothing observed.
- The memory gates and `/claudemd-doctor` do not read Claude Code's `autoMemoryDirectory` setting, as before. On four constructed layouts git does not write (spaces before `gitdir:`, a `//` pointer, a relative pointer through a symlink whose lexical path does not exist, a start directory `<symlink>/../sub`), the bash and JS root resolvers disagree (D#418).
- Under bash 3.2 with no writable temp directory, here-strings in `verify-log`, `sandbox-disposal-check`, `cross-repo-write-check`, `spec-module-gate`, `mem-audit`, `memory-prompt-hint`, `session-start-check`, `test-failure-debug` and `transcript-structure-scan` still fail as they did in 0.111.0 (D#417); this release fixed only what the hooks read at load time.
- On this machine's bash 5.3.9, `pre-bash-safety` aborts (`*** stack smashing detected ***`, exit 134, no output, so the command goes through) on a constructed 150 KB command more often than 0.111.0 did: 21 of 1,200 runs against 2 of 600. It is a crash in bash whose odds move with the scripts' memory layout: with either `pre-bash-safety-check.sh` or `hook-common.sh` back at 0.111.0's bytes it was 0 of 600, so it takes both of D#213's literal assignments, and the release keeps them, because they fix a failure that is certain under its condition. At 40 KB, near the largest real command on the maintainer's machine (46,845 bytes), 0 of 1,000; no real command there is over 100 KB (D#349). A local test run on bash 5.3.9 can go red on one of the 150 KB rows with that message; none of this branch's CI runs did.
- The SessionStart suite's case 20b (a failed background install with a reason) can read an empty output under bash 3.2 at high load: once in this release's full run, and 1 of 16 runs on the 0.111.0 tree as well (D#405).

**Pre-tag review.** Round 1, two reviewers in parallel on 1a2ab6c..f26a672, one on this entry and every place its subjects are described, one on the code: 0 Critical, 0 High, 2 Medium, 24 Low. The Mediums: the upgrade note said a session below the root was never blocked before, which is wrong when the start directory had an index of its own and the root none (the note now says so), and §8 family F18, registered rather than patched under the registry's round rule. Repaired with tests: `verify-log`'s failure arm reads only the `OVERALL:` line (b06f48d), `/claudemd-status` reads a numeric `1` (289e793), `/claudemd-toggle`'s note names a shell export (5c3fc17), an unreadable worktree pointer prints nothing (756b812); the rest was text. Round 2, a new reviewer on the repairs and the CI fixes (f26a672..c047dcb): 0 Critical, 1 High, 1 Medium, 7 Low. The High (a stdout-leak fix that covered 5 of about 21 places, with a check that could not see the rest) and the Medium (the D#301 change's cost under bash 3.2) sat in two components, which went back to 0.111.0's bytes (1c73a64, 655f1f1) instead of a third patch; both items are listed above as not changed. Everything after round 2 is those two reverts, two test adjustments and text, which got this repository's claim-drift pass instead of a third review.

## [0.111.0] - 2026-10-16

**Upgrade notes — fixes from playing the plugin as a user; two `claudemd-cli` exit codes change; spec unchanged (v8.1.0). Plugin users have nothing to do.** Two default behaviours of the published `claudemd-cli` change. `claudemd-cli lint ""` (an empty or blank positional) now exits 2 instead of 0: a `.git/hooks/pre-commit` running `claudemd-cli lint "$1"` gets no `$1`, scanned the empty string and let every commit through, so if your hook is a pre-commit hook, move the same line to `.git/hooks/commit-msg`, which git hands the message file. `claudemd-cli audit a.jsonl b.jsonl` now exits 2 instead of scanning the first file and ignoring the rest, so a CI step that passes a glob runs the command once per file and stops at the first file with hits: `for f in logs/*.jsonl; do claudemd-cli audit "$f" || exit; done` (without `|| exit`, a loop exits with the last file's status, unless the shell runs with `-e`). To keep the old exit codes, pin the CLI to 0.110.0 (`npm i -D claudemd-cli@0.110.0`). To take the plugin back to 0.110.0, follow `docs/ROLLBACK.md` ("Local machine needs the previous version back"): its installer refuses a downgrade unless `CLAUDEMD_ALLOW_DOWNGRADE=1` is set.

**Why.** Five read-only agents installed, used and uninstalled the plugin in sandbox HOMEs the way a user would, on 0.110.0 plus one offline-eval fix. They found two P0s (a personal `CLAUDE.md` backup that six installs could push out of existence; `clean-residue --apply` deleting through a symlink) and thirteen P1s. Each fix below was reproduced first with a test that failed; two blind reviews of the fixes found 8 Medium and 17 Low issues in them, and the Mediums are repaired here. Working record: `tasks/e2e-user-paths-2026-10-16.md` (local).

**What changes for you.**
- **Restoring your own `CLAUDE.md`** works after a default uninstall: the advice in install's warning, the SessionStart banner and README is now `/claudemd-uninstall restore` followed by `/plugin uninstall claudemd@claudemd`. The old advice, `CLAUDEMD_SPEC_ACTION=restore /claudemd-uninstall`, does not start with `/`, so it was not a slash command, and a restore while the plugin stayed installed was undone at the next session start. The uninstall command now takes a `restore` argument. A restore no longer overwrites a home `CLAUDE.md` that is not the spec and differs from the backup (your edits after an earlier restore), and lists it in a new `kept` field.
- **Deleting the spec without a manifest** (after a default uninstall, or with a corrupt manifest) removes only the spec files whose first line is the `# AI-CODING-SPEC` heading and only the `spec-modules/` files claudemd generated (their `# AI-CODING-SPEC … — module: <name>` heading), and lists the rest in `kept`. Before, it either deleted a personal `CLAUDE.md` or did nothing while answering as if done.
- **Install** decides per file whether a home `CLAUDE-extended.md`, `CLAUDE-changelog.md` or `OPERATOR.md` is claudemd's (it carries the spec heading or equals the shipped bytes; a UTF-8 byte-order mark is skipped) or yours (backed up where restore finds it). It no longer writes a new backup of byte-identical files on every install while `CLAUDE.md` is missing, which is how the sixth such install pruned the backup holding your notes.
- **SessionStart banners.** A failed first install says "install failed", not "upgrade failed", and shows install's own reason (for example invalid JSON in `settings.json`) to you only: the model is told a cause was shown, because the parser's message can quote part of `settings.json`. With a reason it drops the `/claudemd-refresh` advice, which cannot fix that file. The session-summary line says "last 24 h, all sessions" when that is the window it counted, where it said "since last turn".
- **`/claudemd-toggle`** adds a `note` when the hook is one of the opt-in ones (`EVIDENCE_GATE`, `REWORK_BREAKER`, …) and its opt-in variable is not set to a value that turns it on: clearing the kill switch alone leaves such a hook inactive, and the old output said "enabled".
- **`/claudemd-sparkline`** counts the hooks' own `*-advisory` events and `reply-language-restate` (230 `rework-advisory` rows in 30 days on the maintainer's machine were missing), takes each bucket's rate over the days the log actually covers, and, when no older bucket is covered for at least half its span (at most a week), prints no arrow except `↘ (silenced)` for a rule that fired before and not since. On a log shorter than the oldest window every rule read ↗ before, falling ones included.
- **`/claudemd-rules`**: rule rows gain `hits.fired`, and the demote lists read it instead of `hits.total`. A check that found nothing (`pass`, `pending-no-baseline`, an npx allowed by a lockfile) and a banner repeating a checkpoint are not firings; a push let through because the commit carries `known-red baseline:`, and an `rm -rf` on a var the agent guarded or made with `mktemp`, are. Before, `§7-ship-baseline` and `§11-session-exit` could not become demote candidates while pushes or banners continued. Today's candidates on the maintainer's log are unchanged.
- **`/claudemd-clean-residue`** does not descend a `claude-<uid>` entry of `~/.claude/tmp` that is a symlink, and does not scan `~/.claude/tmp` at all when the directory itself is a symlink (`claudeTmp.skipped: "symlink"`, with a line on stderr saying why). Every stale entry there is a candidate whatever its name, so `--apply` deleted old files in the link's target, which need not be Claude Code's.
- **Memory gates** (`memory-read-check`, `memory-prompt-hint`, and `banned-vocab`'s prose scan) find `MEMORY.md` and the transcript under the directory the session started in (`CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR`), not the current `cd`. After a `cd` into a subdirectory they used to find neither and let everything through. Replaying the real commit, push and publish calls made from a subdirectory on the maintainer's machine (19 with the author's filter, 127 with the pre-tag reviewer's): 2 now denied in both, each as it would be from the project root, 0 newly allowed. A session started in a subdirectory or a linked worktree is still not covered (Claude Code keys `memory/` by the git root there).
- **`/claudemd-doctor`** names `origin`, `upstream`, `master`, `remote`, `develop`, `staging` and `production` as memory tags too generic to keep: `memory-read-check` matches tags against a push command's arguments, and a memory tagged `origin` denied `git push origin master` in two real sessions.
- **`evidence-gate`** (opt-in): its Done-claim pattern read `\n` as the letter n, so a sentence such as "…the CHANGELOG section Main Changes: or Fixes?" counted as a report label. Over 13,665 real assistant messages the old and new patterns match the same 349.
- **`claudemd-cli audit`** skips a `null` content block and counts a non-array content in its integrity warning, where it crashed with a stack trace and exit 1 (its "hits found" code).
- **§8 (`pre-bash-safety`)**: verdicts unchanged. `npx <pkg>@latest` / `@next` / `@beta` / `@alpha`, and a range that starts with a digit (`@1`, `@1.x`), stay allowed as pinned, and are now recorded as accepted residual family F17 in `docs/S8-RESIDUALS.md`: 0 of the 4,696 package-runner calls in the maintainer's transcripts used a dist-tag or a range, and the deny's own remedy would pin the version the tag resolves to today, running the same code.
- **Offline A/B harness** (maintainers): runs no longer inherit claudemd's hook switches from the parent environment; an arm sets them with `--env=NAME=value`.

**Tests.**
- Every fix above came with a test that failed before it; each commit message names the test and the failure it showed.
- Run as a user in sandbox HOMEs on the release tree: 13 of 13 checks of the first-round fixes, 16 of 16 of the review repairs, and 5 of 5 upgrade paths in which the released 0.110.0 writes the state and this version reads and acts on it (a 0.110.0 personal backup restores; reinstalling over 0.110.0's spec adds no backup; a confirmed delete after the upgrade removes the spec; a hand-written `OPERATOR.md` and a personal `CLAUDE.md` restore after installs by both versions; a 0.110.0 failure sentinel without a reason reads correctly).
- The downgrade in the upgrade notes, in a sandbox HOME with this tree installed as 0.111.0: 0.110.0's installer exits 1 with "refusing downgrade" without the variable and exits 0 with it, leaving the 0.110.0 manifest. `claudemd-cli` on 0.110.0 → this tree: `lint ""` exit 0 → 2, `audit a.jsonl b.jsonl` exit 0 → 2.
- On b744fd3 (the code before the review repairs; 49d6286 adds the version and this entry): `npm run check` exit 0 with a built bash 3.2.57 (node 1,523 of 1,524 passed, 1 skipped because `SHFMT` is not set; bash 2,364 of 2,364; all 44 hook suites also executed under bash 3.2; shellcheck clean on 94 files); `npm run smoke` 2/2. On 49d6286: the same, exit 0, with SIGPIPE ignored; CI 7 of 7 jobs green (run 38983895325).

**Pre-tag review.** Round 1, two reviewers in parallel on b04c288..49d6286: one checked this entry and every other place its subjects are described, one the twelve commits of the last three converge rounds, which no review had seen. 0 Critical, 0 High; 4 Medium and 19 Low across the two reports, three of them the same defect found twice. The Mediums: a delete without a manifest also removed a personal `CLAUDE.md` whose `# AI-CODING-SPEC` line was not its first (the test read the first 256 characters), and every file in `spec-modules/` whatever it was (found by both); and the loop this entry first recommended for CI exited with the last file's status. Repaired: the first-line and module-heading tests above (df30fe6), the loop, the `known-red`-style rows for guarded `rm -rf` (d70f077), F17's ranges (5fea188), a trailing `/` getting past the symlink skip and a timing gap in a hook test (892748d), the hook-less install refusal now naming its fix (fc920d6), and stale text in README, `docs/`, command docs, the CLI header and three comments (000c027 and this entry).
